Also ran several unrelated self-contained example programs (`pattern_test.cto`, `result_test.cto`, `generic_test.cto`) end-to-end to confirm the much larger set of now-concrete stdlib return types (previously silently `Ty::Error` for anything outside the old two short lists) doesn't regress anything — no new failures; two pre-existing, unrelated example-file issues noticed in passing (`math.cto` has no `main` fn, `named_params.cto` uses old brace-body function syntax the parser no longer accepts) are file rot, not caused by this change. | `crates/hir`, `crates/stdlib` |
| 124 | Property-test generation for `List<T>`, named records, and named sum types | Shipped generation, shrinking, and end-to-end value construction for all three — not just generation as originally scoped, and not split further, since investigating actually resolved the item's own open question. **Record/sum-type shape resolution** (the piece item 86 flagged as "not investigated," needing either a typeck pass or reusing one from elsewhere): turned out to need neither — `crates/testrunner/src/harness.rs`'s `build_harness` already receives the whole parsed `Module` a property was declared in, so a new `gen::TypeDecls` just reads `type X = { ... }`/`type X = | A(...) | B(...)` declarations straight off that same AST (mirroring how `crates/hir/src/lower.rs`'s `Cx` builds its own `record_field_names`/`variant_field_types` the same way), resolved recursively via a new `gen::resolve_gen_type` with a cycle guard (a self-referential type like `type Tree = \| Leaf \| Node(Tree, Tree)` is reported as unsupported rather than recursing forever — bounded-depth generation for a genuinely recursive type is a different, harder problem not attempted here). **`List<T>`**: length-prefixed argv encoding (`gen::GenValue::to_args` now returns `Vec<String>` instead of one string, recursively — a list is its length followed by each element's own encoding; a record is the flat concatenation of its fields; a sum type is its variant tag followed by that variant's fields) decoded on the C side via a runtime cursor (`argv_i`) instead of the old fixed `argv[2+i]` indices, since a list's width isn't known until its length prefix is read. **Found and fixed a real bug while verifying end-to-end, not just via unit tests**: the first cut decoded every value as an inline `argv[argv_i++]` expression; that's safe alone, but a two-field record parameter's compound literal (`(Point){ .x = decode1, .y = decode2 }`) puts two such expressions as unsequenced sibling operands — confirmed via a real `-Wunsequenced` warning from the C compiler on an actual generated file, not theorized. Fixed by having every decode (scalars included) materialize through its own statement into a named temporary first, so evaluation order is forced to match emission order regardless of how many sibling params/fields reference decoded values. **Scope boundary, confirmed by direct investigation rather than assumed**: a `List<T>` can never have a record/sum type as its *direct* element — list elements are boxed into a pointer-sized `void*` slot via the same convention `crates/codegen/src/emit_mir.rs`'s `box_value` uses everywhere else (bit-cast for `Float`, `(void*)(intptr_t)(v)` for everything else already pointer-sized), and a real multi-field struct passed by value can't be cast through `intptr_t` at all — confirmed this is a genuine, pre-existing gap in the compiler itself (not introduced or fixed here), so `List<Point>` is reported as the same clear unsupported-parameter-type error as before rather than emitting C that doesn't compile. A record/sum type used as a *field* (not a list element) is unaffected, since struct fields are stored by value with no boxing — nesting there (a record field that's itself a named record/sum, or a `List<T>` of scalars) works. Anonymous inline record annotations (`{ x: Int }` without a `type` declaration) are also out of scope — the compiler itself represents those as `void*` with no real struct ever emitted, an unrelated pre-existing gap. Shrinking (`gen::shrink_step`) gained `List`/`Record`/`Sum` cases offering *both* "drop part of the structure" (empty, halved, drop-first, drop-last) *and* "shrink a surviving part" candidates in the same pass, per the item's own requirement — the existing greedy sweep (unchanged) converges on both minimal length and minimal elements across repeated sweeps, not just one or the other. 25 new `crates/testrunner` tests (type resolution incl. the cycle guard and the list-of-struct rejection, generation shape, `to_args` encoding, `min_argv_width`, and — proving shrinking actually does both things, not just claims to — a list shrinking to exactly 3 elements for a length threshold and to a single element at exactly a value threshold). Full workspace build and test suite clean (0 failures). Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo-test.exe`: a `List<Int>` property, a named-record (`Point`) property, and a named-sum (`Shape`, exercising both a payload variant and a unit variant) property all pass across 100 real generated cases each; a deliberately false `List<Int>`-length property fails and shrinks to the exact minimal counterexample `[0, 0, 0, 0]` (proving both length- and element-shrinking engaged, not just one), and the same seed reproduces the identical failure on a second run. | `crates/testrunner`, `crates/codegen` |
| 90 | `certo.toml` real structured TOML parsing, plus `[features]`/`[targets.production]` schema | Replaced the four separate ad-hoc `.lines()`/`strip_prefix` scanners in `crates/cli/src/main.rs` (`cmd_build`'s `entry` lookup, its `type`/`output` lookup, and `read_schema_sync_flag`) with one real parser: new `crates/cli/src/certo_toml.rs`, `serde`-`Deserialize`d structs (`deny_unknown_fields` on every section, so a typo'd key is now a clear parse error naming the file/line/column instead of being silently ignored) covering the *full* schema from `docs/Certo_Language_Specification.md` §11.4 — `[project]`, `[build]`, `[database]`, `[server]`, `[dependencies]`/`[dev-dependencies]`, `[features]`, `[targets.<name>]` — added `toml = "0.8"` as a direct `crates/cli` dependency (piggybacking on the `serde` derive feature already present for `serde_yaml`). Only the fields with a real, already-existing consumer are wired to behavior; the rest (`database.migrations`/`seeds`, `server.*`, `dependencies`/`dev-dependencies`) are parsed and schema-validated but deliberately inert — no `certo add`/package manager or `certo run --port` exists yet to consume them (`#[allow(dead_code)]` at the module level, not per-field, with a doc comment explaining why, rather than pretending they do something). **Found and fixed a real, undocumented bug in the process**: `schema-sync` was previously read from *anywhere* in the file (section-blind line matching) despite a doc comment claiming `[database]` — the spec's own schema puts it under `[features]`, which is what's now actually enforced. **New `certo build --release` flag**, real (not cosmetic): looks up `[targets.production]` and applies `optimize` (`-O2` vs `-O0`, a real compiler flag difference, confirmed via `-v` showing the actual invocation), `strip-debug` (`-s`, gated to non-Windows — Certo never emits `/DEBUG` on Windows, so there's no embedded symbol table there to strip in the first place, and `-s` is a GNU-driver flag this target's MSVC-mode clang invocation just warns is unused; confirmed via a real build showing the warning before the fix), and `schema` (overrides `DATABASE_URL` for that process only, with `${VAR}` expansion against the environment, matching the spec's own example of keeping a real connection string out of version control — verified end-to-end: a build with a bogus production host in `${VAR}` produced a real DNS-resolution error naming that exact host, proving the override reached the actual schema-sync connection attempt, not just a parsed-and-discarded value). **Found and fixed a second, more consequential pre-existing bug while wiring `--release`**: `cmd_build`'s (and `run_typeck`'s) `project_root` was computed as `input.parent()` — correct when the user passes an explicit file, but wrong the moment `input` was itself resolved from `[build] entry` (e.g. `src/main.cto`), whose parent is `src/`, not the project root where `certo.toml` actually lives; this silently broke `output`-directory detection and `schema-sync` for the single most common project layout (`certo new`'s own scaffold, and every bare `certo build`/`certo` with no args). Fixed by preferring `cwd` as `project_root` whenever `cwd` itself has a `certo.toml`, falling back to `input.parent()` only when it doesn't (preserving the original fallback for a file built from outside its own project); threaded the corrected root through a new `run_typeck_opts_with_root`, leaving `run_typeck`/`run_typeck_opts`'s existing filename-derived behavior untouched for `cmd_check`/`cmd_bench` (both always take an explicit file path, where that computation was already correct). `cmd_new`'s generated `certo.toml` template now includes commented `[features]`/`[targets.production]` example sections (with `schema-sync` accurately described as the only currently-enforced feature flag) instead of omitting them entirely. 7 new `crates/cli` unit tests (full-spec-example parse, minimal-manifest parse, missing-file-is-not-an-error, malformed-TOML error message, unknown-key rejection, `${VAR}` expansion including the unset-var-left-verbatim case). Full workspace build and test suite clean (0 failures, 0 warnings). Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`: a scaffolded project builds and runs unchanged with no `certo.toml` `[features]`/`[targets]` section; `[features] schema-sync = true` with no `DATABASE_URL` set correctly errors naming the `[features]` section (not the old, wrong `[database]`); `--release` with `[targets.production]` (`optimize = false`, `strip-debug = true`, `schema = "${TEST_PROD_DB_VAR}"`) shows `-O0` and (on a non-Windows-style check) `-s` in the real compiler invocation, and the schema-sync connection attempt fails against the exact overridden host, not the dev default; a hand-corrupted `certo.toml` (`[project` with no closing bracket) produces a real `toml`-crate parse error with line/column instead of the old silent pass-through. | `crates/cli` |
| 132 | Parameterized `Decimal(p,s)` | Design decided 2026-08-08 by resolving the two concerns the original scope note raised, both of which turned out simpler than feared once the driving need (DB fidelity — `certo db pull`/`schema-sync` catching a `NUMERIC(19,4)` column declared as the wrong precision in code) was pinned down first. **(1) Grammar**: `Decimal(19, 4)` is a *Decimal-specific* parse rule in `crates/parser/src/parse_type.rs`'s `parse_named_type` (checked before the generic `<Args>`/name-lookup path, only when the path is the exact single segment `"Decimal"`), not a general const-generics mechanism — new `TypeExpr::DecimalParam { precision: u8, scale: u8 }` in `crates/ast`. **(2) Semantics**: `Ty::Decimal` became `Ty::Decimal(Option<(u8,u8)>)` (`None` = today's bare form) in `crates/typeck`; `unify.rs` gained exactly one new arm — a bare `Decimal` unifies with *any* `Decimal(p,s)`, two *different* parameterizations don't — so `Decimal.add`/`sub`/`mul`/`div`/etc.'s existing bare-`Decimal` signatures in `seed.rs` needed zero changes; a `Decimal(19,4)` value just flows into them like any other `Decimal`, verified end-to-end via a real compiled `certo.exe` program (`Decimal.add` on a `Decimal(19,4)` and a bare `Decimal` value produces the correct sum). Every other crate touching `TypeExpr`/`Ty::Decimal` exhaustively (found via compiler-driven fixup, not grep — Rust's exhaustiveness check enumerated every site precisely once the variant shapes changed): `crates/hir/src/lower.rs`'s *own independent* `type_expr_to_ty`-equivalent (`ast_ty_to_ty_with_params`, the exact class of duplicate-copy trap item 129 already hit — previously silently fell through to `Ty::Error` for any unhandled `TypeExpr` variant, now has a real `DecimalParam` arm), `crates/codegen` (`ty_to_c.rs`, `emit_mir.rs`, `emit_module.rs` — all map to the same `certo_decimal_t`, no runtime change), `crates/llvm/src/ty.rs`, `crates/ffi` (`ty.rs`, `header.rs`), `crates/fmt/src/fmt_type.rs`, `crates/lsp/src/analysis.rs`, `crates/traits/src/trait_db.rs`, `crates/dbschema/src/schema.rs`'s `te_to_str`, `crates/mir/src/lower.rs`, `crates/stdlib` (`seed.rs`'s ~19 `Decimal`-typed signatures, all normalized to `Ty::Decimal(None)` — genuinely no behavior change, confirmed by the full existing stdlib test suite passing unchanged). **DB-fidelity wiring** (the actual point): `crates/cli`'s `pg_type_to_certo` now takes `numeric_precision`/`numeric_scale` (all three `information_schema.columns` query sites — `db pull`, `db diff`, schema-sync's live introspection — updated to select and thread them through) and emits `Decimal(p, s)` only when Postgres actually reports both (so `money`, which carries none, stays bare `Decimal` — no fabricated fidelity); `certo db pull` now generates real `Decimal(19, 4)` field types instead of always-bare `Decimal`. **Found and fixed a real regression in this item's own DB-diff comparison, not a pre-existing bug**: making `db pull`/`pg_type_to_certo` sometimes emit `"Decimal(19, 4)"` instead of always `"Decimal"` broke the *existing* string-equality comparisons in three separate places (`check_migrations::types_compat`, `check_schema_sync`'s inline check, and `crates/cli`'s `types_match` for `certo db diff`) — a bare `Decimal` field declaration would suddenly report as drift against any live column with real precision/scale, a false-positive regression for every existing bare-Decimal `DbRow` field the moment this shipped. Fixed by consolidating into one shared `certo_dbschema::decimal_bare_vs_param` (three near-duplicate comparison functions needing the identical fix is exactly the kind of drift trap items 101/129/131 already hit) and or'ing it into all three comparison sites; verified both directions end-to-end against a real local PostgreSQL instance — a bare `Decimal` field against a live `NUMERIC(19,4)` column now correctly reports "schema in sync" (no false positive), while `Decimal(10, 2)` against the same live `NUMERIC(19,4)` column still correctly reports a real `TYPE MISMATCH` via the *existing* drift category (no new category needed — string-formatting both sides as `"Decimal(p, s)"` consistently was enough for the current comparison machinery to catch it for free). **Found and fixed three more real, pre-existing, unrelated bugs while verifying end-to-end against a real database — none introduced by this item, all newly confirmed by it, and all directly blocking verification of this item's own feature**: (1) `certo db pull`'s generated `type X { ... }` was missing the required `=` (`type X = { ... }`) — every file `certo db pull` has ever generated has been invalid, unparseable Certo; the doc example in `docs/CLI-TOOLCHAIN.md` §13 had the identical bug, also fixed. (2) The generated `fromRow` decoder used `parseInt` to decode a `Decimal` column's text value (e.g. `"199.99"`), silently truncating/failing on any real fractional value — fixed to use the already-existing `parseDecimal` instead. (3) `text_to_certo_expr`/`certo_to_text_expr`/`null_default` (the generator's per-type conversion-expression helpers) matched on the *exact* type string `"Decimal"`, so once `pg_type_to_certo` started sometimes returning `"Decimal(19, 4)"`, these silently stopped recognizing Decimal columns at all and generated code that treated the value as raw Text — fixed by matching on the type name with any `(...)` parameter suffix stripped first. **Explicitly not fixed here, spun off as its own task since it's large, general (affects every column type, not just Decimal), and unrelated to precision/scale**: `certo db pull`'s generated code for *any* nullable column (of any type) uses a bare `null` literal that doesn't exist in Certo at all, and its `Insert` codegen assumes every nullable field is `Text?` — confirmed via direct reproduction that `certo db pull` has likely never generated valid code for any schema with a nullable column, a pre-existing, high-severity, orthogonal gap. 6 new `crates/parser` tests (precision/scale parses, bare `Decimal` unaffected, `Decimal(p,s)?` optional-suffix composition, three malformed-input error cases), 5 new `crates/typeck` tests (parses and type-checks, bare↔parameterized unify both directions, different parameterizations are a real type error, arithmetic needs zero special-casing), 1 new `crates/fmt` round-trip test, 5 new `crates/dbschema` tests for `decimal_bare_vs_param`. Full workspace build and test suite clean (0 failures). Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe` against a real local PostgreSQL instance throughout, not just unit tests: a `Decimal(19, 4)` local variable compiled and ran correctly through `Decimal.add`/`Decimal.toText` printing the right arithmetic result; `certo db pull` against a real `NUMERIC(19,4)` column produced a file that itself passes `certo check` cleanly (previously impossible due to the `=` bug) with correctly-precise `Decimal(19, 4)` fields; `certo db diff` correctly distinguished a real precision mismatch from a false-positive bare-vs-parameterized non-mismatch. | `crates/ast`, `crates/parser`, `crates/typeck`, `crates/hir`, `crates/codegen`, `crates/llvm`, `crates/ffi`, `crates/fmt`, `crates/lsp`, `crates/traits`, `crates/mir`, `crates/stdlib`, `crates/dbschema`, `crates/cli` |
| 130 | Bug fixed — `certo_http_serve` (`Stdlib.Http`'s HTTP server) unconditionally `certo_panic`d on non-Windows, and `certo_http_request_header` used an MSVC-only function that would have failed to compile on POSIX at all | Found while investigating item 88 (`live val`) — `Http.serve`, the only HTTP server primitive in the language (used by `certo-ui`'s generated servers and any hand-written `.cto` server), was Windows-only: the POSIX branch was a bare stub, `(void)port; (void)handler; certo_panic(...)`. Separately, `certo_http_request_header` called `_stricmp` (MSVC/Windows-specific) from a function that isn't even guarded by `#ifdef _WIN32` — meaning any POSIX build referencing that function (part of the ordinary, always-compiled `HttpRequest` accessor set) would have failed to compile outright, a second, independent gap in the same file. Fixed both: added `certo_stricmp` (`#define`d to `_stricmp` on Windows, `strcasecmp` from `<strings.h>` on POSIX) and used it in both call sites. For the server itself, refactored `crates/stdlib/src/http.rs` so the request-parsing/response-writing pipeline (`http_srv_strdup`, `http_status_text`, `http_srv_read_request`, `http_srv_read_body`, `http_srv_parse`, `http_srv_send`) is written **once** and shared by both platforms via a `certo_socket_t` typedef (`SOCKET` on Windows, plain `int` fd on POSIX) — `recv`/`send` are BSD-sockets API with identical signatures on Winsock and POSIX, so only the socket handle's type, its "invalid" sentinel, and how it's closed actually differ, rather than duplicating ~150 lines of near-identical parsing logic per platform the way the file's own client-side WinHTTP-vs-POSIX-stub split does. Only `certo_http_serve` itself (socket creation/`bind`/`listen`/accept-loop) has two separate bodies, since that's the one part that's genuinely platform-specific (`WSAStartup`/`SOCKET`/`INVALID_SOCKET` vs plain BSD `socket()`/`int`/`-1`) — the new POSIX body is a plain blocking `accept()`/`read()`/`write()`/`close()` loop with identical single-connection-at-a-time semantics to the existing Windows implementation, deliberately not adding threading/concurrency/async (out of scope — that's the real, separate gap item 88's point (2) still flags for real push/streaming support). Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe` on Windows (the only platform available in this environment): built and ran `examples/http_server.cto` (a real `Http.serve`-based JSON API) before and after the refactor, hit all three routes with real HTTP requests (`GET /`, `GET /health`, `POST /echo` with a body and a `Content-Type` header — exercising the now-shared `certo_stricmp`-based header lookup) — identical correct responses both times, confirming the refactor didn't regress the working Windows path. The POSIX path itself could not be executed in this sandbox (Windows-only environment, no Linux/macOS toolchain available) — verified instead by careful manual review against standard BSD-sockets usage and by confirming every POSIX-only symbol used (`socklen_t`, `SOMAXCONN`, `INADDR_ANY`, `AF_INET`/`SOCK_STREAM`/`IPPROTO_TCP`, `htons`, `close`, `strcasecmp`) is covered by the headers now included (`<unistd.h>`, `<sys/socket.h>`, `<netinet/in.h>`, `<arpa/inet.h>`, `<strings.h>`) — a real POSIX build should be smoke-tested when next available, but the parsing/response logic it calls into is the exact same code already verified working on Windows, not a separate untested implementation. Full workspace build and test suite clean (0 failures) on Windows. | `crates/stdlib` |
| 131 | `certo db migrate`/`rollback` now actually execute against the database, plus a post-migrate staleness warning for `db/schema.cto` | Grew out of item 72b's design discussion (see that item's note) — decided to strengthen the two already-shipped schema-tracking models (`certo db pull`, `[features] schema-sync`) instead of building a fully-live DB-owns-everything model, starting with closing the gap right after a migration runs. **Found a much bigger, unrelated bug before any of that could be meaningful**: `certo db migrate up`/`down` never executed SQL against a real database at all — `crates/migrate/src/runner.rs`'s `run_steps` only ever generated SQL strings and unconditionally marked `.certo_migrations` state as "applied," regardless of whether the user actually ran that SQL anywhere. The docs already claimed otherwise (`--dry-run`: "print the SQL **without executing** it," implying the normal path executes) — confirmed via grep that zero `psql`/connection code existed anywhere in the migrate path (every other DB-touching command — `schema-sync`, `db diff`, `db pull` — does have one). Fixed first, since the staleness check is meaningless if migrating never touches the DB: split `run_steps` into `plan_sql` (pure SQL generation, used for `--dry-run`, no state/DB touched) and `commit_steps` (persists applied/rolled-back state, called *only* after real execution succeeds) — `crates/migrate` itself still does zero process-spawning, matching its existing pure-planning/state-logic role; the actual `psql` execution lives in `crates/cli`'s `run_migration_sql`, alongside the other three DB-shelling call sites. Real execution runs with `--single-transaction --set ON_ERROR_STOP=1`, so a failing statement rolls back the *entire* batch (including any earlier-in-the-same-run successful statements) and makes `psql`'s exit code correctly reflect failure — verified end-to-end against a real local PostgreSQL instance: a two-migration batch where the second migration's `rawSql` referenced a nonexistent table correctly rolled back the *first* migration's already-executed `CREATE TABLE` too (confirmed the table did not exist afterward), and `.certo_migrations` correctly still showed both as pending (safe to retry) rather than the old code's would-be "applied anyway." **Then added the staleness warning**: extracted the DB-connect-and-diff portion of `certo db diff` (previously ~150 lines inline in `cmd_db_diff`) into a reusable `diff_schema_against_live(expected, schema_name)`, preserving `certo db diff`'s own exact existing output/exit behavior unchanged, and called it from a new `warn_if_schema_stale` after a successful non-dry-run `migrate up`/`down` — only if `db/schema.cto` exists (so projects that don't use `certo db pull` are never forced into a DB round-trip they didn't ask for), printing a warning (not failing the command) with the exact `certo db pull` fix. **Found and fixed a second, more consequential pre-existing bug while building this**: `certo db diff`'s table/column matching never bridged `snake_case` DB identifiers to `PascalCase`/`camelCase` Certo names at all — direct string equality between a live table `widgets` and a declared `type Widgets` always failed as "MISSING TABLE Widgets" / "EXTRA TABLE widgets," even though they're the exact same table under the established convention (`certo db pull` generates `PascalCase`/`camelCase`, and — critically — `check_schema_sync.rs`'s *other*, automatic schema-sync path already correctly implements this exact bridging via its own `snake_to_pascal`/`snake_to_camel`). This wasn't a theoretical gap: it reproduced immediately against a real database in this session's own verification, and would have made the just-built staleness warning fire false positives on every real project using `certo db pull`'s own generated files — the single most common case. Fixed by exporting `snake_to_pascal`/`snake_to_camel` from `certo_dbschema` (were `pub(crate)`, now `pub`) and using them in the extracted diff function exactly as `check_schema_sync` already does, while keeping raw (unbridged) DB names in "EXTRA TABLE"/"EXTRA COLUMN" messages, since those describe live DB objects, not declared types. Verified end-to-end against a real local PostgreSQL instance, not just unit tests: `certo db diff` against a correctly-named `type Widgets = { id, name }` now reports "schema in sync" instead of a false MISSING/EXTRA TABLE pair; adding an undeclared field to the type correctly reports a real `MISSING COLUMN`, nothing else. 7 new `crates/migrate` tests (`plan_sql` produces real DDL for both up/down, confirms it has zero state side effects — a regression guard against the old unconditional-mark-applied behavior — and `commit_steps` persists both applied and rolled-back state correctly). Full workspace build and test suite clean (0 failures). | `crates/migrate`, `crates/cli`, `crates/dbschema` |
| 134 | Struct-typed values can now go through `List<T>`/`Tuple`, and through a *named* function used as a generic callback (`dbQueryTyped`, `Query.list`, etc.) — both fixed, including the `certo db pull` `*FindById` segfault items 133/134 originally found | Implemented the design sketch item 134 previously wrote up (see git history — that writeup is superseded by this one). Two independent, compounding gaps, both required to make the motivating case (`certo db pull`'s generated `*FindById`) actually work end-to-end. **(1) `List<T>`/`Tuple` storage for struct `T`**: added `Ty::needs_heap_box()` (`crates/typeck/src/ty.rs`) — true for `Decimal`/`Uuid` and any non-opaque `Ty::Named` (a user record/sum type), false otherwise — shared by both `crates/mir` and `crates/codegen` (previously each had its own ad-hoc, drifting notion of "does this fit a pointer-sized slot"). `box_value`/`unbox_value` (`crates/codegen/src/emit_mir.rs`) now real-heap-allocate (`malloc(sizeof(T)); *ptr = value;`, via a GNU statement-expression since `box_value` must stay a single composable expression usable as a call argument, and clang/gcc are already a hard requirement — item 104) whenever `needs_heap_box()` is true, instead of unconditionally bit-casting/pointer-casting; `certo_list_push`'s second argument (previously a raw `(void*)(expr)` cast) goes through the same `box_value` now. **(2) Named-function-reference callback ABI** (the actual cause of the segfault, not just a compile error): extended `BOXED_ABI_CALLEES` (`crates/mir/src/lower.rs`) with `dbQueryTyped`/`Query.list`/`Query.first`/`Query.groupedList`, and added `lower_named_fn_boxed` alongside the existing `lower_lambda_boxed` — synthesizes a small wrapper function (`__fnref_<caller>_<n>_boxed`) that unboxes each param per the real function's declared signature, calls the real named function, and heap-boxes the result, so the C-runtime-visible function pointer is uniformly `void*(*)(void*)` regardless of the wrapped function's real (possibly struct-returning) signature. **Getting the wrapper to actually fire took two more layers of fix, found only by tracing why the wrapper still wasn't being emitted / was emitted but returned garbage**: (2a) a bare reference to a top-level `fn` (`widgetsFromRow` used as a value, not called) lowered to `Ty::Error` in HIR's `Expr::Path` (`crates/hir/src/lower.rs`) — the `cx.global_types` table it read from is only ever populated for one narrow val-destructuring case, never for ordinary `fn` declarations — so `lower_named_fn_boxed`'s guard (which needs the real `Ty::Fn{params, ret}`) never matched and the callback silently passed through unwrapped, exactly reproducing the original struct-return-through-`void*(*)(void*)` undefined behavior. Fixed by reconstructing the real `Ty::Fn` from `cx.fn_params`/`cx.fn_ret_types` (populated up front for every top-level `fn`, unlike `global_types`) when `global_types` has no entry. (2b) Even with the wrapper now firing, `widgetsFindById` returned corrupted data (empty `name`, garbage `price`, `qty=0`) instead of segfaulting — traced to `dbQueryTyped`/`Query.first`/etc. being registered in `crates/stdlib/src/seed.rs` as `Forall` generics, whose return type (`List<Var(T)>`/`Option<Var(T)>`, i.e. `ret.has_vars()`) is filtered out of HIR's mechanical `stdlib_ret_types()` table — so the call expression's own `.ty` stayed `Ty::Error`, which cascaded into a `val rows = dbQueryTyped(...)` binding and then `List.first(rows)` *also* failing to recover `Ty::Option(Widgets)` — silently skipping the `UnwrapOptStructBox` fixup (item 134's original double-boxing fix for `List.first`/etc., which still needed the call's real element type to know to trigger). Fixed by adding `dbQueryTyped`/`Query.list`/`Query.groupedList`/`Query.first` cases to `generic_container_ret` (`crates/hir/src/lower.rs`), deriving `T` from the mapper argument's own now-correctly-resolved `Ty::Fn{ret, ..}` (from fix 2a) rather than from `stdlib_ret_types()`. **Also fixed while verifying, a separate pre-existing bug**: `crates/codegen/src/emit_module.rs`'s `ast_ty_to_c_str` (the AST-level struct-field type mapper, a *second*, independent copy of `ty_to_c.rs`'s resolved-`Ty` mapper that can drift out of sync) was missing the `DateTime`/`Date`/`Duration`/`JsonValue` opaque-type arms `ty_to_c.rs` already had (items 75/128) — any struct field of one of those types (e.g. `certo db pull`'s generated `createdAt: DateTime`) emitted the literal undeclared C identifier `DateTime`/etc. instead of `CertoDateTime`, a real compile error. Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe` against a real local PostgreSQL instance (not unit tests alone): `widgetsInsert`/`widgetsFindById`/`widgetsFindAll` — the exact `certo db pull`-generated shape from item 133's own repro, a 6-field `Widgets` record including a `Decimal(19,4)` and a `DateTime` field — all run correctly with no segfault and no data corruption (`findById: name=Widget A price=19.9900 qty=5`, exact decimal precision preserved; `findAll count: 3` with correct first-row data). Re-verified the item 134 investigation's own isolated repros (struct/empty-struct/scalar/empty-scalar `List.first`/`.getOrPanic`) still pass. 3 new `crates/hir` unit tests: a bare named-function reference resolves to its real `Ty::Fn`; `dbQueryTyped(...)`'s call type resolves to `List<T>` from the mapper's return type; `Query.first(...)`'s resolves to `Option<T>`. Full workspace build and test suite clean (0 failures). | `crates/typeck`, `crates/mir`, `crates/hir`, `crates/codegen`, `crates/llvm` |
| 136 | Bug fixed — a lambda referencing a variable from an enclosing scope silently read garbage/uninitialized memory (non-deterministic across runs), for *any* lambda anywhere in the language; now rejected with a clear compile error instead | Found while investigating item 122's `every()` design (a hand-written `List.map(xs, (x) => x + outer)` was used to test a related idea) — the result was a different wrong number on every single run of the identical binary (confirmed 43, 24, 15, 56, 4 across five runs), immediately ruling out a stable logic bug in favor of real undefined behavior. Root cause: Certo's lambda lowering has never actually implemented closure capture. Both lambda-lowering paths in `crates/mir/src/lower.rs` (the plain `HirExprKind::Lambda` arm and `lower_lambda_boxed`) build the lambda body in a **completely fresh, isolated `Builder`** with an empty `local_map`. When the body references a local bound in the *enclosing* function, `Builder::get_local`'s fallback (`self.local_map.get(&hir).unwrap_or(&hir)`) silently reinterprets the outer function's `LocalId` as if it were a valid index into the *lambda's own*, much smaller `locals` vec — an out-of-bounds/wrong-slot read that C compiles as reading whatever garbage happens to occupy that stack slot. This affects every lambda-accepting call, not just `List.map` — including `db.transaction { ... }`'s body, which HIR wraps in an implicit zero-param lambda (confirmed by reading the code; `Expr::Transaction` itself turned out to be dead AST with no parser support, so not a live, reachable bug today, but the same underlying gap). **Considered and explicitly declined real closure support this session**: doing it correctly requires an explicit captured-environment pointer threaded through *every* indirect call site (`Ty::Fn`'s C representation would need to become a `{fn_ptr, env_ptr}` pair, `emit_callee`'s item-108 casting mechanism would need updating) **and** every higher-order C runtime function that invokes a callback — `certo_list_map`/`filter`/`find`/`any`/`all`/`groupBy`, `certo_db_query_typed`, `certo_query_list`/`first`/`groupedList` in `crates/stdlib/src/collections.rs`/`db.rs`/`dbquery.rs` — all of which would need a new env parameter threaded through and passed to the callback on every invocation. A cross-cutting, multi-hour change spanning typeck/hir/mir/codegen and the C runtime, not a contained fix; a partial version covering only some call paths would silently reintroduce the exact same corruption class this item exists to close. **Fixed instead**: reject any lambda body (or `db.transaction { }` body) that references an enclosing-scope local, at HIR lowering time, with a clear, correctly-spanned compile error instead of silently miscompiling. New `check_no_lambda_capture`/`collect_free_locals` in `crates/hir/src/lower.rs`, wired into all three sites that construct `HirExprKind::Lambda` (`Expr::Lambda`, `lower_lambda_with_param_hint` for `List.map`/`.groupBy`'s callback-hint path, and the `Expr::Transaction` thunk). Uses a `LocalId` threshold rather than a full scope walk: `Cx::next_local` only ever increments, so any `Local(id)` referenced inside the lambda body with `id < threshold` (the counter's value right before the lambda's own scope was pushed) must be a capture from an enclosing scope; a nested lambda is left unvisited by the outer check (`HirExprKind::Lambda => {}` in the collector) since it gets its own independent check at its own construction site. **Found and fixed a second, unrelated pre-existing gap along the way**: HIR lowering errors (`LowerError`, `certo_hir::lower_module`'s `Err` case) had *no diagnostic surfacing at all* — codegen's own internal call to `lower_module` (`crates/codegen/src/emit_module.rs`) silently wrote `/* HIR lowering errors: N */` into the generated C and returned, with nothing shown to the user; this new check would have been reported nowhere. Fixed by calling `lower_module` a second time in `crates/cli/src/main.rs`'s `run_typeck_inner` (alongside the existing typeck/traits/effects/dbschema checks, right after effects), with a new `hir_error_to_diagnostic` converter (new codes `E0600`/`E0601`) reported the same way as every other check (`render_all`, exit 1) — a general improvement benefiting any future `LowerError`, not just this one. Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`: the exact repro (`List.map(xs, (x) => x + outer)`) now fails cleanly at compile time with `error[E0601]` pointing at the lambda, instead of running and printing a different wrong number each time; a lambda referencing only its own parameter, and a lambda with an internal `let` binding (not a capture), both still compile and run correctly. 3 new `crates/hir` unit tests. Full workspace build and test suite clean (0 failures). | `crates/hir`, `crates/cli` |
| 137 | Bug fixed — `Process.exec`'s own return type, `ProcessResult`, was never registered in any of the codegen opaque-handle type-mapping tables, so *any* code using it — including the plain, inferred `val result = Process.exec(...)` shape, not just an explicit annotation — failed to compile with `error: use of undeclared identifier 'ProcessResult'` | Found while answering a user question about how `Process.exec` works, then confirmed by direct reproduction (not assumed) before fixing: both `val r: ProcessResult = Process.exec(...)` and the plain inferred `val r = Process.exec(...)` emitted the bare, undeclared C identifier `ProcessResult` for the local's declared type — the exact same failure class items 85/93/118/134 already fixed for `DateTime`/`Date`/`Duration`/`JsonValue`/`Timezone`, just never applied to `ProcessResult`, which had been present in `crates/stdlib/src/seed.rs`/`process.rs` since `Process.exec` was first implemented but was missing from `crates/codegen/src/ty_to_c.rs`, `crates/codegen/src/emit_module.rs`'s `ast_ty_to_c_str`, and `crates/typeck/src/ty.rs`'s `needs_heap_box` entirely — not merely mismapped, absent. Fixed by adding `"ProcessResult"` to all three, mapping to `CertoProcessResult*` (a real C pointer, matching `crates/stdlib/src/process.rs`'s own `CertoProcessResult* certo_process_exec(...)` — same shape as `JsonValue`'s existing `CertoJsonValue*` treatment, not the bare-value `Certo<Name>` treatment `DateTime`/`Date`/`Duration`/`Timezone` get). Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`: both the explicit-annotation and inferred-`val` repros now compile and correctly run an external command, print its captured stdout, and report exit code 0. 2 new `crates/codegen` tests asserting the generated C uses `CertoProcessResult*` and never the bare undeclared `ProcessResult` identifier, for both the inferred and annotated shapes. Full workspace build and test suite clean (0 failures). | `crates/codegen`, `crates/typeck` |
| 118 | `Timezone` type, timezone-aware `DateTime` (`Timezone(name)`, `.inTimezone(tz)`, `.formatTz(pattern, tz)`, `Date.todayIn(tz)`) — real IANA zones with correct DST, not fixed offsets | Split out of item 85 (`Duration` done — see Completed table), originally written up as a design sketch ("Windows has no builtin tzdata... needs vendoring or shelling out") — re-investigated and found to be more tractable than that note assumed, then implemented for real. **Windows research finding, confirmed by direct inspection, not assumed**: modern Windows (10 1903+) *does* bundle real IANA tzdata via ICU, contradicting the original note — `icu.dll`/`icuin.dll`/`icuuc.dll` are present in `System32`. Classic ICU4C's documented *versioned*-symbol convention (`ucal_open_70`) does **not** apply to this OS-bundled package: `llvm-readobj --coff-exports icuin.dll` shows `ucal_open`/`ucal_close`/`ucal_setMillis`/`ucal_get`/`ucal_getCanonicalTimeZoneID` exported **unversioned** (as forwarders to `icu.dll`), so no version-suffix probing is needed — `GetProcAddress` with the plain name works directly. Verified with a standalone C test program *before* wiring anything into the compiler: `Europe/London` correctly resolves to UTC+1 (BST) in July 2026 and UTC+0 (GMT) in January 2026. **Also found by testing, not assumed**: `ucal_open` itself does **not** fail on an unknown/bogus zone name (silently falls back to a default) — validation must go through `ucal_getCanonicalTimeZoneID` instead, confirmed to correctly distinguish real IANA names (`isSystemID=1`) from bogus ones (`status` error, `isSystemID=0`) via direct testing. **POSIX (Linux/macOS)**: `tzalloc`/`localtime_rz`/`tzfree` (a GNU/BSD extension present on both glibc and macOS/BSD libc) instead of `tzset`+the process-global `TZ` environment variable — deliberately, since mutating `TZ` isn't thread-safe against another thread's concurrent `localtime`/`gmtime` calls, and this codebase's `spawn`/`parallel` make concurrent use routine; `tzalloc` returning `NULL` doubles as the invalid-zone-name check. **Implementation**: `CertoTimezone` is just `certo_text_t` (the IANA name itself — already pointer-sized, no wrapper struct needed) in `crates/stdlib/src/datetime.rs`, appended to the existing `DATETIME_C` string (`#ifdef _WIN32`/`#else` split, Win32 ICU loaded lazily and thread-safely via `InitOnceExecuteOnce`). New stdlib surface: `Timezone(name: Text): Timezone?` (a plain global function, not a sum-type constructor — resolves as an ordinary call), `Timezone.name(tz): Text`, `DateTime.inTimezone(dt, tz): Text` (ISO 8601 with the zone's real numeric UTC-offset suffix, e.g. `+01:00`, not `Z`), `DateTime.formatTz(dt, fmt, tz): Text` (distinctly named rather than a `DateTime.format` overload, since Certo has no function overloading — same convention as `DateTime.diff` alongside `diffSeconds`/`diffDays` in item 85), `Date.todayIn(tz): Date`. `ty_to_c.rs`/`emit_module.rs`'s `ast_ty_to_c_str` (the item 85/93/134-established dual opaque-type-mapping fix point) and `typeck::Ty::needs_heap_box` all extended with `"Timezone"` alongside `DateTime`/`Date`/`Duration`. **Found and documented, not fixed, a real correctness gap while verifying end-to-end**: `DateTime.formatTz`'s `%Z`/`%z` `strftime` directives are unreliable and must not be used — confirmed via direct testing on a real Windows machine that formatting a `Europe/London` instant with `%Z` printed the *host machine's own* configured Windows timezone name ("AUS Eastern Standard Time"), completely unrelated to London, since `strftime` reads `%Z`/`%z` from `struct tm`'s own zone fields (or the process-wide C-library locale timezone), neither of which this function — or any portable technique — can hand an arbitrary caller-specified zone into; documented prominently in the function's own doc comment, pointing callers at `DateTime.inTimezone`'s numeric-offset output or `Timezone.name` instead. Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`: `Timezone("Europe/London")` → `Some`, `Timezone("Bogus/Nonexistent")` → `None`; a July 2026 instant correctly renders `...T13:00:00+01:00` (BST) and a January 2026 instant `...T12:00:00+00:00` (GMT) via `inTimezone`; `Timezone.name` round-trips the original IANA string; `Date.todayIn(Asia/Tokyo)` returns the correct Tokyo-local date. 6 new `crates/stdlib` tests (registration, each function's exact `Ty::Fn` shape, and that both the POSIX `tzalloc` and Windows `icuin.dll` code paths are actually present in the generated C — a regression guard against one accidentally landing behind the wrong `#ifdef`). Full workspace build and test suite clean (0 failures). | `crates/stdlib`, `crates/codegen`, `crates/typeck` |
| 120 | Box a concrete argument passed into a generic function's own bare-`T` parameter, at any call site (not just constructors) — the call-site argument half of the item 119→120 split | Split from item 119 after shipping it (generic records/sum-type variants at construction/field-access sites). The remaining gap, confirmed by direct testing before any fix: `Secret.wrap(d"19.99")`-style calls (`impl<T> Secret { fn wrap(v: T): Secret<T> = Secret(v) }`) were a hard C compile error for a struct-shaped `T` (`passing 'certo_decimal_t' to parameter of incompatible type 'void *'`), and *appeared* to work for `Int` only via undefined-behavior-adjacent pointer/integer bit-pattern reinterpretation — not a real fix, would not survive ASan or a stricter compiler. Added a new `fn_param_tys` table (function name → declared param types, a bare type-param param stored as `Ty::Var(0)`) — built in `crates/hir/src/lower.rs` alongside the existing `fn_ret_types` (same population sites: top-level `fn`s and `impl` methods, both pre-registration and the actual lowering pass), exposed via `HirModule`, threaded through `crates/mir`'s `lower_fn`/`Builder` exactly like `record_field_types`/`variant_field_types` (item 119's own precedent). At `HirExprKind::Call` lowering (`crates/mir/src/lower.rs`), any argument whose corresponding callee param is declared `Ty::Var(_)` — and whose own type is a *known concrete* type — gets heap-boxed via the existing `Rvalue::BoxSome` (item 119's mechanism, unconditionally correct for any `Ty::Var(0)`-declared field including real structs). **Getting a correct, non-double-boxing implementation required finding and fixing three pre-existing bugs, not just adding the new boxing path**: (1) item 119's *own* constructor-argument-boxing and record-field-construction-boxing checks always (re-)boxed a `Ty::Var(_)`-declared field's value, even when that value was *itself* already an opaque `Ty::Var(_)`-typed value (e.g. `v` referenced inside `wrap`'s own body, calling `Secret(v)`) — a real double-boxing bug (same failure class item 134 fixed for `List.first`), confirmed via a minimal repro (`fn wrapTwice(v: T): Box<T> = Box.wrap(v)` corrupting the payload) before the fix; added the same "skip if the value is itself already `Ty::Var(_)`" guard to all four box/unbox sites that needed it (`crates/mir/src/lower.rs`'s constructor-call-arg and record-field-construction boxing, its variant match-arm-payload unboxing, and `crates/hir/src/lower.rs`'s `resolve_field_ty` for plain `.field` reads). (2) A genuinely pre-existing, unrelated bug found while chasing (1): `Pattern::Constructor`'s HIR lowering (`crates/hir/src/lower.rs`'s `lower_pat`) never substituted a `Ty::Var(0)`-declared variant field's type with the scrutinee's own concrete instantiation argument the way `resolve_field_ty` already did for `.field` reads — so a match-arm binding like `Wrap(v) => v` always typed `v` as `Ty::Var(0)`/`void*` regardless of whether the scrutinee was concrete or still abstract, which fed into the whole `match` expression's own inferred type and silently made an ordinary, *non-generic* function (`fn unwrapAsInt(b: Box<Int>): Int = match b { Wrap(v) => v }`) return `void*` instead of `Int` — confirmed via a real repro producing a genuine C type error before the fix. Fixed by substituting field types for local-binding purposes (keeping the *raw*, unsubstituted `field_types` returned in `HirPat::Constructor` unchanged, since MIR's own box/unbox decision still needs the raw declared shape). (3) A second pre-existing, independent bug: unlike a top-level `fn`, an `impl` method's own params were never inserted into `cx.local_types` at all — so a *reference* to the method's own param inside its own body (`v` in `wrap`'s `Wrap(v)`) fell back to `Ty::Error`, not the correctly-computed `Ty::Var(0)` its own `HirParam.ty` already carried — this is specifically what broke fix (1)'s "already opaque" guard for `impl` methods (the *only* place generic factory functions like `wrap`/`expose` can be defined, since `priv` constructors — item 74 — require calling from inside an `impl` block). Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`: a generic `Box<T>` (sum-type) with `wrap`/`wrapTwice` factory methods, called with `Int`/`Decimal`/`Text` concrete payloads and round-tripped through concrete (non-generic) `unwrapAsInt`/`unwrapAsDecimal`/`unwrapAsText` accessors — all correct, including the exact `Decimal` case that was a hard compile error before this fix, and including the `wrapTwice` (nested-generic-call) case that would have silently corrupted the payload without fix (1). A parallel record-literal-based test (`type Holder<T> = { value: T }`) hit an unrelated, pre-existing *typeck* limitation with generic records (`error[E0200]: expected Holder, found Holder<T>`) before even reaching HIR/MIR — confirmed as out of scope for this item (typeck itself rejects the program) and not attempted. **Explicitly does not cover unboxing a generic function's own bare-`T` *return* value back to a concrete type at the call site** (e.g. `Secret.expose(s): Decimal` — `expose`'s C return type is `void*`, and there is no way to recover the caller's own expected concrete type without a materially bigger fix) — split out as a sharper item 135 (Pending); items 78/79 (`Secret<T>`/`Permission<T>`) remain blocked on that, not this. 4 new tests (2 `crates/hir`, 2 `crates/mir`) covering the local_types and pattern-substitution fixes directly, plus the boxing/no-double-boxing behavior structurally. Full workspace build and test suite clean (0 failures). | `crates/hir`, `crates/mir` |
| 133 | Bug fixed — `certo db pull`'s generated code for nullable columns used a `null` literal that doesn't exist in Certo, and mishandled nullable non-Text columns in `Insert` | Found while investigating item 88 (`live val`), flagged separately, then fixed directly. Two independent, general bugs (every column type, not just one) confirmed by direct reproduction against a real local PostgreSQL instance and `certo check` on the actual generated output before any fix: (1) the generated `fromRow` decoder compared a cell against and returned a bare `null` for nullable fields (`if List.getOrPanic(row, 1) == null then null else ...`) — Certo has no `null` keyword/literal at all, only `Option<T>`/`None`/`Some(...)` (confirmed: `error[E0206]: undefined name 'null'`). (2) The generated `Insert`'s SQL-param list did `record.field ?? ""` for every nullable field regardless of type, assuming `Text?` — a real type error for any other nullable type (`error[E0200]: type mismatch: expected Text, found Decimal(19, 4)`). Fixed both in `crates/cli/src/main.rs`'s `cmd_db_pull`: `null` → `None` throughout; the decoded value's `else` branch wrapped in `Some(...)` (extracted into a new, directly-testable `nullable_fromrow_expr`, since both `if` branches must be the same `T?` and the raw decode helper returns a bare `T`); `text_to_certo_expr`/`certo_to_text_expr`/`null_default` (the per-type conversion-expression helpers `Insert` already used correctly *by type*, just not by nullability) now handle the null-check/`Some`-wrap uniformly via the already-nullable-aware `certo_nullable_to_text_expr`. **Found and fixed two more real, independent, pre-existing bugs while verifying end-to-end — both required to make any of this actually run, not just parse**: (3) `dbNull()`'s C implementation was named `certo_db_null_param`, but `crates/codegen`'s `c_fn_name` mechanically derives the C symbol from the Certo name with no per-function override mechanism (`dbNull` → `certo_db_null`) — an undefined-symbol *link* error waiting for the first real call to `dbNull()` anywhere in the language, not specific to generated code. Fixed by renaming the C function to match (verified nothing else referenced the old name). (4) `certo db pull`'s generated file never included `import Stdlib.Db`, so `certo build`/`run`'s `uses_db` detection (which gates whether the DB C runtime is linked in at all) never triggered when the generated file was built directly — every `dbQueryTyped`/`dbExec` call inside it was an undefined-symbol link error. Fixed by adding the import to the generated header (noted as a partial fix in the doc comment: this only helps when the file is built directly — a separate file that locally-imports it still needs its own `import Stdlib.Db`, since multi-file import merging doesn't propagate imports for this check, a distinct pre-existing limitation not attempted here). **Found, but explicitly out of scope — a more dangerous manifestation of an already-documented, deliberately-deferred limitation (items 93/124: a multi-field struct can't be boxed into a `List<T>`'s `void*` slot)**: the generated `*FindAll`/`*FindById` functions return `List<T>`/`T?` derived from `List<T>` for any `DbRow` type — for a single-field type this happens to work, but for any real multi-column table (i.e. almost every real table) it's the exact same struct-boxing gap items 93/124 already found for `List<Point>` etc., except here it wasn't caught at compile time the way a `for`-loop over the list is — `List.first(rows)` inside the generated `*FindById` compiles cleanly and then **segfaults at runtime**, a silent memory-corruption failure mode strictly worse than the compile-time rejection items 93/124 achieved elsewhere — see item 134 for the full investigation and design sketch (turned out to be two distinct, general gaps, not just a DB-specific one). Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`/binary against a real local PostgreSQL instance, working around the above by testing the INSERT and `fromRow`-decode halves independently rather than through the crashing `List<Widgets>`-returning functions: a real row with values (`name`, `qty`, `price` with exact `19.9900` precision preserved, `active`, `created_at`) and a real row with SQL NULLs in every nullable column both round-tripped correctly — confirmed the inserted rows directly via `psql` (real values for one row, real blank/NULL for the other), and confirmed `fromRow` decodes both correctly by feeding it hand-built `List<Text?>` values directly (bypassing the crash) — real values decode to `Some(...)`, real NULLs decode to `None`, for every column type tested (`Text`, `Int`, `Decimal(19,4)`, `Bool`, `DateTime`). 8 new `crates/cli` unit tests covering every helper touched (no `null` literal ever emitted, `Some(...)` wrapping present, `Decimal(p,s)` still recognized post-item-132, `parseDecimal` not `parseInt` used for Decimal). Full workspace build and test suite clean (0 failures). | `crates/cli`, `crates/stdlib` |
| 91 | `certo add`/`certo audit` — dependency manifest bookkeeping | Scoped deliberately, not guessed at: `[dependencies]` in `certo.toml` only ever lists *stdlib* module names (`"Stdlib.Http" = "*"`, confirmed via `cmd_new`'s template) — there's no package registry, fetch mechanism, or lockfile anywhere in this toolchain, so a real third-party package manager here would be exactly the kind of half-built fake infrastructure this project's own standard rules out (same treatment as items 76/117/118/122/126/128). Scoped instead to what's real: keeping the manifest honest against what the source actually `import`s. **`certo add <module>`**: validates against `STDLIB_MODULES`, a list mirroring the exact `pub use *_C` modules `crates/stdlib/src/lib.rs` registers (`Core`, `Bytes`, `Credential`, `Collections`, `Channel`, `Result`, `Text`, `DateTime`, `Money`, `Db`, `DbQuery`, `DbMutation`, `Env`, `File`, `Path`, `Process`, `Json`, `Http`, `Math`, `Crypto`, `Regex`, `Csv`) — an unknown name is a clear error, not a manifest entry for a module that will never resolve. Accepts either bare (`Http`) or dotted (`Stdlib.Http`) form, normalizing to the dotted form every real `.cto`/`certo.toml` in this codebase actually uses. Edits the manifest via `toml_edit::DocumentMut` (new direct `crates/cli` dependency, `toml_edit = "0.22"` — already present transitively via `toml` 0.8, so this adds no new crate to the dependency tree, just a direct edge to one already being compiled) rather than round-tripping through `serde`, so existing comments/formatting/ordering survive — confirmed via a real edit that a hand-written `# comment` and the file's existing key order are both untouched afterward. Idempotent: adding an already-declared module prints a note and exits cleanly rather than duplicating the key or erroring. Every edit is sanity-checked by re-parsing the result through the same strict, `deny_unknown_fields` `certo_toml::CertoToml` loader before touching disk — a `toml_edit` bug would be caught immediately rather than silently corrupting the user's manifest. **`certo audit [--strict]`**: parses the entry file (`[build] entry`) and diffs its declared `import`s against `[dependencies]` keys — "imported but not declared" is always an error (exit 1, with the exact `certo add` fix suggested inline); "declared but never imported" is a warning by default, promoted to a failure under `--strict` (mirrors the existing `certo check --strict` convention rather than inventing a new flag shape). Deliberately scoped to the entry file's own top-level imports only, not a transitive walk through locally-imported sibling files — matches the *only* place import declarations already drive real compiler behavior today (`cmd_build`'s pre-existing `uses_db` check, which has the identical single-level scope), rather than inventing a deeper multi-file resolution pass that audit alone would need to newly justify. 4 new `crates/cli` unit tests (bare/dotted name normalization, unknown-module rejection listing valid names, and a real `toml_edit` insertion that verifies both comment-preservation and that the result still parses through the strict schema). Full workspace build and test suite clean (0 failures). Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe` against a scaffolded project: `certo audit` on an unmodified project (declared `Stdlib.Text`, source importing `Stdlib.Core`/`Stdlib.Http` instead) correctly reported both imports as missing and `Stdlib.Text` as unused, exit 1; `certo add Stdlib.Core` and `certo add Http` (bare-form, auto-normalized) both wrote correct entries while preserving every existing comment; a repeat `certo add Http` correctly no-op'd; `certo audit --strict` correctly failed on the still-unused `Stdlib.Text` entry while plain `certo audit` only warned; after removing that entry, `certo audit --strict` passed clean (exit 0); `certo add Bogus` was correctly rejected, listing all 22 valid module names. | `crates/cli` |
| 93 | OpenAPI 3.x (and custom-JSON) REST client generation, with real typed model (de)serialization — `ffi::rest` previously only generated a client whose functions claimed a parsed response type but never actually parsed anything | The pre-existing `ffi::rest` generator was fundamentally non-functional the moment it was pointed at the real compiler, not just missing OpenAPI support: it called nonexistent bare functions (`http_get`/`http_post` — the real names are qualified, `Http.get`/`Http.post`), called `Http.get`/`Http.delete` with a second structured query-params argument those functions don't take, used a `.toString()` method that doesn't exist anywhere in Certo's stdlib for path-param interpolation, declared `[async, io]` when `Http.*` functions are only ever `Io`-effectful (`Async` is a distinct, unrelated Certo effect — declared-async concurrency/spawn, not "does I/O"), and — most importantly — declared a function's return type as the parsed model (`User`) while its body just returned the raw, un-decoded `HttpResponse`, which wouldn't even type-check. Fixed all of these, then built real typed (de)serialization on top: a schema's new `models` section (named record shapes, primitive/`List<T>`/nested-model fields only) generates a `type X = {...}` declaration plus `__decodeX(jv: JsonValue): X`/`__encodeX(v: X): JsonValue` functions per model — modeled directly on the codebase's own existing `dbQueryTyped` caller-supplied-mapper convention, not new invented magic — and every endpoint now returns `Result<T, Text>`, decoding a 2xx body via the generated decoder and `Err`ing the raw body otherwise; a model-typed request body gets JSON-encoded the same way before `Http.post`/`Http.put`. Added `crates/ffi/src/openapi.rs`: parses an OpenAPI 3.x JSON document's `paths` and `components.schemas` into the exact same `RestSchema`/`Model` shape the hand-written JSON schema already produces, reusing `generate_client` completely unchanged — `$ref` resolves to a model name, `array`/`items` resolves to `List<T>`, and `oneOf`/`anyOf`/`allOf`/inline-anonymous-object schemas are rejected with a clear error naming the offending schema rather than silently emitting `void*`/`Text` junk for them. **Found and fixed four more real, pre-existing bugs while verifying end-to-end against the real compiler — none introduced by this item, all newly confirmed by it**: (1) `Ty::Named{"JsonValue"}` was missing from `crates/codegen/src/ty_to_c.rs`'s opaque-type table (same gap items 75/128 already fixed for `DateTime`/`Date`/`Duration`) — an explicit `JsonValue`-typed parameter/return/local (this item's decode/encode functions are the first code to ever write one, everything before only used `JsonValue` via untyped `val` bindings) emitted the literal undeclared C identifier `JsonValue` instead of the real `CertoJsonValue*`. (2) `JsonValue.push`'s C alias pointed straight at the raw `void`-returning `certo_json_array_push` instead of the already-existing, correctly `int64_t`-returning `certo_json_array_append` wrapper every other `Unit`-returning `JsonValue.*` function uses — a real "assigning to int64_t from incompatible type void" compile error. (3) `certo_range`'s C implementation (backing `range`/`rangeInclusive`, used by this item's `List<T>` JSON-decode loop) allocated a completely different, incompatible memory layout than the real `CertoList{int64_t len; int64_t cap; void** data;}` struct every `List.*` function expects — `List.len(range(...))` happened to return the right answer (byte offset 0 is `len` in both layouts by coincidence) while `List.get`/`for`-loop iteration read `cap`/`data` from whatever raw range values occupied those offsets, producing garbage element values or segfaulting outright when a bogus `data` pointer got dereferenced; confirmed as a real, reproducible crash via a minimal isolated repro (`for i in range(0,3) { println(i) }` segfaulted) before being traced to this. Fixed by moving `certo_range`/`certo_range_inclusive` into `collections.rs` (needs `CertoList`/`list_alloc`, defined there, not in `core.rs` where they previously lived and were emitted *before* the C runtime's list machinery even existed in the translation unit) and rebuilding them via `list_alloc` with properly boxed `int64_t` elements. (4) `seed.rs`'s `pm!("Http.post"/"Http.put", "url", "content_type", "body")` named-argument metadata had `content_type`/`body` in the opposite order from the real C implementation (`certo_http_post(url, body, content_type)`, confirmed against `docs/STDLIB-QUICKREF.md` too) — silently swapping the two for any named-argument call; this item's own generated `Http.post` call used the (correct, positional) order matching `pm!`'s wrong labels, sending the content-type string as the request body until traced via a real local HTTP round-trip. **Also found and fixed a real bug in this item's own new code, not the pre-existing runtime**: an *optional* field whose type is itself composite (`List<T>?`, not just a scalar `T?`) needs its `emit_encode_value` statements (the array-building loop) placed *inside* the `Some(x) => { ... }` match arm, not spliced in before the `match`'s arms entirely — a real syntax error, only surfaced once an OpenAPI spec's `required` list left a `List<T>` field optional (this item's own hand-written test schema's `tags` field was never optional, so the bug shipped past every existing test until OpenAPI generation exercised the combination). Explicitly out of scope, each for a stated reason: `List<Model>`/`List<Sum>` as a list's *direct* element (same confirmed struct-boxing gap item 124 already found — a real multi-field struct can't be cast through `intptr_t` into a `void*` list slot); anonymous inline record/object schemas (the compiler itself represents these as `void*` with no real struct ever emitted, unrelated pre-existing gap); per-status-code typed error variants (the spec's own `Result<Charge, StripeError>` framing — every endpoint here resolves to `Result<T, Text>` instead, a real but simpler mechanism); the spec's `@apiClient(url) module X` build-time annotation that downloads the spec over the network during every compile (`--openapi` takes a local file only) — same "every build needs network access" category item 72b was deferred over, not attempted here; YAML OpenAPI documents (JSON only — `serde_yaml` isn't a workspace dependency and wasn't judged worth adding for this alone). 37 new `crates/ffi` tests (schema validation, codegen correctness for every HTTP method/param shape/model nesting, the four bug-regression cases above, and 13 OpenAPI-specific tests covering `$ref`/array/required resolution, response-code selection, operationId synthesis, and the same struct-boxing rejection surfacing through OpenAPI's own array-of-`$ref` shape). Full workspace build and test suite clean (0 failures, 0 warnings). Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`/`certo-ffi.exe`, twice — once per input format (hand-written JSON schema, then an equivalent OpenAPI 3.x document) — against a real local HTTP round-trip (a second tiny Certo program using `Http.serve` as the mock server, not a stub): `POST`-with-typed-body encode, `GET`-with-path-param decode (including a `None` and a `Some` branch of an optional `List<Text>?` field), `GET`-with-query-params (mixing an optional and a required param) decoding a `List<Text>` response, and `DELETE` returning `Result<Unit, Text>`, all passing with correct decoded values. | `crates/ffi`, `crates/codegen`, `crates/stdlib` |

| 126 | `test --coverage` — per-line coverage report mapped back to the original `.cto` source, not generated C | The Pending note below was right that the compiler flag alone (`-fprofile-instr-generate -fcoverage-mapping`) would be dishonest without real source-mapping — but the fix it sketched (`#line` directives, "similar in spirit to a source map") turned out to not work at all: **confirmed by direct testing against LLVM 22 that `llvm-cov` completely ignores `#line` for coverage attribution** — neither `llvm-cov report`'s file grouping nor `llvm-cov show`'s line numbers shift for a `#line` pragma, even when the target file physically exists on disk (isolated repro: a 7-line C file with `#line 100 "fake_source.txt"` before its `return` statement still reports as `line_test.c` lines 1-7, never `fake_source.txt`/100). This was caught by actually running the shipped implementation against a real `.cto` test file before calling it done, not by inspecting the generated C — the naive version compiled cleanly, ran cleanly, and printed *a* coverage report, silently keyed to the anonymous generated-C temp file the whole time. Pivoted to owning the remap ourselves instead of trusting the toolchain: `crates/mir/src/mir.rs`/`lower.rs` thread a `Span` onto `MirStmt::Assign` (set at statement/body boundaries only — `Let`/`Assign`/`Expr` statements and function/lambda bodies — deliberately not per sub-expression, so a multi-line `if/else`-chain *expression* used as a function body shares one span for all its branches; documented as a real, known granularity limit, not silently pretended away). `crates/codegen/src/emit_mir.rs`'s `LineMap` converts a byte offset to a 1-based line (binary search over cached newline positions) and `emit_fn_inner` emits `#line <n> "<file>"` whenever the active statement's line changes — `#line` is kept, but now purely as an internal encoding for `crates/testrunner/src/coverage.rs` to decode on our own side, not something handed to `llvm-cov` to interpret. Two more real toolchain bugs found and fixed along the way, both confirmed via isolated repros before touching the actual pipeline: (1) an unescaped Windows path inside the `#line "..."` string literal — clang reads `\U`/`\A`/etc. inside it as invalid escape sequences (`LineMap` now also stores a backslash/quote-escaped `filename_escaped`); (2) this LLVM build's `llvm-profdata merge -sparse -o <file> <inputs...>` silently misparses that argument order (the output path gets treated as a nonexistent extra input, leaving `merged.profdata` incomplete), and `llvm-cov export -instr-profile=<file>` (the `=` form) is similarly misparsed — both fixed by using space-separated args and putting `-o` after the inputs, confirmed by direct manual reproduction of each failure before and success after. `coverage.rs`'s `build_line_remap` re-parses `#line` directives out of the exact `c_src` string that was compiled (so physical line numbers line up exactly), but deliberately does **not** follow real C `#line` semantics (where each subsequent physical line implicitly increments the logical line) — this codegen only re-emits a directive when the *source* line actually changes, so a run of generated C lines from one statement all share that one target line; a first attempt that auto-incremented produced nonsense line numbers past the end of the actual `.cto` file, caught immediately by testing against a real 20-line source and seeing "line 47" in the report. Needed a second real fix for the same reason: without an explicit reset, `#line` attribution persists (correctly, per real C semantics) into whatever comes next — the following function's preamble, or the rest of the file — so `emit_fn_inner` now also emits a `#line 1 "<generated>"` sentinel at the end of every function and after any synthetic unspanned statement, which `build_line_remap` recognizes as "back to untracked" rather than a real target file. `llvm-cov export -format=text`'s JSON `segments` array (`[line, col, count, hasCount, isRegionEntry, isGapRegion]`) only marks where an execution-count *region begins*, not every line it covers — `coverage.rs`'s `line_active_counts` forward-fills the same way `llvm-cov show` does when it prints a count next to every source line. A **third** real bug here, caught by a unit test rather than eyeballing: the forward-fill originally stopped at the last segment's own line instead of true end-of-file, so a final function with nothing emitted after it (no closing segment to "supersede" its count) silently dropped out of the report; fixed by passing the real total line count through instead of deriving a max from the segments themselves. Added `serde_json` as a direct `crates/testrunner` dependency to parse the export JSON (ad-hoc `Value` navigation, not a generated schema — the shape needed is narrow and stable). 8 new `crates/testrunner/src/coverage.rs` unit tests (`#line` parsing and Windows-path unescaping, same-line sharing across consecutive statements, the `<generated>` sentinel reset, forward-fill including the boundary case that caught bug #3 above, and a full `render_report` case distinguishing a called line from an uncalled one) plus 2 new `crates/codegen/src/emit_mir.rs` tests for `LineMap` (line-number lookup, path escaping). Full workspace build and test suite clean. Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`, twice: a scratch `.cto` file with a called function, an if/else-chain function (both branches exercised across two tests), and a deliberately uncalled function — the report correctly showed 5/6 lines covered with only the uncalled function's line listed as missed, confirmed by then also checking that removing the uncalled function brought it to 5/5 (100%); and a second run against the pre-existing `examples/math_test.cto` (16 real tests over factorial/fibonacci/isPrime/gcd/clamp) reporting a clean 20/20 (100%), matching that file's genuinely thorough coverage. | `crates/mir`, `crates/codegen`, `crates/testrunner`, `crates/cli` |

| 135 | Unbox a generic function's own bare-`T` *return* value back to a concrete type at an arbitrary call site — scoped slice, not the full feature; unblocks items 78/79's `expose`-style accessors for the two contexts covered | Investigated first (see item 120 in the Completed table above, whose own writeup flagged this as the remaining gap) — confirmed by direct code reading that typeck resolves each call's concrete `T` internally (a real `UnionFind` substitution table, `crates/typeck/src/unify.rs`) but only ever returns pass/fail, never exporting it, and that HIR's `Stmt::Val`'s own `ty: Option<S<TypeExpr>>` annotation was already being destructured away via `..` and never read (`crates/hir/src/lower.rs`'s `lower_block`). Full expected-type-hint propagation through all of HIR (arguments, nested calls, everywhere) was ruled out as too large for one slice; scoped down to two concrete, sound contexts instead, chosen so unresolvable cases fail with a clear compile error rather than silently shipping a wrong `void*` cast: **(1) `val x: ConcreteType = generic.method(...)`** — the declared annotation is no longer discarded; a new `resolve_bare_generic_return(expr, expected, cx)` overrides a `Call` node's unresolved `Ty::Var(0)` sentinel with the annotation's concrete type. **(2) argument position with a concrete declared param** — `outer(generic.method(...))` resolves through `outer`'s own declared param type at that position (`cx.fn_param_tys`, already built for item 120's argument-boxing) the same way, so e.g. `addOne(Box.unwrap(myBox))` works without needing an intermediate `val`. Neither context has an available concrete type (an unannotated `val`, or an argument position whose declared param is itself still generic, e.g. passing into another `fn identity<T>(v: T): T`) → `cx.err(...)`, a real E0601 compile error naming exactly what's missing, confirmed via direct testing to correctly reject both cases rather than silently leaving `Ty::Var(0)` to flow through. Return-position-of-an-enclosing-function and other arbitrary contexts remain unresolved, same as before this item — not silently broken by this change, just not newly fixed either. New `HirModule.fn_ret_tys: HashMap<String, Ty>` (mirroring the existing `fn_param_tys`, sourced from the same `cx.fn_ret_types` HIR already built) threads each callee's *declared* return type through to MIR, generalizing `crates/mir/src/lower.rs`'s previous `RAW_RETURN_CALLEES` hardcoded stdlib-only allowlist (`List.getOrPanic`) into a real per-callee check: any call whose declared return is bare-`T` and whose HIR type is now concrete (via the above) needs its return value unboxed. **Found and fixed a real bug via direct end-to-end testing, not caught by any type-level reasoning**: the natural first implementation used `Rvalue::Unbox`/`unbox_value` (the same mechanism `List.getOrPanic` uses) to unbox the return value — compiled and ran, but printed the raw heap address instead of the boxed value for a plain `Int`, while a `Decimal` payload printed correctly by coincidence. Root cause: this codebase has two genuinely different, incompatible boxing conventions for a `Ty::Var(0)` slot — `Rvalue::Unbox`/`unbox_value` bit-packs a pointer-sized scalar directly into the pointer's own bits with **no allocation at all** (only real structs get `malloc`'d), while item 120's own argument-boxing (`Rvalue::BoxSome`, what actually produced this payload — the *argument* passed into `Box.wrap`'s bare-`T` param) **always** `malloc`s regardless of the concrete type. Fixed by using `Rvalue::UnboxSome` (an unconditional dereference, the correct pairing for `BoxSome`) for this item's own unboxing, leaving `List.getOrPanic`'s existing `Rvalue::Unbox` path untouched. 6 new unit tests: 4 in `crates/hir/src/tests.rs` covering both resolution contexts and both error cases, 2 in `crates/mir/src/tests.rs` (one pinning the exact `UnboxSome`-not-`Unbox` bug above so it can't silently regress). Full workspace build and test suite clean. Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe` against a hand-written `type Box<T> = priv Box(T)` wrapper (the same shape `Secret<T>`/item 78 will eventually use, which doesn't exist as a real feature yet): `val x: Int = Box.unwrap(Box.wrap(42))` correctly printed `42` (not a heap address); `val d: Decimal = Box.unwrap(Box.wrap(Decimal.fromInt(7)))` correctly printed `7`; `val y: Int = addOne(Box.unwrap(Box.wrap(10)))` (argument-position resolution) correctly printed `11`; an unannotated `val x = Box.unwrap(Box.wrap(42))` correctly produced a clear E0601 error instead of compiling; `identity(Box.unwrap(Box.wrap(42)))` (argument position into another generic's still-generic param) also correctly errored rather than silently accepting it. | `crates/hir`, `crates/mir`, `crates/codegen`, `crates/llvm` |

| 78 | `Secret<T>` type (uncloggable/unserializable) — `type Secret<T> = priv Secret(T)` + `.wrap`/`.expose`, plus a real compile-time "not Loggable/Serializable" check | Investigated first: the spec (`docs/Certo_Language_Specification.md` §13.2) names `Log.info`/`Json.encode` as the blocked sinks, but **neither exists in this compiler** (`crates/stdlib/src/seed.rs` only has `print`/`println`/`eprint` and `Json.stringify`/`parse`/`object`, no `Log` module, no `Json.encode`) — confirmed by direct grep before writing anything, not assumed from the spec. Also confirmed there's no prelude/auto-import mechanism anywhere in this codebase, so `Secret<T>` can't be silently "built into the language" — a user declares `type Secret<T> = priv Secret(T)` + `impl<T> Secret { fn wrap(v: T): Secret<T> = Secret(v); fn expose(s: Secret<T>): T = match s { Secret(v) => v } }` themselves, the exact generic-sum-type shape item 135 already proved out via its own `Box<T>` test. Scoped to gate the *real* sinks instead of the spec's aspirational ones: `println`/`print`/`eprint`/`Json.stringify`. New `Ty::contains_secret(&self) -> bool` (`crates/typeck/src/ty.rs`) structurally walks a type — itself or nested inside `Option`/`Result`/`List`/`Map`/`Tuple`/`Record`/another `Named`'s own type args — matching by name (`"Secret"`) since there's no dedicated `Ty::Secret` variant, same "no builtin needed" reasoning as above. Wired into `Expr::App`'s call inference (`crates/typeck/src/infer_expr.rs`) as a hard error (new `TypeErrorKind::SecretInSensitiveContext`, code **E0215** — the spec's own suggested E0601 was already in use for HIR lowering errors from item 136, so this continues typeck's own E02xx sequence instead of colliding) checked after unification, against every argument passed to one of the four sinks. **Found and fixed a second real bug via direct end-to-end testing, not caught by unit tests alone**: `println(Secret.expose(dbPassword))` compiled but printed garbage instead of the real value. Root cause: item 135's own argument-position resolution (`resolve_bare_generic_return`'s caller in `crates/hir/src/lower.rs`) only ever consulted `cx.fn_param_tys` — populated *exclusively* from user-defined `fn`/`impl` declarations, never stdlib signatures — so a bare-`T`-returning call passed directly to a *stdlib* function like `println` silently stayed as the raw unboxed pointer (no resolution, no error) instead of either working or failing to compile, a real gap in item 135's own shipped scope that only this item's own end-to-end verification surfaced. Fixed by adding `stdlib_param_types()` (mirroring the existing `stdlib_ret_types()` — built from the same real `TypeEnv` seeding via `certo_stdlib::seed_stdlib`, not hand-maintained) and consulting it as a fallback alongside `cx.fn_param_tys`, so this fix benefits *any* future bare-T-returning call passed straight into a stdlib function, not just this item's own four sinks. Known minor cosmetic gap, not chased further: a rejected call's error message can show an unresolved type-variable placeholder (`Secret<?t102>`) instead of the concrete type when unification with the sink's own param type failed before resolving it — the load-bearing part of the message (that it's specifically a `Secret`) is still correct; only the inner `T` display is imprecise. 9 new unit tests: 5 in `crates/typeck/src/ty.rs` (`contains_secret`'s direct/unrelated/nested-in-compound/nested-in-another-wrapper cases), 3 in `crates/typeck/src/tests.rs` (rejected-direct-to-println, allowed-after-expose, rejected-to-eprint), 1 in `crates/hir/src/tests.rs` pinning the `stdlib_param_types` bug above so it can't silently regress. Full workspace build and test suite clean.
