Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: the spec's own exact example shape (an `impl ShoppingCart` method taking bare `self`, `item: Product`, `qty: Int`) compiles and runs correctly, calling `.push(...)` on `self`'s own field and returning a new `ShoppingCart`; item 150's in-body `fn increment(self): Counter` form (a record body method, not an `impl` block) verified separately and also correct; a `trait` method with a bare `self` confirmed to still parse (no regression to a harder failure) and typecheck without complaint, matching the documented, deliberate scope boundary. | `crates/parser` |

| 201 | Record/enum structural equality (`==`/`!=`) now compiles and works — scoped to scalars/Text/Decimal/UUID/nested records/enums, confirmed with the user before implementing | **Investigated real scope before implementing**: the item's own repro (`Money = { amount: Decimal(19,4), currency: Currency }`) revealed two compounding gaps beyond the filed one — `Decimal`/`UUID` are themselves plain-value C structs with zero `==` wiring of their own (`d1 == d2` alone already failed to compile), and "full structural equality" could reasonably be read to include `List`/`Option`/`Map`-typed fields too, which would need new deep-equality runtime helpers touching the same generic-erasure architecture item 200 is stuck on. Presented both findings; user confirmed scope: scalars + Text + Decimal + UUID + nested records/enums, with List/Option/Map/Tuple/Fn-typed fields explicitly excluded — `==` on a record containing one stays exactly as broken as today (a clean C compile error), not silently wrong. **Design**: a one-time pre-pass (`build_type_shapes`, `crates/codegen/src/emit_module.rs`) classifies every user `type` as a plain record, a nullary enum (already correct via native `==`, an int), or a payload-carrying enum, then `ty_is_comparable` recursively decides whether a given field type can be compared at all (scalars/Text/Decimal/UUID always can; a nested record/enum can when *its* fields all can; a generic type param, an `optional` field, or any `List`/`Option`/`Map`/`Tuple`/`Fn`-typed field never can, matching item 200's own "erasure means no single correct comparison here" finding). For every comparable record, a real `static inline bool certo_eq_<Name>(Name a, Name b)` is generated right after its own struct typedef (field-by-field, dispatching per field to `certo_text_eq`/`certo_decimal_eq`/`certo_uuid_eq`/a recursive `certo_eq_<Inner>` call/native `==` as appropriate); for a payload enum, the generated function compares the tag first, then (only on a tag match) the active variant's own fields the identical way. `crates/codegen/src/emit_mir.rs`'s `BinOp::Eq`/`NotEq` now dispatch through this instead of always emitting a bare `==` — extended the exact same special-casing `Text` already had (previously the *only* non-scalar type handled) to also check `Decimal`/`UUID` directly and any type name in the newly-built `eq_types` registry. **A real bug found and fixed while verifying end-to-end**: a *nullary* variant of an otherwise payload-carrying enum (`Origin` in `type Shape = | Circle(radius: Float) | Origin`) is a bare global constant (`Operand::Global`, not `Operand::Local`) whose static type `operand_ty` has no `MirLocalDecl` to recover at all — `Origin == Origin` failed to compile even after the main fix landed. Added a second small registry, `variant_parent` (nullary-variant name → its parent sum type's name, built from the exact same sum-type declarations `eq_types` is derived from), so `emit_binop` can still find the right `certo_eq_<Name>` function for this one case `operand_ty` structurally can't reach. 14 new `crates/codegen` tests (plain-record, nested-record, payload-enum, and the nullary-mixed-enum-global cases all dispatch correctly; a `List`-field record, a generic record, and an `optional`-field record are all confirmed to get *no* generated function, leaving `==` exactly as broken as before rather than silently wrong; plain scalar/Decimal/UUID equality confirmed unaffected/fixed respectively), 1 new `crates/stdlib` test (`certo_uuid_eq`'s body is present). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: the item's own two repros — `Money{amount, currency}` and a plain `Point{x, y}` — both now correctly report `true`/`false` for matching/differing values; a payload enum (`Circle`/`Rectangle`) and its own nullary sibling (`Origin`) all compare correctly, including cross-variant (`Circle == Rectangle` → `false`); a nested record (`Person{name, address: Address}`) recurses correctly into the inner type; bare `Decimal`/`UUID` equality (outside any record) also confirmed fixed; a record with a `List<Int>` field confirmed to still fail to compile cleanly, exactly as before, not silently wrong. | `crates/codegen`, `crates/stdlib` |
| 204 | Spec §6.4's DB-query example (`Stdlib.Db`, multi-line SQL string with backslash continuation, `dbQueryTyped([cutoffDate])`) now parses and typechecks | Confirmed both symptoms as filed, root-caused each separately rather than papering over them. The backslash-continued string (`"...FROM orders o \` / newline / `    JOIN customers...`) doesn't parse at all — traced the lexer's `StringLit` regex (`"([^"\\]|\\.)*"`) and confirmed `\\.` never matches a literal newline in this engine, so a trailing-backslash line continuation genuinely isn't, and never was, real Certo syntax inside a plain string literal; Certo's actual multi-line-string mechanism is the separate triple-quoted `"""..."""` token, confirmed working correctly by direct test (embeds real newlines, which SQL doesn't care about between tokens). `[cutoffDate]` on its own separately failed to typecheck (`E0200: expected Text, found DateTime`) — checked `dbQueryTyped`'s real registered signature (`crates/stdlib/src/seed.rs:1018`, `∀T. (Int, Text, List<Text>, List<Text?> -> T) -> List<T>`) confirming every SQL parameter must already be `Text`, not a bug, a real constraint the doc's own example violated. Neither was a compiler gap; both were the doc's own example using invalid/unconverted syntax. Fixed `docs/Certo_Language_Specification.md` §6.4: `uses Stdlib.Db` → `import Stdlib.Db`, the backslash-continued string → real triple-quoted `"""..."""` form, `[cutoffDate]` → `[cutoffDate.toIso()]`. No code changes; verified by extracting the corrected example into a standalone `.cto` file and confirming it parses, typechecks, and compiles clean via the real `certo.exe`. | `docs` |
| 205 | Spec §6.1's `cmd_db_pull`-generated model example (`type User { ... }`, missing `=`) doesn't match what `cmd_db_pull` actually generates | Confirmed the filed symptom (missing `=` after `type User`) but investigation surfaced it was one symptom of a naming-convention mismatch, not just a typo: checked `cmd_db_pull`'s real generation logic (`crates/cli/src/main.rs:3221`, `snake_to_pascal(table_name)` with no singularization) and confirmed a Postgres table `users` really does produce Certo type `Users` (plural), never `User` — the doc's example was written against a naming convention the tool never actually implements. Fixed §6.1: `type User { ... }` → `type Users = { ... }` (fixing the `=` and the name together), plus `fn userFromRow` → `fn usersFromRow` and `impl DbRow for User` → `impl DbRow for Users` to match. Attempting to verify this against a *real* generated model (rather than trust the doc in isolation) led to compiling the checked-in `examples/db/schema.cto` (a 1922-line, ~51-table real DB dump) for the first time in a long while, which turned out to be far more stale than the filed item — required three separate user check-ins as the true scope kept escalating: (1) `null` (1095 occurrences) isn't valid Certo syntax at all — bulk-substituted to `None` (confirmed safe: no uppercase `NULL` anywhere, so no SQL string literal was touched, and the unrelated `dbNull` function's identifier is untouched by a word-boundary substitution); (2) that substitution alone surfaced 496 *new* type errors — the file's nullable-field pattern (`if x == None then None else <bare value>`) needs the non-null branch's value explicitly `Some(...)`-wrapped under today's real tagged `Option<T>` semantics, unlike whatever older nullable-T convention the file was originally written against — user confirmed doing this rewrite too, applied via a precise line-based script to all 365 matching lines; (3) after also discovering and fixing 44 stale `Character`/`Character?` type references (an unmapped/removed Certo type name → `Text`/`Text?`) and 9 stale `Time with time zone?` references (see the `pg_type_to_certo` fix below), the file dropped to 51 errors, all `E0206: undefined name` on capitalized record field access (e.g. `row.Name`) — user confirmed investigating this as a real, possibly-general compiler bug rather than working around it in the example file alone. **The real bug**: `parse_module_path` (`crates/parser/src/parse_type.rs`) only checked each *subsequent* path segment's case when deciding whether to keep consuming a dotted path, never the *first* segment's — so `f.Zebra` (lowercase local, capitalized field) was wrongly swallowed whole as a bogus 2-segment `Expr::Path` at the atom level, before `parse_postfix`'s real field-access handling ever saw the dot; the path's own inference (`crates/typeck/src/infer_expr.rs`, `Expr::Path` case) then looked up `"Zebra"` directly as a top-level name via `path.segments.last()`, producing exactly the filed `E0206`. Confirmed via a minimal `Zebra`-field repro that this is general (not specific to any field literally named `Name`), and unrelated to items 119/120/142/200's erasure-boundary bug class. Fixed by capturing `first_is_upper` right after parsing the first segment and gating the entire continuation loop on it, so a lowercase-first-segment path never continues past the first dot regardless of the next segment's case. Also fixed `pg_type_to_certo`'s (`crates/cli/src/main.rs`) type-mapping catch-all: an unrecognized Postgres type name fell to `other => snake_to_pascal(other)`, which only splits on `_`, never spaces — so a raw multi-word PG type like `"time with time zone"` produced the near-untouched, undeclared, unparseable Certo type name `Time with time zone`; added explicit `"time" \| "time with time zone" \| "time without time zone" \| "timetz" => "Text".to_string()` mappings alongside the pre-existing `timestamp with time zone → DateTime` mapping (left unchanged). 5 new `crates/parser` tests (capitalized single- and chained-field access now parses as `Expr::Field`, not `Expr::Path`; real module-qualified paths (`Stdlib.Result.Ok`), type-qualified dot-calls (`List.map`), and plain lowercase field access all confirmed unaffected). 2 new `crates/cli` tests (`time`/`time with time zone`/`time without time zone`/`timetz` all map to `Text`; the pre-existing `timestamp with time zone → DateTime` mapping confirmed unaffected). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean (1321 total tests passing), 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: the corrected §6.1 doc example compiles clean; `examples/db/schema.cto` went from "doesn't parse at all" to compiling down to 43 remaining errors, all now distinct, well-understood, newly-discovered gaps unrelated to anything filed here (see items 228/229 below) rather than anything already investigated; the `f.Zebra`/capitalized-field-access fix confirmed directly via both a minimal repro and the full schema file, with `Stdlib.Result.Ok`/`List.map`/plain field access all confirmed to still parse identically to before. | `crates/parser`, `crates/cli` |

Verified by re-reading both sections together post-fix and confirming the model name now agrees throughout the document. | `docs` |

Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. | `crates/hir` |
| 223 | A type error inside validator- or state-machine-*generated* code renders against the wrong source text — fixed via a real span-rewriting pass, not a design sketch | Reproduced exactly as filed before implementing: a validator rule using a named `constraint` whose body references an undefined field (`customer.wrongField`) produces two real type errors (one from each of the generated `V_validate`/`V_validateAll` functions, since a constraint's body is only checked once inlined into generated code — never directly against the original `Decl::Validator` AST, per item 225's own finding) — but pre-fix, both rendered against nonsensical, unrelated locations in the real file (one pointed at a blank line, the other at the wrong substring on a coincidentally-overlapping line), confirmed via a live `certo build` run and cross-checked directly against the pre-fix binary (git-stashed the fix, rebuilt, reproduced, then restored). **Fix, per the user's own confirmed approach** (offset + append into one combined source, not an origin-tagged side-channel): `expand_state_machines`/`expand_validators` (`crates/cli/src/main.rs`) now take a `&mut String` "combined source" buffer that starts as an exact copy of the real file's own text (so every real declaration's existing span, relative to offset 0, still indexes into it correctly) and grows by exactly the bytes of each freshly-parsed generated chunk, appended in the same order it's spliced into `module.decls`. Before splicing, every span in the freshly-parsed generated subtree is rewritten by adding the combined source's current length as an offset — new file `crates/cli/src/span_rewrite.rs`, a recursive visitor (`offset_decl_spans`) scoped at the top level to `Decl::Fn`/`Decl::Type` (the only two declaration kinds either generator ever produces, confirmed by inspecting their own codegen output) but exhaustive with no catch-all arms through every `Expr`/`Pattern`/`TypeExpr`/`Stmt` variant reachable underneath — deliberately exhaustive (not just covering constructs currently generated) so the compiler forces an update if either generator starts emitting a new expression shape later, rather than silently leaving it un-rewritten and reopening this exact bug. `cmd_build` (the only caller of either expansion function) now threads this combined buffer through to the single `run_typeck_opts_with_root` call instead of the raw file source. **2 new `crates/cli` tests** (`span_rewrite_tests` module) call the real `expand_state_machines`/`expand_validators`/`certo_typeck::check_module_seeded` pipeline directly and assert each reported error's span indexes into the *correct* substring of the returned combined-source buffer — one exercising the validator/constraint path, one the state-machine/invariant path (chosen because neither one's body is ever checked anywhere except inside generated code, making both direct, unavoidable tests of the fix rather than happens-to-pass cases). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: both the validator-constraint and state-machine-invariant repros now render their errors against the real, correct generated code (showing the actual generated function text with the right expression underlined) instead of unrelated real-file text; a pre-existing validator error that *was* already correctly located (checked directly against the original AST, not generated code) confirmed unaffected — still renders at its own correct line, alongside the two now-also-correctly-located generated-code duplicates of the same check; `examples/db/schema.cto` (which uses neither validators nor state machines) confirmed to still produce its own known, unrelated 43 errors unchanged, ruling out a general regression. | `crates/cli` |
| 228 | No callable stdlib function converted a runtime `Text` value into a `UUID`, or a `UUID` back into `Text` — both directions, closing all 43 of `examples/db/schema.cto`'s remaining errors | Re-investigated before implementing: the file's own errors showed the gap needed *both* directions, not just the filed Text→UUID one — 36 errors were `expected UUID, found Text` (reading a row) and 3 more were `expected Text, found UUID` (writing an `Insert` param), confirmed to be one underlying gap (UUID had zero Certo-level conversion functions at all) rather than two separate items. User confirmed both directions, `parseUuid` fallible (matching `parseInt`/`parseFloat`/`parseDecimal`/`parseBool`'s own established convention) — bare name for the "parse from Text" side, namespaced `UUID.toText` for the "to Text" side, mirroring `Decimal.toText`'s own precedent. **New C runtime** (`crates/stdlib/src/uuid.rs`): `certo_parse_uuid` (strict canonical 8-4-4-4-12 form — exactly 36 characters, dashes at exactly the 4 standard positions, every other character a hex digit; returns `NULL`/None on any deviation, unlike the existing dashes-optional/always-panics `certo_uuid_parse` that backs the `uuid"..."` literal) and `certo_uuid_to_text` (the inverse, canonical lowercase dashed form). `UUID.toText`'s auto-derived C name (`c_fn_name`'s `camel_to_snake` inserts an underscore before every uppercase letter, so all 4 capitals in "UUID" each get their own: `certo_u_u_i_d_to_text`) needed a `#define` bridge to the real implementation — the same pattern `DateTime.*`'s own aliases already use for the identical naming mismatch. Registered both in `crates/stdlib/src/seed.rs` (`parseUuid: Text -> UUID?`, `UUID.toText: UUID -> Text`) — both are plain, non-generic `Ty::Fn`s, so HIR's `stdlib_ret_types()`/`Ty::Uuid.needs_heap_box()` (already `true`, alongside `Decimal`, since item 201) picked them up automatically with zero codegen/HIR/MIR changes needed. **A second, real bug found and fixed while finishing this**: `certo_to_text_expr`/`text_to_certo_expr`/`null_default` (`crates/cli/src/main.rs`, `cmd_db_pull`'s own Insert/FromRow-generation helpers) all had a `UUID` gap of their own — their catch-alls' own comments explicitly (and wrongly) bundled `UUID` in with `Text` as needing no conversion; fixed by adding real `UUID` arms to all three (nil-UUID `uuid"00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000"` as the typed-zero default, matching `Decimal.fromInt(0)`/`0`'s own "dead branch" convention). **Manually applied the equivalent fixes directly to `examples/db/schema.cto`'s own text** (no live connection to the original "fireworks" database exists in this environment to regenerate it fresh via a real `certo db pull` run) — wrapped all 12 non-nullable and nullable UUID `*FromRow` field reads in `parseUuid(...) ?? <nil-uuid>`, and all 3 UUID `Insert`-param writes in `UUID.toText(...)`. 5 new `crates/stdlib` tests (`parseUuid`/`UUID.toText` registration and types; both C function bodies present, not just forward-declared, mirroring item 197's own precedent; the strict length/dash-position validation confirmed present in the C source directly). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: a standalone repro round-trips a real UUID string through `parseUuid`/`UUID.toText` correctly, correctly rejects a malformed string and a dashless 32-hex-digit string, and confirms equality with a `uuid"..."`-literal-parsed value; `examples/db/schema.cto` went from 43 errors down to a clean `certo check` pass, and its full C codegen (`--emit-c`) compiles cleanly via the real `clang` toolchain (the only remaining failure is a *link*-stage "undefined symbol" for `certo_db_query_typed`/`certo_db_exec`/`certo_db_null`, expected and unrelated — no live libpq is linked in this environment, and the file has no `main` to link into a real executable at all). | `crates/stdlib`, `crates/cli` |
| 229 | `examples/db/schema.cto`'s Postgres `oid` type mapping gap, plus a real, distinct `cmd_db_pull` codegen bug found finishing it — the file's remaining errors beyond item 228's UUID gap | Investigated before implementing: the item's own "not-yet-characterized DateTime/Text mismatches" fully characterize down to two clean, distinct root causes, not several. **(1) The filed `Oid` gap**: `pg_type_to_certo` (`crates/cli/src/main.rs`) had no mapping for Postgres's internal `oid` type (a 32-bit unsigned object identifier, used by `history_.table_oid_`) — fell through the same buggy `other => snake_to_pascal(other)` catch-all item 205 already partly fixed for `time with time zone`/`Character`, producing the undeclared, unparseable field type `Oid`. Fixed by adding `"oid" => "Int".to_string()` (matches its real 32-bit-integer semantics; Certo has no unsigned type to distinguish it with) — same treatment applied directly to `examples/db/schema.cto`'s own `tableOid: Oid` declaration and its `FromRow`/`Insert` conversions (`parseInt(...) ?? 0` / `intToText(...)`). **(2) A new, previously-unknown bug, found and confirmed via user check-in before fixing**: `cmd_db_pull`'s own `FindById`/`DeleteById` generator collapses a non-`Int` primary key's *declared* Certo parameter type to bare `Text` (`pk_certo = if pk_ty == "Int" {"Int"} else {"Text"}`), but the *conversion expression* applied to that same parameter was computed from the original, pre-collapse `pk_ty` (e.g. `"DateTime"`) — wrapping an already-`Text`-typed `id` in a conversion function that expects the *original* type (`DateTime.toIso(id)`), which fails to typecheck since `id: Text` was declared one line above. Confirmed via the real `log` table's `log_timestamp` (DateTime) primary key in `examples/db/schema.cto` — narrow blast radius in this file (only 1 table) but a general bug for any DateTime/Decimal/UUID-keyed table anywhere; the bug had gone unnoticed for UUID-keyed PKs specifically only by coincidence (both the old buggy `pk_to_text` call *and* the old buggy `certo_to_text_expr`'s UUID-as-identity catch-all canceled each other out for that one type, until item 228's own fix added a real `UUID` arm to `certo_to_text_expr`, which would have newly broken every UUID-keyed `FindById`/`DeleteById` had this fix not landed alongside it). Fixed by converting from `id`'s own *declared* type (`pk_certo`) instead of the pre-collapse `pk_ty` — a one-line change (`certo_to_text_expr("id", pk_certo)`), verified correct across every case (Int/Text/DateTime/Decimal/UUID/BoundedText primary keys) by hand-tracing `pk_certo`'s collapse against `certo_to_text_expr`'s own match arms. Applied the equivalent fix directly to `examples/db/schema.cto`'s own `log` table (`DateTime.toIso(id)` → plain `id` in both `logFindById`/`logDeleteById`). 1 new `crates/cli` test (`pg_type_to_certo_maps_oid_to_int`). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end**: combined with item 228's fixes, `examples/db/schema.cto` (all 43 original errors, across both items) now passes `certo check` cleanly and compiles cleanly through the real C codegen/`clang` pipeline (link-stage-only failure, see item 228's own note). | `crates/cli` |
| 215 | An unsupported UI `layout` primitive silently vanished with zero trace in the default Htmx codegen target | Confirmed exactly as filed: `crates/ui/src/server.rs`'s static-view handler filtered out every line starting with `<!--` before splicing `layout_to_html`'s output in, discarding the diagnostic HTML comment `layout_to_html` itself emits for any unrecognized primitive — the legacy `--html` mode already kept these comments; only the default Htmx path stripped them. No historical rationale found for the filter (checked `git log -L` on the block — present unexplained since the initial upload). | 221 | `validator ... trigger on ...`'s SQL was built internally but silently discarded — never reached `certo db migrate` | Confirmed exactly as filed: `emit_validator()` builds real, already-idempotent trigger SQL (`CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` + `DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS` + `CREATE TRIGGER`), but `ValidatorOutput::to_source()` never included it and nothing else in the codebase ever read the `trigger_sql` field at all. Real fix required more than wiring an existing value through: `certo db migrate` takes **no source file whatsoever** — it only reads `.cto` files from a fixed `migrations/` directory, tracked as one-time-applied state — so it had no way to discover `validator` declarations, which can live anywhere in a project's real source tree. Presented two scoping questions to the user before implementing (confirmed): (1) source discovery via `certo.toml`'s `[build] entry` — the same file `certo build` already treats as the project's entry point — plus its local imports, mirroring `cmd_build`'s own project-root/import-resolution logic exactly, rather than a new whole-project directory scan; (2) trigger installation is **idempotent on every run**, not tracked as one-time-applied state like regular migrations (matching `docs/section-16-validators.md` §16.7's own "installs itself... when you run `certo db migrate`" phrasing, and matching the SQL's own already-idempotent shape). **Design**: new `collect_validator_trigger_sql` (`crates/cli/src/main.rs`) reads `[build] entry`, parses it plus local imports, builds the module's `constraint_bodies` (mirroring `expand_validators`'s own setup), and calls `emit_validator` on every `Decl::Validator` with a `trigger` block, collecting each one's `trigger_sql`. Degrades to an empty result (not a hard error) when there's no `certo.toml`/`[build] entry`, or when the entry file fails to read/parse — `certo db migrate`'s core job (applying pending file-based migrations) must never be blocked by an unrelated problem in the main source file. **A real, non-obvious control-flow bug found and fixed while wiring this in**: `cmd_migrate`'s "up" arm previously **exited early** (`if steps.is_empty() { println!("Nothing to migrate."); return; }`) before ever reaching trigger logic — since triggers are idempotent/always-reapplied rather than tracked state, this early return would have meant a trigger only ever installed on a project's *first* `certo db migrate` run (whichever run happened to have a pending file migration) and silently never synced again after any later edit to the validator's own source. Restructured so the early-return only fires when *both* regular migrations and trigger SQL are empty, and file-based migrations/triggers are now applied (and reported) independently of each other. 6 new `crates/cli` tests (`trigger_sql_tests` module — no certo.toml/no `[build] entry`/no validators all correctly return nothing; a validator *without* a `trigger` block contributes no SQL; a validator *with* one contributes real, correctly-shaped SQL; a validator declared in a locally-imported file — not the entry file itself — is still found). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end against a live local Postgres instance**: a real trigger-bearing validator's `certo db migrate` run actually created the `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION`/`CREATE TRIGGER` in the database (confirmed via `pg_trigger`); a second `certo db migrate` run (with no pending file migrations at all) still correctly re-installed the trigger rather than short-circuiting to "Nothing to migrate" (the exact control-flow bug above, confirmed fixed); `--dry-run` printed the SQL without touching the database (confirmed via `pg_trigger` showing nothing after a dropped trigger + dry-run); a project with no `certo.toml` and only a `migrations/` directory confirmed completely unaffected (still just "Nothing to migrate", no regression). | `crates/cli` |
| 227 | A bare `Ok(...)`/`Err(...)` constructed directly as an unannotated inline lambda's own body segfaulted the moment the enclosing call's result was consumed — fixed through four compounding layers, each confirmed with the user before proceeding as the true scope kept escalating | **Layer 1, confirmed real via a direct HIR probe (not just the filed repro)**: `Ok(x + 1)`'s own call type was plain `Ty::Error` *in its entirety* — not "a `Result` with one bad slot" as the item's own filed text first described — because `Ok`/`Err` are `Forall`-wrapped stdlib constructors whose *other* type parameter is never constrained by their own single argument, and nothing anywhere in HIR's call-type recovery (`stdlib_ret_types`, `generic_container_ret`, the local-fn-value fallback, `recover_generic_variant_call_ty`) had any special case for them at all — unlike every other gap this session's own "known type thrown away" bug class (items 179/180/182/189/191/199/211) already covered. **Fix 1**: new `Some("Ok")`/`Some("Err")` arms in `generic_container_ret` (`crates/hir/src/lower.rs`) recover the one half knowable from the constructor's own argument, leaving the unconstrained half `Ty::Error` — mirrors `recover_generic_variant_call_ty`/`recover_generic_record_ty`'s own established "recover what's known, `Error` for the rest" convention for user-defined generics, confirmed with the user as the right precedent to follow before writing it. **Fix 2**: split `flatMap`/`Result.flatMap` out of the arm it previously shared with `recover`/`Result.recover` (whose own callback returns a raw `T`, never a `Result`, so never hits this gap) and backfilled its error-type slot directly from the receiver's own already-known error type — not a guess, a correct derivation, since `flatMap`'s real signature (`Result<T,E> -> (T -> Result<U,E>) -> Result<U,E>`, `crates/stdlib/src/seed.rs`) guarantees `E` never changes. Both fixes confirmed end-to-end (`certo build`+run, not just typecheck) against the exact filed repro (dot-call and bare-call forms) and a named-function-callback regression guard. **Layer 3, found testing more thoroughly than the filed repro required and confirmed with the user before fixing**: a *conditional* callback body (`(x) => if x > 3 then Err("too big") else Ok(x)` — an equally natural, arguably more common shape than an unconditional tail call) still segfaulted on the `Ok` branch. Root cause: `Expr::If`/`Expr::Match`'s own branch-type merging only ever checked whether a branch's type was *literally* the top-level `Ty::Error`, never looking inside a `Ty::Result` for an `Error` nested in one slot — so `Err(...)`'s recovered `Result<Error,Text>` was accepted wholesale the instant it wasn't itself bare `Ty::Error`, and the `else` branch's `Ok(x)` was never even consulted. **Fix 3**: new `merge_partial(a, b)` helper, used by both `Expr::If`'s then/else combination and `Expr::Match`'s multi-arm fold, recursively merging same-shape `Result`/`Option` containers slot-by-slot instead of picking one branch's type wholesale. **Layer 4, a second, independent root cause found immediately after fix 3 alone still didn't close the conditional case, confirmed with the user before adding a genuinely new mechanism**: `flatMap`/`recover`/`mapErr`'s own callback *parameter* (`x`) got zero type hint at all in HIR, unlike `List.map`/`sortBy`/etc., whose callback param is already hinted from the list's own element type (items 113/162b/170) — a bare, unused `x` had nothing to structurally recover a type from at all, only ever "accidentally" resolving via an unrelated mechanism (e.g. arithmetic like `x + 1` reconstructing `Int` on its own). **Fix 4**: extended the *existing* `needs_lambda_hint`/param-hint mechanism (`crates/hir/src/lower.rs`) to also cover `flatMap`/`recover`/`mapErr`, sourcing the hint from the receiver's own `Result<A,B>` (the success side `A` for `flatMap`, the error side `B` for `recover`/`mapErr`) the same way list functions source theirs from a list's element type — plus a real, separate, pre-existing bug this uncovered along the way: `stdlib_param_names()` (a hand-maintained table entirely distinct from `crates/stdlib/src/seed.rs`'s own `pm!`-registered param names, used only by typeck) never had *any* entry for `flatMap`/`mapErr`/`recover` in either their bare or `Result.`-qualified spellings, so the whole `needs_lambda_hint` mechanism was silently unreachable for them regardless of spelling — added all six entries (bare + qualified × 3 functions). Also added the matching qualified `pm!` entries in `seed.rs` for consistency with the bare ones already there (harmless, not the actual fix, but a real, separate small gap in the same neighborhood). 8 new `crates/hir` tests (bare `Ok`/`Err` return-type recovery in isolation; the exact filed `flatMap`-with-bare-`Ok`-lambda and its `Err` mirror; the conditional if/else shape; a named-function-callback regression guard; and one documenting the genuinely-unrecoverable case — a callback whose tail is *unconditionally* `Err(...)` with its own param never referenced at all — as an honest, intentional structural-recovery limit, not a silently-wrong result). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`** across 7 real programs: the filed repro's dot-call and bare-call forms; the `Err`-branch (receiver already erred, short-circuiting); the conditional `if`/`else` shape in *both* directions (the `Err` branch, which happened to already work by coincidence before any of today's fixes, and the `Ok` branch, which segfaulted before fix 3+4 and now correctly prints); the pre-existing named-function-callback path (regression guard); and `mapErr`/`recover` (which needed no `Ok`/`Err`-recovery fix themselves but share the newly-touched param-hint code path) — all produce correct output, and `examples/db/schema.cto` (unrelated, no `flatMap` usage) confirmed to still typecheck cleanly, ruling out a general regression. | `crates/hir`, `crates/stdlib` |
| 224 | `V.validate`/`V.validateAll`'s call-site signature always had 1 parameter (`entity` only), even with a `context` block — plus a second, distinct dot-call name-mismatch bug found investigating the same question | **Bug 1 (context signature)**: confirmed exactly as filed — `hoist_decl`'s `Decl::Validator` arm (`crates/typeck/src/infer_decl.rs`) always registered `Ty::Fn { params: vec![entity_ty], .. }`, rejecting the real, spec-shaped 2-argument call a context-bearing validator's actual generated function needs. Resolved the filed design question with the user: the context parameter is typed *nominally* (`Ty::Named("{Name}Context")`, matching the real synthesized type's own name from `emit_validator.rs`'s `ctx_type = format!("{}Context", vname)`) rather than an anonymous structural `Ty::Record`. Confirmed the `certo check`-vs-`build` divergence this creates is safe, not new: under `check` alone (which never runs `expand_validators`, so the type name is never actually declared), it degrades to this codebase's own pre-existing "an undeclared capitalized name is an accepted opaque nominal type" leniency — already established elsewhere, and strictly better than before (a context-bearing call was previously rejected at *any* arity, not just imprecisely typed). **Bug 2 (dot/underscore name mismatch, a different, more precise mechanism than the item's own filed guess)**: reproduced directly — `V.validate(entity).isOk()` (UFCS dot-call) failed to compile even for the simplest context-free validator, while `match V.validate(entity) { Ok(_) => ..., ... }` on the identical value worked. Root-caused precisely: the real generated function is named with an underscore (`V_validate`, so the generated Certo source parses as an ordinary function — `emit_validator.rs`'s own `build_fn_sig`), but a dot-qualified call site (`V.validate(...)`) lowers to the *dotted* global name (`"V.validate"`, via `Expr::Field`'s own `Module.fn` handling in `crates/hir/src/lower.rs`). `c_fn_name` (`crates/codegen/src/emit_mir.rs`) already normalizes both spellings to the identical C symbol, so the call itself always linked and ran correctly — only HIR's own `cx.fn_ret_types` lookup (a literal-string `HashMap` with no dot/underscore normalization) silently missed, falling all the way through to `Ty::Error`. Not, as the item's own filed text guessed, a missing `Decl::Validator` walk in `lower_module`'s pre-registration — the real generated `Decl::Fn` *is* already registered correctly, just under a different key than the call site looks up. Fixed with a small, targeted fallback: when the direct dot-form lookup misses, also try the underscore-normalized form before falling through further. 6 new tests (`crates/typeck`: context-bearing `validate`/`validateAll` accept `(entity, ctx)`; the single-arg form is correctly still rejected; a context-free validator's signature is unaffected. `crates/hir`: a dot-qualified call to an underscore-named real function resolves its real return type; a genuine dotted-name `impl` method call is unaffected by the new fallback). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: the exact filed `isOk()` UFCS repro now compiles and runs correctly; a real context-bearing validator's 2-argument `.validate(entity, ctx)`/`.validateAll(entity, ctx)` calls both compile, run, and report correct results (single-error and two-error cases); `certo check` alone (no expansion) accepts the same context-bearing call site cleanly, confirming the nominal-type leniency degrades safely. | `crates/typeck`, `crates/hir` |
| 225 | `E0704` (constraint field not in scope) — the bounded half of this item; `W0100`/`W0101`/`W0102` split out as their own item (230) once investigation confirmed they need an entire new warning-diagnostic subsystem first | Confirmed the scoping split from the item's own filed text was correct: E0704 needed real, new, but *bounded* deferred-resolution logic; W0100-102 needed foundational infrastructure this codebase has zero of today (see item 230). **Design**: new `check_constraint_scope` pass (`crates/typeck/src/infer_decl.rs`, called from `check_module_seeded` as a fifth module-wide pass, mirroring `check_ffi_unsafe`/`check_priv_ctors`'s own established pattern) — for every `Decl::Validator`, builds its real scope (the lowercased entity type name, plus every declared `context` field name), then for each rule's `require`/`else` expression, finds every bare name matching a declared `constraint`, and recursively checks *that constraint's own body* for field-access base names outside the validator's scope — reported at the `require`/`else` site referencing it, not the constraint's own declaration, matching the spec's own exact E0704 example. Runs as a *direct* check against the un-expanded `Decl::Validator`/`Decl::Constraint` AST, so (unlike item 220's own inlined-constraint checking) it works under `certo check` alone, with no dependency on `expand_validators` ever running. New `walk_expr` helper (exhaustive over every `Expr` variant, mirroring `walk_ffi`'s own established style in the same file) powers both the constraint-reference scan and the field-access scan. Deliberately scoped to *direct* constraint references only — a constraint referencing another constraint isn't transitively expanded, since no example in `docs/section-16-validators.md` demonstrates that shape and guessing at the right resolution order wasn't worth it. New `TypeErrorKind::ConstraintFieldNotInScope` variant plus a real `docs/ERROR-REFERENCE.md` entry (`--explain E0704` now works — confirmed E0703/705/706/707 are all still missing their own entries there, a pre-existing gap predating this item, not fixed here). 4 new `crates/typeck` tests (the spec's own exact repro shape; the same constraint once the field is properly declared; a constraint referencing the entity's own variable directly, not just a context field; a plain field-access rule with zero named constraints, confirming the whole pass is a safe no-op when a module has none). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`, under `certo check` alone (no `build`/expansion at all)**: the spec's own `UserIsAdmin = user.role == Admin` example correctly reports E0704 when `user` isn't declared in context, and correctly compiles clean once it is; a constraint referencing the entity variable itself (not a context field) confirmed not to false-positive. | `crates/typeck`, `crates/cli`, `docs` |
| 233 | The local-import file resolver has no kebab-case conversion at all — spec §2.1's own literal example didn't match the real convention — resolved as a **doc-only fix**, matching the item's own second option | Fixed the doc rather than the compiler: `crates/cli/src/main.rs`'s real, working convention (each module-path segment matches a literal, case-sensitive directory/file segment, PascalCase, no conversion at all) was already confirmed correct and load-bearing by the audit — building kebab-case conversion into the resolver would be new, real parser/CLI work for a naming convention nothing else in the codebase uses. Updated `docs/Certo_Language_Specification.md` §2.1's own prose (now states the file path mirrors the module path's segments exactly, PascalCase, no case conversion) and its own worked example (`src/orders/processing.cto` → `src/Orders/Processing.cto`, matching `module Orders.Processing`), plus §2.2's naming-conventions table row (`File names: kebab-case` → `File names: matches the module path segment exactly`). The item's own noted secondary issue (a missing-module import fails with a confusing, unrelated `undefined name` error rather than a clear "module not found" diagnostic) is a real, separate, still-open small gap — not fixed here, since it's a compiler UX improvement, not a doc mismatch. | `docs` |
| 236 | Spec §7.4's own `withAudit` example didn't compile — unbound generic type parameters `T`/`E` — **doc-only fix** | Added `<T, E>` to the example's own `async fn withAudit<T, E>(action: Text, f: () => Result<T, E>): Result<T, E> = {...}` signature in `docs/Certo_Language_Specification.md` §7.4, matching items 205/206/217's own precedent for a doc-only example bug. **Verified via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: the corrected signature (adapted into a minimal, real, standalone repro) passes `certo check` cleanly. **A separate, new, real bug found while verifying end-to-end** (not fixed here, not part of this doc-only item): the corrected example, once actually built and run (a generic `async fn` returning `Result<T,E>`, called via `await`), segfaults at runtime — confirmed the segfault is specific to the *generic* case (an otherwise-identical non-generic `async fn withAudit(action: Text, f: () => Result<Int, Text>): Result<Int, Text>` compiles, runs, and produces correct output) and unrelated to `defer` (still segfaults with `defer` removed). Filed as a new, separate item (see below) rather than expanding this doc-fix's own scope. | `docs` |
| 237 | Spec §7.3's `Channel<Order>(capacity: 100)` syntax doesn't parse (no `Type<Arg>(...)` call syntax exists anywhere in the grammar) and doesn't match the real, working API — **doc-only fix, matching the item's own recommended resolution** | Corrected the example in `docs/Certo_Language_Specification.md` §7.3 to `val channel = Channel.new(100)`, the real, already-working API (type parameter fully inferred from the argument — no explicit type-argument application needed or supported anywhere in this language's generics model). Did not add general `Type<Arg>(...)` call-site syntax to the grammar, per the item's own recommendation — a real, larger parser-disambiguation feature with no confirmed concrete need. **Verified via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: `Channel.new(100)` compiles and runs correctly (a standalone repro constructing and printing confirmation of the channel). | `docs` |
| 241 | Spec §9.4's own DateTime/Timestamp bare-operator arithmetic examples (`date + duration`, `timestamp - timestamp`) contradicted the compiler since item 214 added `E0218` to reject them — **doc-only fix** | Swapped the two example rows in `docs/Certo_Language_Specification.md` §9.4 for their real, working equivalents: `date.addDuration(duration)` and `timestamp.diff(timestamp)` (both confirmed as real, already-registered `crates/stdlib` functions). **Verified via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: a standalone repro using both corrected forms (`Date.today().addDuration(Duration.days(5))`, `Timestamp.now().diff(Timestamp.now())`) compiles and runs correctly, producing a real (near-zero) duration. **A separate, small, new doc mismatch found immediately adjacent while verifying** (not fixed here — flagged for a future pass, not worth its own item given its size): the very next table row, `Date.today(tz) | Current date in given timezone`, is also inaccurate — the real `Date.today()` takes zero arguments; the real timezone-aware variant is separately named `Date.todayIn(tz)`. | `docs` |
| 231 | `for x in a..b { ... f"{x}" ... }` segfaults the moment the loop variable is interpolated into an f-string — the loop mechanics themselves are fine, only text-coercion of the variable crashes | Confirmed exactly as filed, and root-caused to **two** compounding sites, not one — the audit's own diagnosis named only the shallower of the two. **Site 1**: `crates/hir/src/lower.rs`'s `1..5`-range desugar left the synthesized `range`/`rangeInclusive` `Call` node's own `ty` hardcoded to `Ty::Error` instead of `Ty::List(Box::new(Ty::Int))` — fixed directly, since both stdlib functions are always `(Int,Int)->List<Int>`, never generic (`crates/stdlib/src/seed.rs`), bypassing the general `Expr::App` call-type-recovery chain entirely since this is a dedicated desugar, not a real call expression. **Site 2, the deeper of the two, found tracing further than the audit's own diagnosis**: `Expr::For`'s own lowering *did* separately compute the loop variable's element type from the (now-fixed) iterator type, but only ever wrote it into the `HirExprKind::For` node's own `binding_ty` field — used solely by MIR's own fallback recovery — and never into `cx.local_types`, the `HashMap` that `Expr::Path`'s own lowering reads *directly* for every in-body reference to a local (`cx.local_types.get(&local).cloned().unwrap_or(Ty::Error)`). Every in-body reference to the loop variable (`f"{i}"`, `i + 1`, anything) was silently getting `Ty::Error` regardless of site 1's fix, since `cx.local_types.insert(local, elem_ty)` was never called before lowering the loop body. Fixed by inserting into `cx.local_types` *before* `lower_expr(body, cx)` runs. 3 new `crates/hir` tests: `range_call_resolves_to_list_int_not_error` (site 1 in isolation), `for_loop_binding_ty_resolves_to_int_not_error` (the `HirExprKind::For` node's own field), `for_loop_variable_reference_inside_the_body_resolves_to_int` (an in-body `val n = i + 1`'s own type — the actual site 2 regression guard, since site 1 alone doesn't fix this). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: the exact filed repro (`for i in 1..5 { val s = f"{i}"; println(s) }`) now runs correctly instead of segfaulting, printing `1` through `5`; a second loop using the exclusive-range spelling (`1...4`) confirmed independently correct; the non-interpolating sibling case (`total = total + i`) confirmed still correct (no regression). | `crates/hir` |
| 232 | `certo check` never resolves local (multi-file) imports at all — only `certo build` does, so any project with legitimate cross-file imports spuriously fails `certo check` even though the identical source builds and runs fine | Confirmed exactly as filed. Fixed by factoring `cmd_build`'s own inlined import-resolution block into a new shared helper, `resolve_local_imports(module, input, colour, verbose)` (`crates/cli/src/main.rs`), and calling it from both `cmd_build` (replacing the old inlined block) and `cmd_check` (new call, right before `run_typeck_opts`) — `cmd_check` now takes `&mut Module` where it previously took an immutable binding, so the resolved imports' declarations can be merged in before typeck runs. 2 new `crates/cli` tests (`resolve_local_imports_tests` module): a two-file project's imported function is merged in and visible after resolution, not before; a `Stdlib`/`Core`/`Collections`/`Text`/`DateTime`/`Money`-prefixed import is never mistaken for a local file (decl count unchanged). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: the exact filed repro (a `main.cto` importing a sibling `MyModule.cto`) now passes `certo check` cleanly (previously `E0206: undefined name 'greet'`), and `certo build` on the same files continues to work unchanged (no regression to the working path this was factored out of). | `crates/cli` |
| 234 | `import X as Y` (aliasing) and `import X.{ a, b }` (named-import restriction) are both complete no-ops — every local import merges the *entire* target file's declarations into the importing module's flat global namespace, unqualified, regardless of which import syntax was written | Confirmed exactly as filed. **A significant architectural finding surfaced mid-investigation, confirmed with the user before choosing an approach**: `crates/resolve` (the dedicated name-resolution crate, with its own `ImportKind::Named`/`Aliased` handling already fully written in `resolve_module.rs`/`scope.rs`) turns out to be **dead code for the real `certo` CLI binary** — grepped every call site of `certo_resolve::resolve`/`resolve_seeded` and found exactly one consumer, `crates/lsp`; `certo check`/`certo build` never call it at all. The real pipeline's name lookups happen entirely inside `crates/typeck`'s own flat `TypeEnv` (belt-and-suspenders E0206) and, later, `crates/hir`'s own separate `Cx` — meaning `crates/resolve`'s already-correct alias/named-import scope logic was never actually reachable from the compiler a user runs. Presented two architecturally different fixes to the user (thread alias-awareness into both typeck's and HIR's own separate qualified-name lookups, vs. rewrite the AST once at import-merge time) and confirmed the AST-rewrite approach. **Design**: `crates/cli/src/main.rs`'s `resolve_local_imports` now branches on `imp.kind`. `Named(names)` filters the imported file's own top-level declarations (`decl_own_name`, mirroring `resolve_module.rs`'s own `hoist_decls` match exactly) down to only the listed identifiers — everything else is dropped entirely, not just left unreferenced, so an unlisted name genuinely becomes undefined (confirmed: `secretHelper()` now fails `E0206`, previously always succeeded). A per-name alias (`import X.{ a as b }`) renames the matched decl to the new bare name via a new scope-aware AST rewrite pass (`rewrite_expr`/`rewrite_decls_free_refs`, exhaustive over every `Expr`/`Stmt` variant, mirroring `walk_expr`'s established exhaustive-match style from item 225 but mutable and shadowing-aware via a `Vec<HashSet<String>>` scope stack pushed/popped at every real binding site — fn/lambda params, `val`/`var`/`for` bindings, match-arm patterns — matching `crates/hir/src/lower.rs`'s own established push/pop-scope convention) so the renamed decl's own internal self/sibling references inside the imported file are rewritten too, not just its own top-level name. `Aliased(alias)` namespaces every `Decl::Fn` in the imported file under `alias.`, reusing the *existing* `Type.method`-qualified-global convention `impl` methods and `computed` properties already use — so `M.fn(...)` reaches the exact same lookup path a `Type.method(...)` call already does in both `crates/typeck` and `crates/hir`, with **zero new lookup logic needed in either crate**; the same rewrite pass handles the internal cross-reference case (confirmed necessary: `greet()` calling `helper()` internally, both merged and renamed under the alias, only works because the rewrite pass also rewrites `helper()`'s call site inside `greet`'s own body to `M.helper()`). Deliberately scoped to `Decl::Fn` only for `Aliased` (not `val`/`var`/sum-variant qualification) and to `Expr`-bearing decl bodies only for the rewrite pass (`fn`, `val`, `var`, `impl` methods — not `validator`/`constraint`/`trait`/`statemachine` bodies) — matches this item's own filed repro precisely; each is a real, narrower, still-open gap, not attempted here since nothing demonstrates a concrete need. Also confirmed and left as an honest, documented limitation rather than a silent miscompile: a `Named` import of a function whose own body depends on a *sibling* helper not also named in the same import list fails loudly with `E0206` pointing at the missing helper, not a mysterious C-level error. 5 new `crates/cli` tests (`resolve_local_imports_tests`): named-import filtering drops an unlisted decl entirely; a per-name alias renames both the decl and its own internal self-reference; an aliased import namespaces functions and rewrites their internal cross-references; plus the 2 pre-existing regression guards (Whole-import merge, stdlib-import skip) still pass unchanged. Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions, 0 new warnings. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`** across 6 programs: `import MyModule as M` then `M.greet()` (which internally calls `helper()`) now compiles and runs correctly, printing the real value; the same file's *bare* `greet()` now correctly fails `E0206` (previously silently worked, defeating the whole point of aliasing); `import MyModule.{ greet, helper }` compiles and runs correctly; `import MyModule.{ greet, helper }` calling the unlisted `secretHelper()` now correctly fails `E0206` (previously silently worked); `import MyModule.{ helper as h }` then `h()` compiles and runs correctly; `import MyModule.{ greet }` alone (omitting the sibling `helper` `greet` depends on) fails loudly with `E0206: undefined name 'helper'`, confirming the documented transitive-dependency limitation surfaces as a clear error rather than a confusing downstream failure. | `crates/cli` |
| 238 | A `computed` record property or an in-body `fn` method containing an inline lambda passed to *any* stdlib higher-order function (`sumBy`/`map`/`sortBy`/`spawn`/etc.) generates invalid C — blocks spec §8.5's own flagship aggregate-root pattern (`computed subtotal: Money = items.sumBy(.lineTotal)`) entirely | Confirmed exactly as filed, root cause exactly as diagnosed: every `impl`/in-body method and `computed` property gets a qualified HIR name `"{TypeName}.{methodName}"` (e.g. `"Cart.total"`), which `crates/mir/src/lower.rs`'s dozen or so synthesized-helper-name sites (`__lam_{fn_name}_{idx}`, `__sum_by_{fn_name}_{idx}`, `wrap_name`/`inner_name`/`outer_name` for `sortBy`/`minBy`/`maxBy`/`spawn`/etc.) all splice in raw via `b.fn_name`, with no sanitization — and since every synthesized name starts with `__`, `crates/codegen/src/emit_mir.rs`'s own `c_fn_name` skips its usual sanitization entirely for them (`if name.starts_with("__") { return name.to_string(); }`), so the literal `.` reaches the C emitter unescaped, producing an illegal identifier (`__lam_Cart.total_0`) and spurious redefinition errors from the truncated-at-the-dot collisions. Fixed at the single point every synthesized name is built from rather than at each of the 12 sites individually: `Builder::new` (`crates/mir/src/lower.rs`) now stores `fn_name.replace('.', "_")` instead of the raw name — confirmed safe by checking every other consumer of `b.fn_name`/`f.name` in the file first: the *top-level* function's own emitted name (`MirFn { name: f.name.clone(), .. }`, used later by `c_fn_name`'s own real sanitization pipeline) reads the original `f.name`, not `b.fn_name`, so is completely unaffected; `b.fn_name` itself is read *only* by the 12 synthesis sites, confirmed via grep before making the change. 2 new `crates/mir` tests (`impl_method_qualified_name_dot_is_sanitized_in_lifted_lambda_helper_name`, `computed_property_qualified_name_dot_is_sanitized_in_lifted_lambda_helper_name`) — both lower a dot-qualified method/property whose body passes a lambda to `sumBy`, and assert every lifted helper's own name contains no raw `.`. Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`** with two separate repros: a `computed total` property on `Cart` using `.sumBy(fn(i) = ...)` (previously failed the C-compile stage with `expected ';' after top level declarator`; now compiles and runs, printing the correct sum) and an `impl Cart { fn total(self) = self.items.sumBy(...) }` in-body method using the identical pattern plus a second method using `.map(...)` (both previously failed identically; now compile and run, producing correct output). | `crates/mir` |
| 235 | Fixed-width numeric types (`Int8`/`Int16`/`Int32`/`UInt`/`Float32`) are fully declared in the type system and codegen per spec §4.1's own primitive-types table, but are completely unconstructable dead types — no integer/float literal can ever infer as one, and no conversion function exists to produce one from `Int`/`Float` either | Confirmed exactly as filed, but **the original filed text's own framing turned out to be wrong in a way that mattered, corrected with the user before implementing**: the item guessed this would "mirror `Decimal`/`BoundedText`'s own bidirectional-unify treatment" — investigation found no such precedent exists anywhere. `Decimal`/`BoundedText`'s apparent flexibility is purely a *post-inference* unify-time rule between two forms of the *same* already-produced type (`Ty::Decimal(None)` unifies with any `Ty::Decimal(Some(p,s))`); it has nothing to do with a *literal* being retyped by its surrounding context. This typechecker's `infer()` is pure bottom-up with no `check()`/bidirectional counterpart anywhere, confirmed by grep — a real general fix would be a typeck-engine-level feature, not the "moderate, well-precedented" work originally described. Presented this finding and three concrete scope options to the user, who confirmed the narrow option: a syntax-level special case at exactly the annotated positions where an expected type is already available today (`val`/`var` declarations, function/impl-method return position), not a general bidirectional-checking engine. **Design**: new `literal_matches_fixed_width(expr, ty)` (independently implemented once in `crates/typeck/src/infer_expr.rs` and once in `crates/hir/src/lower.rs` — kept as two separate syntax-level checks rather than cross-crate-coupled, since MIR derives a function's *real* C return type from its body's own computed operand type, not `HirFn.ret_ty`, so HIR's own copy of `lower_lit`'s rigid `Ty::Int`/`Ty::Float` default needed independent correcting or the C codegen would still be wrong even once typeck stopped rejecting the program) recognizes a bare int/float literal — including a negated int literal (`-5000`, parsed as `UnOp::Neg` wrapping the literal; `UInt` deliberately excluded from the negated case, since it has no negative values) — against one of the 5 fixed-width types, and both crates' own annotated-position call sites (`Stmt::Val`/`Stmt::Var`/`Decl::Val`/`Decl::Var`/`Decl::Fn`'s and `Decl::Impl`'s own return-position unify calls in typeck; the matching `Stmt::Val`/`Stmt::Var`/`Decl::Fn`/`Decl::Impl` lowering sites in HIR) use it to substitute the declared type directly instead of unifying/keeping the rigid default. A non-literal `Int`-typed expression (`val x = 100; val a: Int8 = x`) is deliberately still rejected, not silently truncated — confirmed by a dedicated regression test. **Also completed the item's own second, previously entirely-missing half**: added `intToInt8`/`int8ToInt`/`int8ToText` and the equivalent triples for `Int16`/`Int32`/`UInt` to `crates/stdlib/src/seed.rs` (mirroring the *already-existing* `Float32` conversion set exactly — confirmed via grep that `Float32` alone had gotten this treatment previously, unrelated to this item, and every other fixed-width type had none) plus their real C implementations in `crates/stdlib/src/core.rs` (`certo_int8_to_text`/etc., mirroring the existing Float32 block's own style). Named `intToUint`, not `intToUInt` — `c_fn_name`'s `camel_to_snake` (`crates/codegen/src/emit_mir.rs`) inserts an underscore before *every* uppercase letter, so a mid-identifier `UInt` (two capitals) would mangle to `_u_int` instead of `_uint`; caught by manually tracing the mangling before picking the name, not by a failed build. Also fixed the item's own noted related symptom: added all 5 fixed-width types to `is_displayable` (`crates/typeck/src/infer_expr.rs`, gating `E0211`) and to `coerce_to_text`'s own match (`crates/codegen/src/emit_mir.rs`), so `f"{x}"` now works for every one of them, each correctly routed to its own new `certo_<ty>_to_text`. 9 new `crates/typeck` tests, 5 new `crates/hir` tests (checking the lowered node's own `.ty` field directly, not just that typeck accepts the program — the two crates can disagree), 1 new `crates/stdlib` test (mirroring the existing `float32_conversion_functions_registered` test's own shape for the 4 new types' dozen conversion functions). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: `val`/`var` declarations across all 5 types with real literal values, `f"{x}"` interpolation for each, round-trip conversion (`intToInt8`/`int8ToInt`), function-return-position literals (including negative `Int32`), and both negative-case rejections (a float literal against `Int8`, a negative literal against `UInt`) all behave correctly. **A genuinely separate, real, high-impact bug found by accident while testing this item's own negative-literal case** (`val y: Float = -3.5` unexpectedly failed to typecheck) — confirmed via `git stash` to predate this session entirely, not a regression from this work; filed separately as item 248 rather than folded into this diff. | `crates/typeck`, `crates/hir`, `crates/stdlib`, `crates/codegen` |

| 248 | `-3.5` (negating *any* `Float` literal) has never typechecked, anywhere, ever — a genuinely surprising, high-impact gap found by accident while verifying item 235's own fixed-width int negation fix; confirmed with `git stash` that it predates this session entirely | Confirmed exactly as filed. **Design**: `crates/typeck/src/infer_expr.rs`'s `UnOp::Neg` arm no longer unconditionally unifies its operand against `Ty::Int` — a new `is_numeric_ty` helper accepts any already-resolved *scalar* numeric type (`Int`/`Int8`/`Int16`/`Int32`/`UInt`/`Float`/`Float32`) directly, preserving it; a still-unresolved type var (an unannotated negative literal) still falls through to the original Int-unify (preserving the existing default), and a genuinely non-numeric type (Text, …) still gets the original, real "expected Int" diagnostic. **Deliberately excludes `Decimal`**, found mid-implementation: negating a `Decimal` typechecks fine syntactically but its C representation is a *struct* (`certo_decimal_t`) — a raw C unary `-` on it doesn't compile, and (confirmed separately, more surprisingly) neither does binary `+`/`-` on two Decimals at all — accepting it here would trade a clean typeck rejection for a confusing raw C compile error; filed the Decimal-arithmetic gap itself as new item 249, out of scope here. `crates/hir/src/lower.rs`'s own `Expr::UnOp` lowering had a matching, independent gap — it unconditionally hardcoded `ty: Ty::Error` on *every* negation (masked for `-Int` only because `Ty::Error`'s C fallback, `int64_t`, happens to coincide with `Int`'s) — now correctly propagates the operand's own real type for `Neg`, and `Ty::Bool` (previously also `Ty::Error`) for `Not`, confirmed safe since nothing downstream keyed on `Not`'s type being `Error` (its own destination local's C type is derived directly from this field). 7 new `crates/typeck` tests (Float/fixed-width negation, Decimal/Text negation still rejected, unannotated-literal default preserved) and 2 new `crates/hir` tests (checking the lowered node's own `.ty` field directly for both `Neg` and `Not`, not just that typeck accepts the program). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: `-3.5` (annotated and bare), negating each fixed-width type, and `not b` all compile and produce correct output; negating a `Decimal` or `Text` still cleanly rejected at typecheck with a real diagnostic, not a C-stage failure. | `crates/typeck`, `crates/hir` |
| 240 | `Regex.replace` replaces *every* match in the input, not just the first — directly contradicts its own spec table row (`Replace first match with replacement`) | Confirmed exactly as filed. Rewrote `crates/stdlib/src/regex.rs`'s `certo_regex_replace` (the embedded C runtime) from a `while (*s)` loop that kept re-matching and substituting after every replacement, to a single `_re_find` call plus one prefix/replacement/suffix copy — matching its own name and spec row precisely, no loop at all needed for "first match only." No Rust-level unit test added (this codebase has no existing test harness for its embedded-C-string stdlib functions specifically — every prior fix in this exact file was likewise verified only via a real compiled program, matching this file's own established convention) — verified instead via direct compiled-program checks, matching the file's own testing precedent. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: `Regex.replace("[0-9]", "a1 b2 c3", "X")` now correctly prints `aX b2 c3` (previously `aX bX cX`); a no-match input is returned unchanged; a zero-width match at the string start (`^`) is also handled correctly; sibling functions in the same file (`Regex.match`, `Regex.split`) confirmed unaffected. | `crates/stdlib` |
| 244 | A trigger `when` condition whose value is a runtime reference (e.g. spec §16.7's own literal example, `when status != OLD.status`) is wrongly emitted as a quoted SQL *string literal* instead of a real column reference — the gate compares against the literal text `'OLD.status'`, which almost never matches, so it fires far more often than the spec's documented "on any status change" behavior | Confirmed exactly as filed, root cause exactly as diagnosed. Fixed `crates/codegen/src/emit_validator.rs`'s trigger `when`-clause codegen to branch on the condition value's own AST shape: a bare single-segment `Expr::Path` (an enum-variant literal, e.g. `Submitted`) still gets quoted as a real SQL string; anything else (`OLD.status`, parsed by `parse_trigger_value` as `Expr::Field { Path(["OLD"]), "status" }`) is emitted unquoted — `fmt_expr`'s own rendering of that shape (`"OLD.status"`) is already valid PL/pgSQL syntax verbatim, so no translation was needed, only *not quoting it*. 2 new `crates/codegen` tests (a new `gen_trigger_sql` helper mirroring the existing `gen_validator`): `OLD.status` in a `when` condition is emitted unquoted; a bare enum variant is still quoted. Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via `certo db migrate --dry-run`** against the spec's own exact example (`trigger on Update when status != OLD.status`): the generated SQL now reads `IF NEW.status = OLD.status THEN RETURN NEW; END IF;` (previously `'OLD.status'`, a 9-byte string literal that could never match); the enum-variant case (`when status == Submitted`) confirmed still correctly quoted (`'Submitted'`). | `crates/codegen` |
| 246 | `.age` on an `Option<Timestamp>` (`Timestamp?`) produces silently wrong runtime values instead of the spec's own documented `Duration.max`-on-`None` behavior — no crash, no error, just garbage numbers | Confirmed exactly as filed, root cause exactly as diagnosed. Fixed HIR's `Expr::Age` lowering (`crates/hir/src/lower.rs`) to check `base.ty`: for `Ty::Option(inner)`, synthesizes a real `Match` (mirroring `Expr::SafeField`'s own established `Some`/`None`-match-desugar pattern just above it in the same file) — `Some(x)` unwraps to the payload local and calls the original `DateTime.diff(DateTime.now(), x)`; `None` produces `Duration.max` directly as `HirExprKind::Int(i64::MAX)` typed `Duration` (`Duration`'s own C representation, `CertoDuration`, is a plain `int64_t` millisecond count — `crates/stdlib/src/datetime.rs` — so the largest representable span is exactly `i64::MAX`, no new stdlib constant needed). A non-optional `Timestamp`'s `.age` is unaffected, still the original straight-line desugar. **A deeper, separate, pre-existing bug found and isolated while verifying this fix, not fixed here**: a bare `val x: Timestamp? = None`/`Some(...)` literal never resolves its own declared local type at all (`Ty::Error`) — `.age` on a *function parameter* typed `Timestamp?` (the item's own actual filed repro shape) works correctly with this fix, but a `val`-bound bare `Some`/`None` doesn't reach it due to that separate upstream gap; filed as new item 250. 2 new `crates/hir` tests: the Match desugar's own shape (2 arms, `Some` unwraps to a real `DateTime.diff` call against the *unwrapped* local not the still-optional value, `None` produces `Duration.max`); the non-optional path is unaffected. | 242 | `extern "C"` FFI declarations can never actually link against a real external C library — every call site and forward declaration is unconditionally name-mangled with the same `certo_` prefix and snake-casing every ordinary Certo function gets, and no real C library ever exports a symbol under that mangled name. Spec §12.1's own only worked example (`strlen(s.toCString())`) also doesn't typecheck at all — `Text.toCString()` doesn't exist anywhere in `crates/stdlib` | Confirmed exactly as filed, root cause exactly as diagnosed. `c_fn_name` (`crates/codegen/src/emit_mir.rs`) is called pervasively across the file (dozens of call sites, most with no per-call context — a bare `Operand::Global(name)` render has no way to know whether `name` names an extern declaration), so threading an `is_extern` flag through every one of them would have been a large, invasive refactor for what's really a small, narrow fix. Instead added a `thread_local!`-backed extern-name registry (`EXTERN_NAMES`/`set_extern_names`) that `c_fn_name` consults first (mirroring its own existing `__`-prefix early-return) — populated once, before any codegen runs, by `emit_module`'s own top-level entry point (the only place that already walks every `Decl::Fn` and knows `f.is_extern`); codegen is single-threaded and non-reentrant per process, so this is safe and fully self-contained to `crates/codegen`. **Found and fixed a checked-in test that was asserting the bug itself**: `extern_fn_emits_prototype_not_definition` (`crates/codegen/src/tests.rs`) explicitly asserted the *mangled* name (`certo_rust_add`) as expected behavior — exactly why this had never been caught; updated it to assert the real, unmangled name instead. 3 new/updated `crates/codegen` tests: the corrected extern test itself; an ordinary function sharing a similar name to an extern declaration still gets mangled normally (registry doesn't over-apply); two modules compiled sequentially in the same process don't leak extern names into each other (registry is correctly reset per `emit_module` call, guards the thread-local's own safety argument). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**, linking against a genuine, already-existing system C library function (`extern "C" { fn llabs(n: Int): Int }`, called as `unsafe { llabs(-42) }`) — compiles, links, and correctly prints `42`; separately confirmed that declaring the real 32-bit libc `abs` under a 64-bit Certo `Int` signature now correctly fails at the *C-compile* stage with a genuine `conflicting types for 'abs'` error against the real system header, proof the forward declaration now actually reaches the real symbol rather than a fictional `certo_abs`. **`Text.toCString()` investigated, not implemented** — the spec's own example return type (`*Byte`) is a raw pointer type (`TypeExpr::Ptr`) that parses but has no real backing anywhere in `crates/typeck` beyond unwrapping to its own inner type (`*Byte` → bare `Byte`, an undeclared opaque nominal type) — implementing `toCString()` "properly" per the spec's own exact signature is blocked on a real raw-pointer-type feature this compiler doesn't have, not a small stdlib addition as originally guessed; left for a future item if a concrete need surfaces. | `crates/codegen` |
| 245 | The collection-membership `in`/`not in` operator — used throughout spec §16.3, §16.5 (a dedicated "Collection Membership" subsection), and 6+ times across §16.10's own worked example — doesn't exist anywhere in the grammar at all | Confirmed exactly as filed, root cause exactly as diagnosed. **Design**: new `BinOp::In` AST variant (`crates/ast/src/expr.rs`); `crates/parser/src/parse_expr.rs`'s `parse_comparison` recognizes `EXPR in LIST-EXPR` at comparison precedence (same level as `==`/`<`), with two-token lookahead (`cur.peek2()`) for `not`+`in` — `not in` desugars to `UnOp::Not` wrapping the same `BinOp::In` node rather than a parallel `NotIn` variant, reusing `not`'s own already-correct typeck/HIR/codegen handling (freshly verified correct by item 248, immediately above). Typeck (`crates/typeck/src/infer_expr.rs`) unifies the right side against `List<left's type>`, result `Bool`. HIR (`crates/hir/src/lower.rs`) desugars `BinOp::In` directly to `List.contains(list, item)` — note the *reversed* argument order vs. `in`'s own `item in list` source order, matching `List.contains`'s real `(list, item)` signature — mirroring the existing range-operator desugar's own "direct desugar, hardcode the known result type, bypasses `Expr::App`'s call-type-recovery chain" treatment just above it in the same file, since `List.contains` (fixed under item 181) already exists and works with zero changes needed. 3 new `crates/parser` tests (bare `in` parses to `BinOp::In`; `not in` parses to `UnOp::Not` wrapping `BinOp::In`; `in` binds looser than `+`), 4 new `crates/typeck` tests (matching/mismatched element types, `not in`, right side must be a list), 1 new `crates/hir` test (the `List.contains` desugar's own exact shape and reversed argument order). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: both the spec's own bare (`s in [Approved, Picking]`) and `not in` (`order.status not in [...]`, including through a real field access on the left side) forms now compile and run correctly across sum-type and `Int` element types, arithmetic on the left (`1 + 1 in [1,2,3]`) correctly binds at the right precedence, and an element-type mismatch still correctly fails at typecheck with a real diagnostic. | `crates/ast`, `crates/parser`, `crates/typeck`, `crates/hir` |
| 250 | A bare `Some(x)`/`None` literal never resolves its own real type without an *external* recovery mechanism already in place at its use site — a `val`-bound Optional constructed directly from a literal is silently `Ty::Error`, even though the exact same value used as a function argument or parameter works fine | Confirmed exactly as filed, root cause exactly as diagnosed — structurally identical to item 227's own `Ok`/`Err` fix, just never applied to `Option`'s own constructors. **Design**: added a `Some("Some")` arm to `generic_container_ret` (`crates/hir/src/lower.rs`), mirroring the existing `Some("Ok")`/`Some("Err")` arms exactly — recovers `Ty::Option(arg.ty)` directly from `Some(x)`'s own argument, needing no external annotation at all (unlike `Ok`/`Err`, which need one half backfilled from elsewhere, `Some(x)`'s single payload is fully knowable from the argument alone). Separately extended `resolve_bare_generic_return` to also recognize a bare `HirExprKind::Global("None")` with `ty: Ty::Error` and backfill it from the `expected` type passed in at its two existing call sites (a `val`'s own declared annotation; a function argument's callee-declared param type) when `expected` is `Some(Ty::Option(_))` — a **deliberately conservative** choice: unlike the adjacent `Ty::Var(_)`-on-a-`Call` case (which forces a hard error when nothing resolves it, since that case is *always* genuinely unresolvable), an unannotated bare `None` with no `expected` available is left silently `Ty::Error`, unchanged from the pre-fix behavior — introducing a *new* hard-error path for code that happened to work today without ever needing the real type would be a regression risk disproportionate to this fix's own narrow scope. 4 new `crates/hir` tests: `Some(5)` resolves `Option<Int>` from its own argument with no annotation; `None` resolves from a `val`'s own declared `Option<Int>` annotation; an unannotated bare `None` stays `Ty::Error` with no new hard error (confirmed via `try_lower` returning `Ok`); the pre-existing function-argument backfill path still works for both `None` and `Some(x)` (regression guard for the *other* call site). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**, both directly and via item 246's own `.age`-on-`Timestamp?` machinery as an integration check: `val someTs: Timestamp? = Some(Timestamp.now())` followed by `.age.toSeconds()` now correctly prints `0` (previously garbage); `val noneTs: Timestamp? = None` followed by `.age.toSeconds()` now correctly prints `9223372036854775` (`Duration.max`, previously garbage); the function-argument form (`ageOf(None)`/`ageOf(Some(...))`, item 246's own original repro shape) confirmed still correct; a bare unannotated `val x = None` confirmed to still pass `certo check` cleanly, no new regression. | `crates/hir` |
| 249 | `Decimal` has *zero* arithmetic operator codegen — not just unary negation (found while scoping item 248), binary `+`/`-`/`*`/`/` and every ordering comparison all fail identically at the C-compile stage, despite typechecking cleanly | Confirmed exactly as filed, but **the scope was smaller than the item's own filed text guessed** — it said this "needs a decision on the real rounding/overflow semantics before implementing," but investigating found the real, fully-implemented `certo_decimal_add`/`sub`/`mul`/`div`/`lt`/`lte`/`gt`/`gte`/`negate` runtime functions **already existed**, complete with real fixed-point (`{value: int64_t, scale: int8_t}`) semantics, scale-alignment for add/sub, scale-summing for mul, extra-precision for div — in `crates/stdlib/src/money.rs`, alongside `certo_decimal_eq` (already wired up for `==`/`!=`, confirmed via item 201's own earlier work). No design decision was needed at all; only the codegen *routing* to call these already-existing functions was missing. **Design**: `crates/codegen/src/emit_mir.rs`'s `emit_binop` now checks whether either operand is `Ty::Decimal` for `Add`/`Sub`/`Mul`/`Div`/`Lt`/`LtEq`/`Gt`/`GtEq` and routes to the matching `certo_decimal_*` call instead of a bare C operator, mirroring `Eq`/`NotEq`'s own existing `certo_decimal_eq` routing exactly; `Rvalue::UnOp`'s codegen does the same for `Neg`, routing to the already-existing `certo_decimal_negate`. Also re-enabled `Decimal` in typeck's `is_numeric_ty` (`crates/typeck/src/infer_expr.rs`), which item 248 had deliberately excluded specifically because this exact codegen gap existed at the time — now that the gap is closed, negating a `Decimal` correctly typechecks *and* compiles instead of being rejected.
