Updated item 248's own `negating_a_decimal_still_cleanly_rejected` test (renamed and inverted, since a Decimal negation now genuinely works). 4 new `crates/codegen` tests: all 8 arithmetic/comparison operators route to their real `certo_decimal_*` function (table-driven over all 8 at once); negation routes to `certo_decimal_negate`; a regression guard confirming ordinary `Int` arithmetic is unaffected by the new Decimal-routing check. Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: `d"19.99" + d"5.01"` → `25.00`, `-` → `14.98`, `*` → `100.1499`, `/` → correct (existing extra-precision division display format, unrelated to this fix), unary negation → `-19.99`, and all six comparison operators (`<`/`>`/`<=`/`>=`/`==`/`!=`) all produce correct results — none of this compiled at all before this fix. | `crates/codegen`, `crates/typeck` |
| 247 | A generic `async fn` returning `Result<T, E>` segfaults at runtime when its result is `await`-ed — an otherwise-identical *non*-generic `async fn` with the same shape works correctly | **The item's own "async" framing turned out to be a red herring, confirmed with the user before proceeding**: reproduced the identical segfault with zero async/await anywhere, using a plain `fn wrapResult<T,E>(f: () => Result<T,E>): Result<T,E> = { f() }` — the real bug is general, not async-specific. Root cause: `resolve_bare_generic_return` (`crates/hir/src/lower.rs`, item 227's own mechanism for backfilling a generic call's unresolved return type from a `val`'s declared annotation) only ever recognized a *bare* `Ty::Var(_)` as the whole return type (e.g. `fn identity<T>(x: T): T`) — never one *nested inside* a compound type like `Result<T, E>`. Every declared type param collapses to the identical sentinel `Ty::Var(0)` regardless of name (confirmed directly), so `fn wrapOk<T,E>(v: T): Result<T,E>`'s declared return is `Ty::Result(Var(0), Var(0))` — both slots the same sentinel at different tree positions — meaning resolution has to be positional, not identity-based. **Design**: new `ty_contains_var` (recursively checks for a `Ty::Var` anywhere inside `Option`/`Result`/`List`/`Map`/`Tuple`/`Named`/`Record` — deliberately excludes `Ty::Fn`, see below) and `merge_var_slots` (recursively substitutes each `Var` slot in the actual type with whatever sits at the *same position* in the expected type, keeping already-concrete slots from `actual` as-is rather than overwriting wholesale) replace the old bare-`Ty::Var`-only check. **Two real regressions found and fixed while verifying broadly, not just the filed repro**: (1) including `Ty::Fn` in `ty_contains_var` wrongly forced resolution of a function *value*'s own harmlessly-erased parameter types (`val h = const("hi")`, whose ignored/unused param stays deliberately generic) — removed `Ty::Fn` from the check entirely, since a function value's own future caller handles its arguments at the call site, unlike a container's payload; (2) blindly hard-erroring whenever a compound type couldn't fully resolve broke a real, working case (`Box.wrap(42)` passed as an argument to `Box.unwrap`, whose own pattern-match-based unboxing — item 119's convention — needs no help from this function at all) — added a `hard_error_if_unresolvable` parameter, `true` only at the `val`-binding call site (where an unresolved value is always a real problem) or when the type is a bare `Ty::Var` regardless of call site (always a dead end, nothing else ever resolves it — confirmed necessary via `identity(Box.unwrap(Box.wrap(42)))`, where `identity`'s own param is itself generic too); `false` otherwise, matching the pre-existing safe behavior for compound-typed call arguments. 5 new `crates/hir` tests: both `Result<T,E>` slots resolve from a `val` annotation; only the still-unresolved slot gets merged, an already-concrete slot is kept; the fix generalizes to `Option<T>` too; the pre-existing function-argument backfill path still works; a genuinely unresolvable case (no annotation anywhere) is still a hard error. Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions, including every pre-existing item-135/227-era test. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: confirmed the type-recovery fix works correctly at the HIR level (the match arm's own bound local now correctly types as `Ty::Int`, not `Ty::Error`) — **but the user-visible runtime symptom (segfault/garbage) persists regardless**, traced to a separate, deeper representation-mismatch bug (filed as new item 251, design-sketch only, not attempted here) that this fix alone doesn't close. Shipping the type-recovery fix on its own terms, since it's real, independently correct, and closes real cases (e.g. anything reading the resolved type directly rather than through the specific match-unboxing path item 251 covers) — confirmed this split with the user before proceeding rather than reverting a working, tested fix because a separate bug remains. | `crates/hir` |
| 251 | A generic function's `Ok(v)`/`Some(v)` construction *always* heap-boxes its payload (since `v`'s type is genuinely erased/unknown at that construction site — this compiler never monomorphizes), but the match site that later consumes a fully-resolved, concrete instantiation of that same `Result`/`Option` decides whether to unbox based purely on the payload's own resolved type (`needs_result_box`, `crates/mir/src/lower.rs`) — which has no way to know this *specific* value was actually boxed upstream, since a directly-constructed, non-generic `Ok(5)` is never boxed. The two ends disagree about the value's own runtime representation | Confirmed exactly as filed for `Result`. **User confirmed the "always box" direction** (matching `Decimal`/`UUID`'s own already-consistent treatment) over a new flow-sensitive tracking mechanism. **A second, related but structurally *different* bug found investigating `Option`'s own side, before assuming the same fix would cover it**: `Some(v)`'s own construction (`crates/mir/src/lower.rs`) already *unconditionally* heap-boxes its payload regardless of type — so it isn't a "sometimes 0, sometimes 1 box" mismatch like `Result`'s; it's "sometimes 1, sometimes 2 boxes" — a still-generic `v: Ty::Var` is *already* a boxed pointer at that construction site (item 119/120's own convention), so `Some`'s own unconditional box wraps a pointer *around* a pointer, while the eventual concrete-typed match site only ever dereferences once. Confirmed directly via `--emit-c`: `certo_wrap_some`'s own body boxed an *already-boxed* `_l1` a second time, and the match site's single `*(int64_t*)(...)` read back the inner pointer's own address bits as if they were the integer. **Design**: (1) `needs_result_box` (`crates/mir/src/lower.rs`, gating both `Ok`/`Err`'s construction *and* their match/`?`-desugar consumption — the same function, so fixing it once fixes both ends together) now also returns `true` for `Int`/`Int8`/`Int16`/`Int32`/`UInt`/`Float32`/`Bool`/`Char`/`Unit` — every scalar type that previously fell through inconsistently; `Float` stays excluded (its own existing `__certo_f2i`/`__certo_i2f` bit-reinterpretation path, cheaper than a real allocation); `Text`/`BoundedText` stay excluded (already pointer-sized, no box needed either way). (2) `Some(v)`'s own construction now skips its own box specifically when `v`'s type is still `Ty::Var` (already a boxed pointer from the caller's own item-119 handling) — using the already-boxed pointer directly instead of wrapping it again; a concretely-typed `Some(5)` is unaffected, still boxed exactly once as before. **A third, closely related gap found while re-testing the item's own original filed repro (the `async`/`await` shape) end-to-end, not just the non-async reduction item 247 used**: `await genericCall(...)`'s own bound value is `HirExprKind::Await(inner)`, not a bare `Call` — item 247's own `resolve_bare_generic_return` fix only ever matched `Call` directly, so it silently never even looked at an awaited generic call at all, regardless of that fix. Extended it to see through `Await`, resolving the inner call and syncing the outer `Await` node's own type to match (mirroring `Expr::Await`'s own established "type mirrors the inner expression's type" convention, item 191). **Found and fixed a checked-in test asserting the old buggy behavior directly**, the same pattern as items 234/242's own discoveries: `result_int_payload_is_not_boxed` explicitly asserted an `Int` `Result` payload stays unboxed — renamed and inverted to `result_int_payload_is_now_always_boxed`. 6 new/updated `crates/codegen` tests (all 8 Decimal-style scalar types now box in `Result`; a generic `Some(v)` of a bare type param does *not* double-box; a concrete `Some(5)` is still boxed exactly once, unaffected) and 2 new `crates/hir` tests (an awaited generic `Result` return resolves via a `val` annotation; the same case with no annotation anywhere is still a clean hard error, matching item 247's own established precedent — not a crash). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions across all 49 test groups. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`** across 5 real programs: a non-async generic `Result<T,E>` construction+match now correctly prints `42` (previously wrong output); a generic `Option<T>` construction+match now correctly prints `42` (previously wrong output, confirmed via `--emit-c` to have been the double-box bug specifically); the item's own *original*, literal `async`/`await` repro now fails with a clean, actionable compile error when unannotated (previously segfaulted) and — once the error message's own suggested annotation is added — compiles and correctly prints `42`; a broad regression sweep of ordinary, non-generic `Result`/`Option` usage (a record payload, a `Decimal` payload, integer division, `List.find`) confirmed all still correct, unaffected by either fix. | `crates/mir`, `crates/hir`, `crates/codegen` |
| 230 | `W0100`/`W0101`/`W0102` need a warning-diagnostic subsystem that doesn't exist anywhere in this codebase today — split out of item 225 once its bounded half (E0704) was implemented | Confirmed the checks themselves are each small, exactly as filed, but **the "no warning infrastructure exists" premise turned out to be only half true, and the "W0102 needs item 226 first" premise turned out to be wrong, both found investigating before implementing**. `certo_diagnostics::Diagnostic`/`Severity` (`crates/diagnostics/src/lib.rs`) already had a complete, fully-rendering `Severity::Warning` variant and a `Diagnostic::warning(...)` constructor, built at some point but never once called from anywhere outside that crate itself (confirmed via grep — zero usages) — the *rendering* half of "build a warning class" was already done, just never wired to any real check or CLI path. Separately, confirmed directly that `context { field: T loaded by <any expression> }` already type-checks correctly today using an ordinary function call in place of `db.<table>.find(...)` — W0102's own check (does *any* context field lack `loaded by`) doesn't depend on `db.<table>` working at all, only on the `loaded by` *syntax* existing, which it already does. **Design**: new `Warning`/`WarningKind` (`crates/typeck/src/error.rs`, mirroring `TypeError`/`TypeErrorKind`'s own exact shape) and `check_module_warnings` (`crates/typeck/src/infer_decl.rs`, a new pass run *separately* from `check_module_seeded` rather than widening that function's own `Result<(), Vec<TypeError>>` return type — the safer choice, since `check_module`/`check_module_seeded` are called from dozens of existing test call sites across the crate that would all need updating for a broadened Ok-side type; a parallel function costs nothing and touches nothing existing) implement all three checks directly against the un-expanded `Decl::Validator` AST, mirroring `check_constraint_scope`'s own "no `certo_resolve`/no `expand_validators` dependency" design so these work under `certo check` alone. W0100/W0101 walk each rule's `overrides` relationship (skipping gracefully, not warning, when the named rule doesn't resolve — see item 252 below); W0102 mirrors `emit_validator.rs`'s own `all_have_loaded_by` gate exactly (the same condition that already decides whether `validateWithDb` gets generated at all). Wired into `crates/cli/src/main.rs`'s `run_typeck_inner`: printed via the same `render_all` renderer real errors use, but never aborts and needs no exit-code changes. **Deliberately did not build a `--strict`-style promotion path** — the spec never calls for one, and nothing yet needs it; adding it now would be speculative scope beyond these three checks. 9 new `crates/typeck` tests (W0100 fires/doesn't fire depending on explicit `priority`, skips a nonexistent override target; W0101 fires only for a literal `true` condition; W0102 fires per missing-`loaded-by` field, not for an empty context, not when `loaded by` is present) plus 3 new `docs/ERROR-REFERENCE.md` entries (`--explain` now works for all three). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: a validator with an unprioritized `overrides` and a bare-`true` overriding condition correctly prints both W0100 and W0101 and still reports `ok` (build not aborted); a context field with no `loaded by` correctly prints W0102; a fully-annotated validator (`priority` set, non-tautological condition, `loaded by` present) produces zero warnings; `--explain` correctly prints the new W0100/W0101 doc entries. **A separate, real bug found while implementing W0100/W0101's own "does the overridden rule name exist" safety check, not fixed here**: `E0700`/`E0701`/`E0702` are fully implemented in `crates/resolve` but never actually reachable from `certo check`/`certo build` (the same "`crates/resolve` is dead code for the real CLI" finding item 234 made, hitting a second, independent feature) — a rule's `overrides doesNotExist` silently compiles with no diagnostic at all; filed separately as new item 252. | `crates/typeck`, `crates/cli`, `docs` |
| 252 | `E0700`/`E0701`/`E0702` (rule dependency cycles, `after`/`overrides` referencing a non-existent rule) are fully implemented in `crates/resolve` — but `crates/resolve` is dead code for the real `certo` CLI binary (its only real consumer is the LSP), so none of these three checks ever actually ran for `certo check`/`certo build`. A rule declaring `overrides doesNotExist` silently passed `certo check` with no diagnostic at all | Confirmed exactly as filed. **Design**: ported the three checks directly into `crates/typeck` rather than trying to wire the real CLI pipeline up to `certo_resolve::resolve`/`resolve_seeded` — matching this session's own precedent (item 230's validator-warning checks) of running new validator checks straight against the un-expanded AST, with zero dependency on `crates/resolve`. Added 3 new `TypeErrorKind` variants (`RuleCycle`, `AfterRuleNotFound`, `OverridesRuleNotFound`, `crates/typeck/src/error.rs`) plus a new Pass 6 in `check_module_seeded` (`crates/typeck/src/infer_decl.rs`): `check_rule_dependencies` builds a `rule_map` of every rule name in the validator, checks each rule's `after`/`overrides` references resolve to a real name in that map (E0701/E0702 respectively), then — only when there were no reference errors, mirroring `crates/resolve`'s own `resolve_validator` guard exactly — runs `dfs_rule_cycle` (a direct port of `crates/resolve`'s own `detect_rule_cycles`/`dfs_cycle`: classic recursive DFS over the `after`-dependency adjacency map, using a `rec_stack` to detect and report the first back-edge as a cycle) for E0700. `docs/ERROR-REFERENCE.md` already had correct entries for all three codes from an earlier, unrelated pass — confirmed via grep, no doc changes needed. Wired 3 new match arms into `crates/cli/src/main.rs`'s `type_error_to_diagnostic` (`Diagnostic::error("E0700"/"E0701"/"E0702", ...)`, mirroring the file's own established per-code style). 4 new `crates/typeck` tests, ported directly from `crates/resolve/src/tests.rs`'s own 4 equivalent cases (`validator_after_missing_rule_e0701`, `validator_overrides_missing_rule_e0702`, `validator_rule_cycle_e0700`, `validator_no_cycle_chain_ok`) but exercised through this crate's own `check`/`first_error_kind` helpers instead of `crates/resolve`'s. Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: a two-rule `after` cycle (`rule a { after b ... }` / `rule b { after a ... }`) now correctly aborts with `E0700` and prints the real cycle path (`a → b → a`); `after nonexistent` now correctly aborts with `E0701`; the item's own original repro, `rule r { overrides doesNotExist priority 100 require true else OE.X }`, now correctly aborts with `E0702` (previously silently passed with `ok`); a valid 3-rule `after` chain (`a`, `b after a`, `c after b`) still correctly passes with no false positive. | `crates/typeck`, `crates/cli` |
| 226 | `loaded by db.<table>.find(...)` — the exact syntax every one of spec section 16's own `context` block examples uses — doesn't type-check anywhere in this codebase; `db` is never a bound name at all | **Investigating this fresh turned up the gap was bigger than the item's own filed text assumed**: `db.transaction { ... }` doesn't even *parse* today (confirmed live: `E0206: undefined name 'db'`, not a special form) despite `Expr::Transaction` being a fully-built AST node with complete downstream support (effects `DbWrite` tagging/enforcement, HIR lowering to `Call(Global("__db_transaction"), [thunk])`) — nothing in `crates/parser` ever produced it from real source; and `__db_transaction` itself was only forward-declared in the generated C header, never implemented, so it would have failed to *link* even if it had parsed. **User confirmed building the full ambient-`db` feature** (not deferring it as a design sketch like items 200/72b/216) after a plan-mode design pass nailed down three product decisions: (1) the connection auto-connects lazily via `getenv("DATABASE_URL")` on first use per thread — no explicit `db.connect(...)` bootstrap anywhere, matching every spec example's own zero-setup usage; (2) `db.<table>.<method>(...)` sugars only the three methods `certo db pull` already generates into `db/schema.cto` (`.find(id)`→`{table}FindById`, `.all()`→`{table}FindAll`, `.delete(id)`→`{table}DeleteById`) — no new insert/update generation; (3) `db.<table>` only resolves for tables already pulled (consistent with item 72b's own decision not to require live-DB introspection on every `certo build`/`check`) — a real `E0711` diagnostic ("did you run `certo db pull`?") otherwise, not a silent failure. **Design**: `db.transaction { ... }` needed exactly one new, narrowly-scoped parser rule (`crates/parser/src/parse_expr.rs`, mirroring the existing `unsafe { }`/`every(...) { }` contextual-keyword precedent — recognizes the literal 4-token sequence `db . transaction {` and parses a block, producing the *already fully-built* `Expr::Transaction` node directly) — every downstream stage needed zero changes. `db.<table>.<method>(args)` deliberately got **no new AST node, no bound `db` global, no new `Ty` variant** — recognized purely structurally as a 3-level `Field{Field{Path("db"), table}, method}` call shape in both `crates/typeck/src/infer_expr.rs` (`try_db_accessor_call`) and `crates/hir/src/lower.rs` (`try_db_accessor_rewrite`), gated on `db` not being a real local (`ctx.env.lookup("db").is_none()`/`cx.lookup_local("db").is_none()`) — `db` is deliberately never seeded into `TypeEnv` at all, which makes "is `db` bound locally" the correct and only shadowing check needed, so an ordinary local/param named `db` naturally wins. On a match, both crates build a brand-new, ordinary `Expr::App` calling the real generated function directly with a synthesized call to a new zero-arg builtin `__certo_db_conn()` spliced in as the leading argument, then recurse into the exact same call-checking/lowering machinery an ordinary hand-written call would go through — no arity/unify logic duplicated at all. New `TypeErrorKind::DbAccessorNotFound`/`E0711` (`crates/typeck/src/error.rs`, next-free code after E0710) for an unrecognized method or a missing generated function. **A real, independent bug found and fixed along the way**: `crates/effects/src/infer_effects.rs`'s `callee_lookup_key` only recognized bare/2-level receiver shapes — the 3-level `db.<table>.<method>` shape fell through to `None`, meaning `[io]` effect inheritance from the real generated function would never have fired at all (a `pure`-declared function calling `db.customers.find(...)` would have wrongly typechecked as pure); fixed by extending it to recognize the same 3-level shape, mapped through the identical method→suffix table. **Ambient connection runtime** (`crates/stdlib/src/db.rs`, appended to `DB_C`): a `_Thread_local int64_t` connection slot (confirmed portable with zero extra flags via a direct compile spike through this project's own exact clang invocation before relying on it), `__certo_db_conn()` (lazy-connect, degrades safely to a `0` handle on failure since every existing `certo_db_*` function already no-ops safely on that), and `__db_transaction`'s real implementation — now just `certo_with_transaction(__certo_db_conn(), thunk)`, delegating entirely to the already-correct, nesting-aware transaction function that existed the whole time. **Two link-time risks found and fixed, beyond the item's own original scope guess**: (1) `uses_db` (`crates/cli/src/main.rs`) was detected purely from literal `import Db` statements, but the whole point of ambient `db` sugar is zero-ceremony — a real program using it would very plausibly never have such an import (confirmed: `certo db pull`'s own generated `schema.cto` itself calls seeded globals directly with no imports at all) — so `-lpq`/`DB_C` would silently never get linked; fixed with a new `module_uses_db_sugar` AST-shape scan (any `Expr::Transaction` or `db.<table>.<method>` call anywhere in the entry module's own decls, including validator `context`/rule bodies) OR'd into the existing import-based check. (2) `HTTP_C` links unconditionally regardless of `uses_db` (`crates/stdlib/src/lib.rs`), so the new per-request teardown call added to `__certo_http_handle_connection` had to be wrapped in a new `#ifdef CERTO_DB_ENABLED` (defined by the CLI preamble only when the corrected `uses_db` is true) — without this, every ordinary non-DB HTTP program would have broken at link time. New tests: 2 `crates/parser` (the block-parse shape; `db` not immediately followed by `.transaction {` still parses as an ordinary field access, confirming the shadow precedent), 7 `crates/typeck` (successful resolution for all 3 verbs, wrong-arity surfaces as an ordinary type mismatch reusing the generic call machinery unchanged, unknown table/unrecognized method → E0711, a real local named `db` correctly shadows the sugar), 1 `crates/effects` (the `callee_lookup_key` fix — a `[pure]` function calling `db.customers.find(...)` now correctly requires `[io]`), 3 `crates/hir` (the exact lowered `Call(Global(...), [Call(Global("__certo_db_conn"), []), ...])` shape for both `db.transaction` and `db.<table>`; the shadow case doesn't rewrite), 2 `crates/stdlib` (the seeded `__certo_db_conn` type; `DB_C`/`HTTP_C` string-content assertions confirming the real implementation and `CERTO_DB_ENABLED` guards are present, not stubs). New `docs/ERROR-REFERENCE.md` entry for `E0711`. Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe` against a live local PostgreSQL instance**: ran `certo db pull` against a real `customers` table, then confirmed `db.customers.all()`/`.find(id)`/`.delete(id)` all execute real queries with correct results; `db.transaction { ... }` wrapping a real `dbExec` insert plus a forced `Err` correctly rolled back (row count unchanged, confirmed directly in Postgres afterward) while a later real delete correctly committed; confirmed `db.transaction` outside a `[db.write]`-declared function is correctly rejected with the pre-existing, real `E0403` enforcement (proof the effects machinery — built long before this item, never previously reachable — now actually runs); a plain `Http.serve(...)` program touching no DB at all still built and linked cleanly (the `CERTO_DB_ENABLED` gating regression guard); an HTTP handler using `db.customers.find(...)` hit 5 times returned correct results each time with Postgres's own `pg_stat_activity` connection count settling back to 0 both immediately after and 2 seconds later, confirming the per-request thread-local connection is both correctly scoped and correctly torn down, not leaked. **A separate, real, unrelated bug found and left unfixed while writing the end-to-end test**: `for x in <List<Record>>` mis-codegens the loop variable's own assignment (`certo_list_get_or_panic` returns `void*`, assigned directly to a struct-typed local with no cast, a real C compile error) — reproduces with zero DB/item-226 code involved; filed separately as new item 253. | `crates/parser`, `crates/typeck`, `crates/hir`, `crates/effects`, `crates/stdlib`, `crates/codegen`, `crates/cli`, `crates/resolve`, `docs` |
| 243 | Validator DB triggers install successfully (item 221) but enforce *nothing* — the generated trigger function's rule bodies are reduced to comments, and it unconditionally `RETURN NEW` regardless of whether any rule actually fails | **Re-investigated fresh and found the real scope was smaller than either the item's own filed text or `docs/validator-compiler-guide.md`'s own design sketch assumed.** Both assumed three hard pieces were needed: (a) a real Certo-expr-to-SQL translator, (b) a session-context mechanism (`current_rule_context()`/`db.session.set(...)`) for the trigger to see `loaded by`-context-field values, unconfirmed to exist anywhere, (c) real `RAISE EXCEPTION`/rollback wiring. (b) and (c) turned out to be non-issues: a context field's own `loaded by db.<table>.find(key)` expression already fully encodes the join needed — translated directly to a correlated subquery, no session-context/JSONB mechanism required at all — and Postgres automatically rolls back the whole enclosing transaction on any uncaught `RAISE EXCEPTION` inside a trigger, no separate rollback wiring to build. **User confirmed the full scope** (context-field subquery translation, `RAISE EXCEPTION` with the error variant name as the message, `.age`/Duration → SQL `interval` translation) via a plan-mode design pass before implementing. **Design**: new recursive `expr_to_sql`/`resolve_duration_ms`/`variant_message`/`context_field_table_and_key` translator in `crates/codegen/src/emit_validator.rs`, mirroring the *already-correct* `validateAll` codegen loop structurally (reusing its existing `ordered`/topo-sorted-rules, and a new SQL-specific `overridden_by_rules`/`override_guard_sql` mirroring `overridden_by`/`override_guard`) but emitting SQL fragments instead of Certo source text. Handles comparisons/`and`/`or`/`not`, entity-field access (`NEW.field`), context-field correlated subqueries, named-`constraint` inlining (a new parallel `HashMap<&str, &S<Expr>>` AST-level map passed alongside the pre-existing `constraints: &HashMap<String,String>` *source-text* map, since the SQL translator needs to recurse into a real `Expr` tree, not re-parse rendered Certo source), bare enum-variant-literal quoting (generalizing item 244's own `is_bare_literal` heuristic), `.isSome()`/`.isNone()`, and `.age` vs. either a literal `Duration.days/hours/minutes/seconds/milliseconds(N)` call or a named `temporal` declaration (both constant-folded using the exact multipliers in `crates/stdlib/src/datetime.rs:107-111`, rendered as a SQL `interval '<N> milliseconds'`). Deliberately returns `None` (not a hard error) for anything it doesn't structurally recognize — a rule whose gate can't be *fully* translated (its own `require`, its `else`'s error-variant name, every `after`-prerequisite's own translation, and any overriding rule's own translation must *all* succeed) falls back to the pre-existing `-- Rule: name` comment placeholder alone, conservative and correct since a partially-translated gate could silently under- or over-enforce — plus a new plain build-time warning (`ValidatorOutput.trigger_warnings`, printed by `crates/cli/src/main.rs`'s `collect_validator_trigger_sql`) naming the rule and that it's unenforced by the trigger, so this gap is now visible immediately instead of needing rediscovery via raw `psql` the way item 243 itself was found. **A genuinely surprising discovery mid-implementation**: `OrderError.NotDraft`-style qualified error-variant references (used in every rule's `else` clause) parse as a *single* `Expr::Path` with a 2-segment `ModulePath`, not `Expr::Field` as first assumed by analogy with ordinary value-level field access (`order.status`) — confirmed by direct AST inspection after every new test initially failed identically; `variant_message` was written against the wrong shape and silently treated every rule as untranslatable until this was found and fixed. **A second real bug found and fixed via live end-to-end testing, unrelated to the translation logic itself**: the untranslatable-rule fallback comment text contained a Unicode em-dash, which broke with `invalid byte sequence for encoding "UTF8"` when the generated SQL was piped through `psql` on this platform's pipeline (a real, reproduced failure, not theoretical) — fixed by using plain ASCII in all SQL-embedded text (the separate warning string, only ever printed via `eprintln!` and never piped through `psql`, was unaffected and left as-is). 9 new `crates/codegen` tests covering every translation case (entity-field, `and`/`or`/`not`, context-field subquery, constraint inlining, `.isSome()`, `.age` against both a literal Duration and a named temporal, `overrides`/`after` gating, and the graceful untranslatable-rule fallback plus its warning). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`, `certo db migrate`, and a live local PostgreSQL instance** across three separate validators: an entity-field rule (`order.status == Draft`) genuinely rejected and rolled back a forbidden raw-SQL `UPDATE`, confirmed directly via the row's own unchanged state afterward; a context-field rule (`customer.status == "Active"`, `loaded by db.customers.find(order.customerId)`) correctly rejected an update that would have pointed the order at an inactive customer, via the real generated correlated subquery; an isolated `overrides` validator confirmed the exact application-level gating semantics hold in SQL too — an over-limit update was rejected when not admin-approved, and the identical over-limit update succeeded once the overriding condition was also satisfied; and a validator with one deliberately untranslatable rule (referencing an arbitrary non-`db.<table>.find` `loaded by` expression) still installed successfully, printed the new build warning naming the gap, and continued to fully enforce its other, translatable sibling rule. | `crates/codegen`, `crates/cli` |
| 253 | `for x in <expr>` mis-codegens when the list's element type is a record (struct) — a real C compile error, not just wrong output | Confirmed exactly as filed, root cause exactly as diagnosed. `crates/mir/src/lower.rs`'s own `HirExprKind::For` lowering only ever special-cased `Ty::Float`/`Ty::Decimal(_)` (bit-reinterpreting the raw `certo_list_get_or_panic` `void*` result via `__certo_i2f`) — everything else, including every record/sum-type struct, fell to a direct, unboxed assignment, which C rejects outright for a genuine struct destination. **Design**: replaced the ad-hoc special case with the same general `elem_needs_unbox`/`Rvalue::Unbox` pattern `lower_sum_by_call` (item 162b) already established right below it in the same file — for anything `Ty::needs_heap_box()` (records, sum types, `Decimal`, `Uuid`, `Fn`) or `Ty::Float`, the raw slot is first captured in a `Ty::Var(0)` (`void*`) local, then a real `Rvalue::Unbox` recovers the value via `emit_mir.rs`'s own general-purpose `unbox_value` helper (already used by tuple-field access and list-pattern head-element extraction) — which itself still special-cases `Ty::Float` via `__certo_i2f` internally, so Float behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged. **A second, latent, distinct bug found and fixed as a natural consequence of doing this generally rather than special-casing just records**: the old code's own `Ty::Decimal(_)` special case ran it through the *same* `__certo_i2f` bit-reinterpretation as `Float` — wrong, since `Decimal`'s C representation is a `{value: int64_t, scale: int8_t}` struct, not a bit-castable scalar; it now correctly goes through the same real heap-unbox path as any other struct. **A real, unrelated bug found and fixed mid-implementation** (not part of the original filed scope, caught only because a full-suite regression run — this session's own established discipline — flagged it immediately): the initial edit accidentally dropped a `b.switch_to(loop_body_bb)` call, silently merging the loop's own condition-test block with its body block and erasing the bounds-check `If` terminator entirely — caught by the pre-existing `for_loop_lowers_to_list_iteration` test failing, not discovered independently. **A third, separate, real bug found while writing this item's own end-to-end verification, left unfixed and filed as new item 254**: constructing a list *literal* of `Decimal`/`Uuid` elements (e.g. `[d"1.50", d"2.75"]`, with no `for` loop involved at all) fails at the C-compile stage identically — `crates/codegen/src/emit_mir.rs`'s `operand_ty` has no match arm for `MirConst::Decimal`/`MirConst::Uuid`, falling to `Ty::Error`, so `box_value` never recognizes these need real heap-boxing at construction time either. A different code path and root cause from this item's own for-loop-extraction fix, confirmed to reproduce with zero for-loop code present. 4 new `crates/mir` tests (a record-element list now unboxes correctly; an `Int`-element list still uses no unbox at all, a regression guard; a `Decimal`-element list now uses a real `Unbox` instead of the old, wrong `__certo_i2f` bit-cast; a `Float`-element list still unboxes with `Ty::Float`, confirming behavior-preservation post-unification). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: the item's own original repro shape (`for w in [Widget {...}, ...] { println(w.name) }`) now compiles and correctly prints each widget's name (previously a hard C compile error). | `crates/mir` |
| 254 | A list *literal* of `Decimal`/`Uuid` elements (e.g. `[d"1.50", d"2.75"]`, no `for` loop or any other consumption involved) fails at the C-compile stage — a real, distinct bug from item 253's own for-loop-extraction fix, same underlying "generic void* slot" ABI but the opposite direction (construction, not extraction) | Confirmed exactly as filed, root cause exactly as diagnosed — genuinely a small, bounded fix, no scale/precision investigation needed after all. `crates/codegen/src/emit_mir.rs`'s `operand_ty` (consulted by `box_value`, which every list/tuple-literal construction path already calls to decide whether an element needs real heap-boxing) had match arms for `MirConst::Int`/`Float`/`Bool`/`Str`/`Unit` but none for `MirConst::Decimal(String)`/`MirConst::Uuid(String)` — both fell to the catch-all `Ty::Error`, so `box_value` never recognized a bare `Decimal`/`Uuid` *constant* operand (one never bound to a local first, e.g. a literal directly inside `[...]`) as needing boxing at all, unlike every other `needs_heap_box()` type. **Fix**: two new match arms, `MirConst::Decimal(_) => Ty::Decimal(None)` and `MirConst::Uuid(_) => Ty::Uuid` — confirmed `Ty::Decimal`'s own precision/scale parameter doesn't affect codegen at all (`ty_to_c` maps every `Ty::Decimal(_)` to the same `certo_decimal_t` regardless), so `None` needed no further investigation to be exactly as correct as any `Some((p, s))` would be. 2 new `crates/codegen` tests (a `Decimal` list literal now emits a real `malloc`-based heap-box, not the old broken direct cast; same for a `UUID` list literal). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: the item's own original repro (`[d"1.50", d"2.75", d"10.00"]`, plus a `UUID` list literal alongside it) now compiles and runs correctly — `List.len` reports the right counts for both, and a `for`-loop over the `Decimal` list (composing directly with item 253's own fix) correctly prints each value via `.toText()`. | `crates/codegen` |
| 208 | Spec §7.2's claim "Compiler verifies tasks do not share mutable state" has zero backing implementation | **Re-investigated fresh and found real scope far narrower than the item's own filed text assumed** ("this class of static analysis... would be a large, real feature"), because of a fact about this specific language that a general framing misses: confirmed directly (`grep -rln "is_mutable\|LocalKind\|Mutability" crates/*/src/*.rs` returns nothing anywhere) that Certo's entire mutation surface is `name = expr` reassignment of a bare local (`Stmt::Assign { target: S<String>, .. }`) — no mutable record-field assignment, no references exposed to user code (records only support the immutable `.with(...)` copy-update). So "shared mutable state across `parallel {}` tasks" isn't general pointer/reference aliasing analysis at all — it's a bounded, purely syntactic property: does a name assigned to somewhere get referenced by two or more of a `parallel{}` block's own sibling task expressions (each of which runs concurrently on a real OS thread, `__certo_thread_spawn`)? **A separate, more fundamental, genuinely surprising discovery made while scoping this** (confirmed via a direct, minimal repro: reassigning a `val` and reassigning an ordinary function parameter, neither ever declared `var`, both pass `certo check` silently with no error at all) — Certo has **no `val`/parameter mutability enforcement anywhere in the compiler today**, a real, separate, larger gap. **Confirmed this split with the user before implementing**: build 208 narrowly, defining "mutable" *empirically* from `Stmt::Assign` targets actually present in the source (independent of whether `var` was ever used to declare the name, and independent of the separate val/param-reassignment gap) — filed that larger gap separately as new item 255, not attempted here. **Design**: new `check_parallel_shared_mutable_state` pass (`crates/typeck/src/infer_decl.rs`), wired into `check_module_warnings` (item 230's own warning machinery, reused rather than inventing a new mechanism) for every `Decl::Fn`/`Decl::Impl` method body. Two purpose-built AST walkers mirroring `collect_referenced_idents`'s own exhaustive-match style (`crates/parser/src/parse_decl.rs` — duplicated rather than shared across the crate boundary, matching this codebase's own established per-crate-walker convention): `collect_assigned_names` (every `Stmt::Assign` target reachable anywhere in the function — deliberately "anywhere in this function," not precisely lexically scoped, an accepted approximation) and `collect_referenced_names` (every free identifier in one task expression). For each `parallel {}` block found, a name assigned somewhere in the function AND referenced by 2+ of its own sibling tasks gets a new `W0103`/`ParallelSharedMutableState` warning (non-fatal, matching every other W-code's own established convention — never aborts the build). New `docs/ERROR-REFERENCE.md` entry. 5 new `crates/typeck` tests: fires when two tasks share an assigned var; doesn't fire when only one task references it; doesn't fire for a name never assigned anywhere (an ordinary immutable val/param); doesn't fire for a `var` declared and fully contained within a single task's own body (confirms the approximate "assigned anywhere in the function" scoping doesn't false-positive across genuinely unrelated tasks in practice, since the *reference* side of the check is still precisely per-task); fires inside an `impl` method body too. Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: a `parallel {}` block where two tasks both reassign the same outer `var` correctly prints the new `W0103` warning (build still succeeds, non-fatal); the identical shape with each task using its own independently-declared local variable produces zero warnings, confirming no false positive. | `crates/typeck`, `crates/cli`, `docs` |
| 255 | Reassigning a `val` or an ordinary function parameter (never declared `var`) is accepted silently everywhere in the compiler — no `val`/parameter mutability enforcement exists at all | Confirmed exactly as filed. **Design**: rather than threading a "declared mutable" flag through `TypeEnv::define` (used pervasively — dozens of call sites across `infer_expr.rs`/`infer_decl.rs` for every kind of binding this compiler has, most of which aren't a reassignable local variable at all, e.g. type params, function names, generic-record-field shorthand), a new small, self-contained `check_mutable_assignments` pass (`crates/typeck/src/infer_decl.rs`, wired as Pass 7 in `check_module_seeded`) walks each `Decl::Fn`/`Decl::Impl` method body with its own real lexical scope-stack (`Vec<HashMap<String, bool>>`, pushed/popped on `Expr::Block` entry/exit — the only place `Stmt`s can appear at all in this language). Every `Stmt::Val`'s (possibly destructuring) pattern registers all its bound names as immutable (a new `collect_pattern_names` helper, covering constructor/record/tuple/list/as/or-patterns); every `Stmt::Var` registers its one name as mutable. A `Stmt::Assign` looks up its target in the *innermost* scope that declares it at all — correctly resolving real shadowing in both directions (a `val` shadowing an outer `var` still correctly rejects reassignment; a `var` shadowing an outer `val` may be freely reassigned) — something a simpler "flat set of names ever declared `var` anywhere" would get wrong. Function/lambda parameters and match-arm bindings are never registered at all, so looking them up correctly finds nothing and is treated as immutable, with no special-casing needed. **A real bug in this design found and fixed before shipping, not part of any pre-existing code**: the first version used a flat `HashSet<String>` of "names ever declared `var`," which — caught by a dedicated shadowing test before the fix, not by accident — incorrectly allowed reassigning a `val x` that shadowed an outer `var x`, since it only checked "was `x` ever `var` anywhere" rather than resolving to the *innermost* declaration; replaced with the `HashMap<String, bool>`-per-scope design described above. New `TypeErrorKind::AssignToImmutable`/`E0219` (next-free general-type-error code after E0218) plus a real `docs/ERROR-REFERENCE.md` entry. 6 new `crates/typeck` tests (reassigning a `val`; reassigning a parameter; reassigning a real `var` still passes; a `val` shadowing an outer `var` still rejects; a `var` shadowing an outer `val` may be reassigned; a name bound by a destructuring `val (a, b) = ...` still rejects). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions, and a full sweep of every `.cto` file under `examples/` confirmed zero new `E0219` hits — no existing real program in this repo relied on the previously-permissive behavior. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: the item's own original repro (reassigning a bare parameter, reassigning a `val`) now correctly reports two real `E0219` errors; ordinary `var` reassignment still passes cleanly; both shadowing directions behave exactly as designed; a destructuring `val` correctly rejects reassigning one of its own bound names. | `crates/typeck`, `crates/cli`, `docs` |
| 203 | Leading-dot property/lambda shorthand (`.lineTotal`, `.discount(subtotal)`, `on: .productId`, `.status == Pending`) doesn't exist anywhere in the grammar | **Re-investigated fresh and found the item's own filed text conflated two genuinely different features, one real and one the spec itself already disclaims.** Direct spec read: §9's own text (line 315) explicitly states *"the syntax below is the real, shipped API... it intentionally does not use... leading-dot field predicates (`.status == Pending`); those are not implemented"* — the db-query predicate-comparison form (`.status == Pending` inside `db.orders.where(...)`, §10.2's dashboard vision section) is spec-disclaimed, not a real gap; **confirmed with the user to leave it out of scope entirely**, matching the spec's own words. The §8.5 aggregate-roots form (`.lineTotal`, `.discount(subtotal)`, `on: .productId` — a bare leading-dot chain as an *entire* argument, never embedded inside a larger comparison) is real, working-example syntax that genuinely doesn't parse. **A second, larger surprise**: the item's own text worried this needed "a real type-inference feature" that didn't exist — it already does, just in HIR, not typeck: `crates/hir/src/lower.rs` already applies a per-call-site "lambda param type hint" (sourced from a sibling argument's own already-resolved type) for a specific, hardcoded list of callees (`List.map`/`groupBy`/`sortBy`/`minBy`/`maxBy`/`sumBy`/`upsert`, `Result.flatMap`/`recover`/`mapErr`) — confirmed directly that `List.sumBy(items, (item) => item.lineTotal)`, hand-written, already typechecks and compiles correctly today. **User confirmed scoping to pure parser sugar reusing this existing list**, rather than also generalizing the hint mechanism to arbitrary callees. **Design**: `crates/parser/src/parse_expr.rs`'s `parse_postfix` (the function that already builds an ordinary postfix chain — `.field`, `(args)`, `?`, etc. — onto a parsed atom) now special-cases a bare `.` at the very start of a postfix chain: it synthesizes a receiver `Expr::Path(["__dot"])`, feeds it into a newly-factored-out `parse_postfix_continuation` (the *same* chain-building loop, unchanged, now reusable with any starting receiver), then wraps the resulting chain in an ordinary `Expr::Lambda` with one unannotated param named `__dot` — a real AST node indistinguishable from one the user hand-wrote, so every downstream stage (typeck's ordinary `Expr::Lambda` inference, and the *existing* HIR hint mechanism above) handles it with zero new code. **A real, separate bug found and fixed before this could ship, not part of the original plan**: the naive version of this leaked its own implementation detail — `certo fmt` printed `.lineTotal` back out as `__dot => __dot.lineTotal`, exposing the internal synthesized parameter name in the user's own source (confirmed directly by actually running `certo fmt` on real output, not just assumed) — fixed by teaching `crates/fmt/src/fmt_expr.rs`'s `Expr::Lambda` printer to detect this exact shape (one unannotated param literally named `__dot`, body a direct field access or single call rooted at a reference to it) and print it back in its original shorthand spelling; anything else still prints as an ordinary lambda. **A separate, unrelated, real gap found while verifying the spec's own `coupon.map(.discount(subtotal))` example**: `Option.map` doesn't exist anywhere in stdlib at all (confirmed: `coupon.map(...)` on a `Coupon?` fails with "no field `map`") — out of scope for this item (a missing stdlib function, not a parser/shorthand issue), filed separately as new item 256. 4 new `crates/parser` tests (field shorthand desugars to a real one-param lambda; method-call shorthand with args; the labeled-arg form `on: .productId`; ordinary field access on a real receiver is unaffected) and 5 new `crates/fmt` tests (field/method-call/labeled-arg shorthand all round-trip to their original spelling with no `__dot` leak; round-tripping is idempotent; a genuinely hand-written lambda still prints normally, confirming the detection doesn't over-fire). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**, using the spec's own real repro shapes: `items.sumBy(.lineTotal)`/`.quantity` compiled and produced correct summed output; `items.map(.productId)` and `items.upsert(newItem, on: .productId)` both compiled and produced correct results (upsert correctly matched and updated rather than duplicated); `certo fmt` on all three shapes (plain field, method-call-with-args, labeled-arg) round-tripped back to the user's own original spelling with no internal name ever visible. | `crates/parser`, `crates/fmt` |
| 256 | `Option.map` doesn't exist anywhere in stdlib — `coupon.map(f)` on a `Coupon?` fails with "no field `map`" | Found while verifying item 203's own leading-dot shorthand against the spec's own §8.5 example. **This item's own originally-filed text was itself wrong**: it assumed the fix would "mirror `Result.map`'s own existing desugar" — investigation found `Result.map` doesn't exist either (only `isOk`/`isErr`/`flatMap`/`mapErr`/`getOrElse`/`recover`/`all`/`allSettled`); pivoted to using the confirmed-working `List.map` as the real template instead. **Design**: typeck signature `∀A,B. (Option<A>, A=>B) -> Option<B>` (`crates/stdlib/src/seed.rs`, mirroring `List.map`'s own `Forall` shape) plus a real C runtime function (`crates/stdlib/src/core.rs`). `qualifying_name()` already mapped `Ty::Option(_) => "Option"` (no UFCS work needed). Added `"Option.map"` to `BOXED_ABI_CALLEES` (`crates/mir/src/lower.rs`) so an unannotated callback lambda lifts to the boxed-ABI convention `List.map` already uses. **Three separate, real bugs found only by testing against the real compiled `certo.exe`, none caught by `cargo build`/`cargo test` alone until each was tracked down and a regression test added**: (1) a compile error (`member reference base type 'int64_t'`) — MIR's own *separate*, independently-existing `elem_ty_hint` mechanism (used to recover a boxed-ABI callback's real param type, distinct from HIR's own hint list) only ever matched `Ty::List(inner)`, never `Ty::Option(inner)` — extended to match both. (2) a segfault, found immediately after fixing (1) — `Some(x)`'s own box representation isn't uniform the way `List<T>`'s element storage is: a scalar payload (`Some(42)`) boxes as a single `int64_t` cell (`Rvalue::BoxSome`, needs one deref to reach the bit pattern the boxed-ABI callback expects), but a struct-shaped payload (record/Decimal/UUID/Fn — anything `needs_heap_box()`) boxes at its own real size instead, so the payload pointer *already is* the exact value the callback expects — dereferencing it as `int64_t` first read the struct's own first field as if it were a pointer and crashed. Fixed by adding a second runtime function, `certo_option_map_boxed` (skips the deref entirely), with `crates/mir/src/lower.rs` picking between the two callees per call site based on the receiver's real `Option<T>` payload type (the only place that type is still known); also added `"Option.map"` to the pre-existing `OPT_UNWRAP_CALLEES` list so a heap-boxed *return* type gets the same already-established double-box correction `List.first`/`Map.get`/etc. already rely on. (3) `Option.map(o, (c) => c.method(x))` — a *method call* (as opposed to bare field access) inside the callback — miscompiled to nonsensical field-access-then-call-as-function-pointer codegen instead of a real function call: `"Option.map"` was listed in HIR's `needs_lambda_hint` match arms (from item 203's own investigation) but never actually registered in `stdlib_param_names()`, so `cx.stdlib_params.get("Option.map")` was always `None` and the entire hint-seeding branch — gated on that lookup succeeding — was dead code for `Option.map`; bare field access on the callback param had coincidentally kept working regardless, purely because bug (1)'s fix independently recovers the param's real type for unboxing, masking this until a method call (which separately needs the dot-call UFCS rewrite's own type lookup to resolve) was tried. Fixed by registering `"Option.map"` in `stdlib_param_names()` (mirroring `List.map`'s own `&["list","f"]` entry) and adding a matching `Some("Option.map") => ...Ty::Option(...)` arm to `generic_container_ret`. New tests: `crates/stdlib` (signature shape, `CORE_C` contains both `certo_option_map` and `certo_option_map_boxed`), `crates/hir` (return-type recovery via lambda hint, dot-call lowers to the same shape as the qualified form, and a dedicated regression test asserting a method call inside the callback actually resolves to a real `Call` node rather than staying a bare field access), `crates/codegen` (scalar callback routes to `certo_option_map`, record callback routes to `certo_option_map_boxed`, method-call-in-callback regression reaches the real generated function). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: `Some(Coupon{discount:5}).map(.discount) ?? 0` and the `None` case both print correctly (5, 0); a scalar `Option<Int>.map((n) => n*2)` still works for both `Some`/`None`; the spec's own motivating shape — `coupon.map(.applyTo(subtotal)) ?? subtotal` (a *method call* via the leading-dot shorthand from item 203, on a record payload) — produces the correct result for both `Some`/`None`, confirming items 203 and 256 now genuinely compose end-to-end as the spec originally intended. | `crates/stdlib`, `crates/hir`, `crates/mir`, `crates/codegen` |
| 200 | Row polymorphism (spec §4.3's own example, `fn getName<R: { name: Text }>(record: R): Text = record.name`) typechecks (item 100's bound-checking works) but codegen emits invalid C | This item's own prior investigation (still accurate) found two real fixes: (a) per-call-site monomorphization — a new compilation strategy this codebase has never used anywhere; (b) a compiler-inserted runtime field-accessor passed alongside the erased value. **User confirmed direction (b)**, since it keeps the existing type-erasure architecture (one compiled C body per function, always) intact. **Design**: the accessor is an ordinary zero-arg closure (`certo_fn_t`) — no new MIR primitive, no codegen changes at all; almost every piece of plumbing already existed for unrelated reasons. New `Cx` field `fn_row_bounds: HashMap<String, Vec<(usize, String, Ty)>>` (`crates/hir/src/lower.rs`) — fn name → (param index, field name, field's declared type) for every field named in a `Bound::Row`, populated in the existing pre-registration hoisting pass via a new `collect_row_bound_fields` helper mirroring `crates/typeck/src/infer_decl.rs`'s own `collect_row_bounds`, but keyed by **param index** (HIR erases every type param to the same `Ty::Var(0)` sentinel, so it can't distinguish bound params from unbound ones by `Ty` alone — it needs to know *which positional argument* to build an accessor from). At a row-bound function's own definition site, `add_row_bound_accessor_params` appends one extra `HirParam` per bound field (type `Ty::Fn{params: vec![], ret: Ty::Var(0)}` — the exact same erased-closure shape item 76's HKT params already use) and registers a new `Cx` field `row_field_accessors: HashMap<LocalId, Vec<(String, LocalId, Ty)>>` (never needs clearing — `next_local` is a single module-wide counter, so old entries' `LocalId`s can never recur). `Expr::Field` lowering (the ordinary, non-call arm, right alongside the existing `is_computed`/BACKLOG-143 check) consults this table first: a field access on a row-bound local for a field actually named in the bound lowers to a `Call` on the accessor `Local` instead of an impossible direct struct-member read on an erased `Ty::Var`; a field access for a name *not* in the bound falls through unchanged to the pre-existing (separately broken — see the new item below) path. At each call site, `HirExprKind::Call`'s own lowering synthesizes one accessor-argument Lambda per bound field by building a synthetic `Expr::Field` AST node over the call's *original, untouched* source AST for the row-bound argument and re-lowering it through the ordinary field-access path — since the argument's own concrete type (e.g. `Coupon`) is fully known at the call site, `resolve_field_ty` resolves it with zero special-casing; re-lowering the receiver a second time (once for its own positional arg, once inside the accessor body) is the same accepted tradeoff the dot-call UFCS rewrite already relies on (`lower_expr` only builds a tree, it never executes anything). The resulting `ret_hint: Ty::Var(0)` Lambda gets auto-boxed on return via `Rvalue::BoxSome` by MIR's *existing*, unmodified item-76/126 Lambda-lowering — zero new code there. The **one real MIR change**: item 135's existing `user_raw_return`/`Rvalue::UnboxSome` mechanism ("a call whose callee's declared return is `Ty::Var(_)` but whose call-site type is now concrete needs unboxing") was keyed by callee **name** (`b.fn_ret_tys.get(name)`), which can't apply to a call through a `Local` closure value at all — generalized with one new condition, `local_closure_raw_return` (checks the callee's own HIR `Ty::Fn{ret: Ty::Var(_), ..}` shape directly instead of its name), OR'd into the exact same `needs_return_unbox`/`UnboxSome` branch already there. Calling the accessor itself needed no codegen changes: item 108's existing `emit_callee`/`emit_call_env_arg` already casts any `Local`'s `.fn` to the correct native C function-pointer type from its own tracked `Ty::Fn` and passes `.env` first, with zero awareness this is a row accessor rather than an ordinary closure. **A real, separate, pre-existing soundness gap found during this investigation, deliberately left unfixed and filed separately** (see the new item immediately below): typeck's own `resolve_field_ty` never actually validates that a field access on an unresolved `Ty::Var` receiver names a bound field at all — it just returns a fresh, disconnected type var that happens to unify against whatever the surrounding context expects. This item's own HIR-side field-membership check means a non-bound field access on a row-bound param falls through to that pre-existing (already broken) path unchanged — not a regression, and not this item's job to fix. 6 new `crates/hir` tests (a row-bound fn gains the expected extra accessor param; field access on the bound param lowers to a `Call` on the accessor, not a `Field` node, with the bound's own declared field type; a call site synthesizes exactly one accessor arg per bound field; the accessor's own body correctly resolves against the concrete receiver type at that call site; a *non*-bound field access is unaffected — confirms no over-firing; the same mechanism works through an `impl` method with `self` at param index 0). 1 new `crates/mir` test (the accessor call gets `Rvalue::UnboxSome`, never `Unbox` — using the wrong pairing was confirmed to segfault during development, reading a malloc'd cell's own heap address as if it were the field's raw bits). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**, using the spec's own exact motivating example (`fn getName<R: {name: Text}>(record: R): Text = record.name` called on a real `Coupon` record) — prints the correct name. Also verified: a 2-field bound (`{name: Text, discount: Int}`) called with **two structurally-different concrete record types** (different field counts, different field orders) through the *same compiled function body*, each producing the correct per-type result — direct proof the true-type-erasure architecture is preserved, not secretly monomorphized; and the `impl` method path (a row bound on a method's own additional type param, with `self` occupying param index 0), also correct. | `crates/hir`, `crates/mir` |
| 257 | Row-polymorphism bound checking is real for call-site arguments (item 100) but never actually consulted for field access *inside* a row-bound generic function's own body — accessing a field the bound never declared silently typechecked anyway | Confirmed exactly as filed while implementing item 200's own codegen fix. **Root cause was deeper than "just consult `ctx.env.row_bounds`"**: `crates/typeck/src/infer_decl.rs`'s body-checking (`check_decl`'s `Decl::Fn`/`Decl::Impl` arms) allocates a *brand-new* fresh `TyVar` for each type param via `ctx.fresh()`, completely disconnected from the hoisting pass's own `TyVar`s that `row_bounds` is keyed by (`hoist_decl` allocates ITS OWN, separate set purely to build the function's registered `Ty::Forall` signature for callers) — confirmed directly these never correspond, so a naive lookup against `row_bounds` using the body-checking var would never hit. **User confirmed scope**: fix only the row-bound case; a field access on a *completely unbound* generic (`fn f<T>(x: T) = x.foo`, no row bound at all) is a separate, broader soundness question and stays exactly as permissive as before (deliberately out of scope). **Design**: new `TypeEnv` field `current_body_row_bounds: HashMap<TyVar, (String, Vec<(String, Ty)>)>` (`crates/typeck/src/env.rs`) — keyed by the *fresh* var actually used for the body currently being checked, valued with the type param's own source name (for a clear diagnostic) plus its required fields; set right after `check_decl` allocates each function's/method's fresh type-param vars (reusing the *existing* `collect_row_bounds` helper directly, against the fresh vars instead of the hoisting vars), cleared once that body is done being checked. `resolve_field_ty`'s `Ty::Var(v)` arm (`crates/typeck/src/infer_expr.rs`) now checks this table first: a hit validates the field against the bound's own declared fields (resolving to the bound's declared type on success, a new error on failure); a miss falls through to the exact same `ctx.fresh()` as before, unchanged — this is what keeps a genuinely unrelated not-yet-resolved inference var (the pre-existing, legitimate use of this arm) from ever being affected. New `TypeErrorKind::FieldNotInRowBound`/`E0220` (next free E02xx code after E0219), naming both the type param and its actual declared fields in the message, plus a real `docs/ERROR-REFERENCE.md` entry (also closes a small pre-existing gap: E0217-E0219 turned out to have never been documented there at all, from items 143/214/255 — noted but not fixed here, out of scope). 8 new `crates/typeck` tests (the legitimate single- and multi-field bound cases still typecheck cleanly — a regression guard for item 200's own codegen; an out-of-bound field access now produces `FieldNotInRowBound`/E0220, both structurally and by asserting the exact type-param-name/field/bound-fields values in the message; the same check fires inside an `impl` method body too; a completely unbound generic's field access is confirmed unaffected) plus 1 new `crates/cli` test (`--explain E0220` finds the new doc entry). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions, and a full sweep of every `.cto` file under `examples/` confirmed zero new `E0220` hits — no existing real program in this repo relied on the previously-permissive behavior. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: the item's own original repro (`record.discount` inside a `{name: Text}`-bound generic) now correctly reports a real `E0220` at the field-access site — a clear, useful diagnostic, instead of the old confusing raw C compile error (`member reference base type 'void *' is not a structure or union`) two build stages later; the legitimate item-200 case (a 2-field bound called with two structurally-different concrete record types) still compiles and runs correctly, confirming no regression; a completely unbound generic's field access still fails with the exact same pre-existing raw C compile error as before, confirming the narrow, deliberately-scoped fix. | `crates/typeck`, `crates/cli`, `docs` |
| 273 | `certo test --watch` doesn't exist — a hard `Unknown option` error, exit 2, despite being in spec §11.2's own CLI reference table | Confirmed exactly as filed. `crates/cli/src/main.rs`'s `cmd_test` only ever parsed `--filter`/`--coverage`/`--timeout=`/`--no-color`; any other `-`-prefixed arg hit the generic "Unknown option" exit path. **Design**: mirrors `cmd_run`/`cmd_build`'s own already-shipped `--watch`/`-w` handling exactly (same re-invoke-self pattern, same `cmd_watch::watch_loop` helper) — added `"--watch" | "-w" => watch = true` to the existing arg-parsing match, then, once `files` is known to be non-empty, a new intercept: if `watch`, call `cmd_watch::watch_loop(files.clone(), ...)` with a closure that re-invokes the current binary as `certo test <original args minus --watch/-w>` and returns its exit status, then `return` before the normal (non-watching) test-running loop below ever runs. `cmd_test` already collected `files: Vec<PathBuf>` (multiple input files, unlike `cmd_run`'s single-file case) — `watch_loop` already accepted a `Vec<PathBuf>` from the start, so watching multiple test files needed no changes to `cmd_watch` itself. Updated `--help` text and usage string to list `--watch, -w`. No automated unit test added — `cmd_run`/`cmd_build`'s own `--watch` features have no automated tests anywhere in this codebase either (the re-invoke-self/infinite-poll-loop shape doesn't fit the existing pure-function test style, and there's no CLI-subprocess-integration-test harness anywhere in `crates/cli` to build one on), so this follows the same established precedent rather than inventing a new one for a small, symmetric extension. Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: started `certo test <file> --watch` against a real passing test, confirmed the initial run reports `All 1 tests passed`; edited the test file's own expected value while `--watch` was still running, confirmed it detected the change, automatically re-ran, and correctly reported the now-failing test (`✗ [FAIL]`) — the real watch-and-rerun behavior, not just flag acceptance. | `crates/cli` |
| 272 | §10.3's "auto-derived validation" promise has zero implementation — not just unpopulated, entirely absent | **Investigation split this into two genuinely different claims bundled under one spec comment, confirmed with the user before implementing.** `BoundedText(n)` → validation is real, checkable type information the target record's own field declaration already carries. `Money` → `Min(0)` is not: `Money` (spec §8.4) is just `{amount: Decimal(19,4), currency: Currency}` with no non-negative bound anywhere in the type system, and legitimately goes negative in the domain (refunds, discounts) — **user confirmed skipping this half entirely** rather than inventing an arbitrary "CurrencyInput fields are non-negative" rule with no real derivation behind it. **A second scope fork found mid-investigation**: `TypeExpr::BoundedTextParam`'s own doc comment (`crates/ast/src/types.rs`, from item 147) explicitly documents `BoundedText(n)` as "a compile-time refinement only... no runtime length check is performed anywhere" in the whole compiler — so a server-side rejection in the generated POST handler would make it the *first* runtime `BoundedText` enforcement point anywhere in Certo, a bigger architectural step than "wire up existing info." **User confirmed client-side `maxlength` only**, consistent with that existing "refinement, not enforcement" design — no server-side changes. **Design**: `crates/ui/src/server.rs` — new `bounded_text_max_len(module, target_type, field_name)` looks up the form's own `target` record type via the already-existing `find_type_fields` helper (used elsewhere for `@ui.generate`, item 87) and matches the field by name, unwrapping a `?`-suffixed optional field via `bounded_text_max_len_of` (recurses through `TypeExpr::Option`) since `BoundedText(n)?` still bounds every non-null value the same way. `module: &Module` threaded down through `emit_form_get_handler` (previously didn't need it) into `field_html_expr`, which now takes an extra `max_len: Option<u32>` param and renders a `maxlength="n"` attribute on both the `<input>` and `<textarea>` branches (not `<select>`, where it has no meaning) when present — `emit_form_post_handler` untouched, per the confirmed no-server-side-check scope. 5 new `crates/ui` tests (a `BoundedText(n)` field gets `maxlength="n"`; an optional `BoundedText(n)?` field still gets it; a plain `Decimal` field gets none; a `RichText`-widget field backed by `BoundedText` gets `maxlength` on its `<textarea>` too; a form field with no matching `type` declaration in the module — the exact shape several pre-existing tests already use — still emits no `maxlength`, a regression guard). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo-ui.exe`**: generated a full server for a `type Product = { name: BoundedText(5), description: BoundedText(2000)?, price: Decimal(10,2) }` form — the `name` `<input>` and `description` `<textarea>` (a `RichText` widget) both carry the correct `maxlength`, `price` (unbounded) carries none, and the complete generated `server.cto` (including the new attributes embedded in Certo string literals) compiles cleanly end-to-end via `certo build --emit-c`. | `crates/ui` |
| 267 | `List.flatMap`'s call-site return type is never recovered in HIR — causes a real segfault when the result is consumed downstream (not just measured) | Confirmed exactly as filed, but the fix needed one more piece than the filed text alone implied. **Design**: added `Some("List.flatMap") => args.get(1).and_then(callback_ret_ty)` to `crates/hir/src/lower.rs`'s `generic_container_ret` — unlike `List.map`'s own arm, no extra `Ty::List` wrapping, since `flatMap`'s callback (`A => List<B>`) already returns `List<B>` directly. **Rebuilding and re-testing the exact audit repro still segfaulted** — the callback's own param (`x` in `(x) => [x, x]`) was never hinted from the receiver list's element type at all: `List.flatMap` was completely absent from both `needs_lambda_hint` *and* `stdlib_param_names()` (the same dead-code trap item 256 hit for `Option.map` — `cx.stdlib_params.get("List.flatMap")` was always `None`, so `needs_lambda_hint`'s own listing for it never actually ran), so `x` stayed `Ty::Error`, `[x, x]`'s own body type resolved to `List<Error>`, and a `for` loop over that result gave its own loop variable `Ty::Error` too — which an f-string interpolating it couldn't tell needed `certo_int_to_text`, the exact "read a raw Int as a Text pointer" segfault class already documented on `Expr::WithTimeout`'s own lowering. Fixed by adding `List.flatMap` to both tables (mirroring `List.map`'s own entries exactly); `List.flatMap` was already correctly listed in MIR's `BOXED_ABI_CALLEES` from item 162's original work, so no MIR changes were needed. 1 new `crates/hir` test (the call's own type now fully resolves to `List<Int>`, not `Ty::Error`/`List<Error>`) and 2 new `crates/codegen` tests (the boxed-ABI lift still fires; an f-string interpolating an element read back out of the flatMap result emits a real `certo_int_to_text` call, proof the element type survives all the way through). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: the audit's own exact repro (`List.flatMap(xs, (x) => [x, x])` consumed by a `for` loop + f-string) now prints `1 1 2 2 3 3` correctly instead of segfaulting. | `crates/hir` |
| 271 | `Duration.months(12)` — section-16-validators.md §16.9's own worked example — doesn't exist in stdlib | Confirmed exactly as filed; scoped to `.months` only (the only calendar unit either spec doc actually uses anywhere — `.weeks`/`.years` appear nowhere, so not added speculatively). **Design**: `crates/stdlib/src/seed.rs` — `Duration.months` registered with the identical `fn1(Ty::Int, dur())` shape as `Duration.days`, no `pm!`/HIR registration needed (single-arg constructors need neither, matching every other `Duration.*` constructor's own precedent). `crates/stdlib/src/datetime.rs` — `certo_duration_months(n)` scales to milliseconds via a fixed 30-day month (`n * 2592000000LL`), the same "fixed-length, not calendar-aware" convention every other Duration unit already uses (`.days`/`.hours` don't account for a real month's 28-31 day variance or a DST-shifted day either — there's no real calendar-month concept anywhere in this representation to derive an exact value from). 2 new `crates/stdlib` tests (typeck signature shape; the C runtime's exact 30-day multiplier). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**: `Duration.toDays(Duration.months(12))` correctly prints `360`; adding a further `Duration.days(30)` correctly prints `390`. | `crates/stdlib` |
| 266 | `List.forEach` — spec §3.2's own literal example (`List.forEach(xs) { x => println(x) }`) — doesn't exist anywhere in stdlib | Confirmed exactly as filed. **Design**: `crates/stdlib/src/seed.rs` — `List.forEach: (List<A>, A => Unit) => Unit`, mirroring `List.map`'s own shape with a `Unit`-returning callback; real C runtime `certo_list_for_each` (`crates/stdlib/src/collections.rs`) mirrors `certo_list_map`'s own iteration loop, calling the callback purely for its side effects and discarding its (always-`Unit`) return. Registered in every table `List.map`/`List.flatMap` already needed for a correctly-hinted, correctly-boxed callback — `stdlib_param_names()` and `needs_lambda_hint` (`crates/hir/src/lower.rs`) so the callback's own param gets hinted from the receiver list's element type, and `BOXED_ABI_CALLEES` (`crates/mir/src/lower.rs`) for the boxed-ABI lift — applying item 256's and this same session's own item 267 lesson *proactively* this time rather than rediscovering the identical dead-code trap a third time. 1 new `crates/stdlib` test (signature shape, `COLLECTIONS_C` contains the real runtime function), 1 new `crates/hir` test (a method call inside the callback body resolves correctly, proving the element-type hint actually fired), 1 new `crates/codegen` test (the boxed-ABI lift fires). Full `cargo build --workspace`/`cargo test --workspace` clean, 0 regressions. **Verified end-to-end via the real compiled `certo.exe`**, using the spec's own exact example shape: `List.forEach(xs) { x => println(intToText(x)) }` correctly prints each element in order; a second repro confirmed a closure capturing an outer `val` (not just the loop variable) works correctly, and that `forEach` composes correctly as a non-tail statement inside a larger function body. | `crates/stdlib`, `crates/hir`, `crates/mir` |
| 275 | §16.5 "String Conditions" still documents `text.matches(regex)` as valid validator-rule syntax in `docs/section-16-validators.md` — the same fictional API item 163 already fixed, but in a *different* file item 163 never touched | Confirmed exactly as filed — a documentation-only fix, matching item 163's own precedent (no code change, since the real, working `Regex.*` API already exists and item 163 already established the correction pattern for the other two spec docs). Replaced `product.sku.matches("^[A-Z0-9\-]{3,20}$")` with `Regex.match(pattern, text)`'s real, pattern-first signature. **A second, separate, genuinely new gap found while verifying the fix as a real validator rule, not just read-and-replace**: the shipped regex engine (`crates/stdlib/src/regex.rs`, a hand-rolled backtracker whose own header comment honestly states what it supports — `. * + ? ^ $ [] ()`) does **not** support `{n,m}` bounded-repetition quantifiers at all — confirmed live: `Regex.match("a{3,5}", "aaaa")` returns `false` (the engine treats `{`/`}`/digits/`,` as ordinary literal characters to match verbatim, rather than erroring or warning), so the original example's own exact pattern (`{3,20}`) would have silently rejected *every* input, valid or not, once compiled — a silent-false-negative on every write, the same "installs but doesn't enforce" failure shape items 218/243/244 already found elsewhere in validators, here in the regex engine itself. Rather than ship a corrected method name over a pattern that can never match, replaced the bounded quantifier with an equivalent `Regex.match("^[A-Z0-9-]+$", ...) and Text.len(...) >= 3 and Text.len(...) <= 20` compound condition, verified via a real end-to-end validator (`ProductCheck.validate(...)`) to correctly accept `"ABC-123"` and reject both a bad-character SKU and a too-short one. The `{n,m}` quantifier gap itself is filed separately as new item 276, not fixed here (a real regex-engine feature, out of scope for a docs-accuracy pass). | `docs` |
| 259 | Recursive (self-referential) sum types — spec §3.3's own literal `Tree<T>` example — crash the compiler | Confirmed exactly as filed for the crash half; **investigated the "make it actually work" half and found it's a genuinely separate, bigger feature, confirmed with the user to file separately rather than build here (see new item 277 below)**. Root cause of the crash: `crates/codegen/src/emit_module.rs`'s `ty_is_comparable` — which decides whether a non-generic user type gets a generated `certo_eq_*` function — recurses through a sum type's own declared variant-field types with zero cycle protection; for `Node(value: Int, left: Tree, right: Tree)`, checking whether `Tree` is comparable requires checking whether `Node`'s own `left: Tree` field is comparable, which requires checking `Tree` again, forever — a real, direct Rust stack overflow in the compiler itself (confirmed live: `certo build` on the spec's own `Tree` example crashes with "thread '<unknown>' has overflowed its stack"), not a Certo-program bug. The function's own prior doc comment explicitly (and, it turns out, wrongly) argued this class of cycle was already "someone else's problem" — assuming any self-referential type is already an infinite-size C struct that fails to compile before this code ever runs; true for a record referencing itself directly by value, false for a sum type, since a payload field is only ever inline-by-value in this codegen (never boxed) when it's *not* self-referential, so the struct itself is perfectly finite — only `ty_is_comparable`'s own unguarded recursion loops forever, regardless of C layout. **Design**: split the function into a stable 2-arg public wrapper (`ty_is_comparable`, keeping both existing call sites unchanged) and a new `ty_is_comparable_inner` taking a `visiting: &mut HashSet<String>` of type names currently being checked on the current call stack — hitting a name already in `visiting` means a cycle was found before any base case, so it conservatively returns "not comparable" (matching the function's own already-established conservative stance for anything without a single correct comparison, e.g. generic/optional/container fields) instead of recursing further.
